# Decision Log

| | |
|---|---|
| **Owner** | Project management |
| **Status** | Living document |

Status values: **Accepted**, **Proposed** (recommended, awaiting confirmation), **Open**
(not yet decided), **Superseded** (replaced by a later decision). Changing an accepted decision requires a new entry that supersedes it.

## Decisions

| ID | Decision | Status | Rationale | Alternatives considered |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DEC-01 | Build a production platform where users deploy autonomous agents on their own accounts | Accepted | Founder direction; software business, no custody | AI-native fund trading own capital; research copilot |
| DEC-02 | Rust for the core (runtime, risk, execution, connectors, journal); Python for research, model tooling, SDK | Accepted | Speed and safety on the hot path; Python ecosystem for research | Python-only; C++ |
| DEC-03 | The mandate is the contract: agents cannot act outside the approved spec | Accepted | Core trust promise | Prompt-level instructions only |
| DEC-04 | LLMs produce opinions; deterministic code decides sizing and places orders | Accepted | Auditability; resistance to prompt injection; speed | LLM makes the final call (TradingAgents style) |
| DEC-05 | Reducing risk never needs approval; increasing risk beyond limits always does | Accepted | Asymmetric safety; enables autonomy | Approve everything; approve nothing |
| DEC-06 | Timeouts and ambiguity resolve to a safe default that never adds risk | Accepted | Fail-safe behavior | Timeout executes the proposed action |
| DEC-07 | Journal intent before sending any order; idempotency keys; event-sourced recovery | Accepted | No duplicate orders; full audit | Best-effort logging |
| DEC-08 | One process per agent deployment | Accepted | Isolation, independent lifecycle | Many agents per process |
| DEC-09 | Organization → Workspace hierarchy; workspace is the tenant; billing per organization | Accepted | Founder requirement; clean isolation boundary | Flat accounts |
| DEC-10 | Thin global control plane; sensitive services run with the data plane; three deployment modes from one installer | Accepted | Strategy and trading intent stay on the customer's site; on-prem as packaging | Full hosted control plane with edge data plane only |
| DEC-11 | Notifications carry only opaque IDs; approval details load from the workspace deployment | Accepted | Privacy of trading intent | Full details in notifications |
| DEC-12 | Never price per trade or on assets or profits | Accepted | Regulatory posture; neutral incentives | Per-trade fees; performance fees |
| DEC-13 | Exchange testnet first, crypto perpetuals first | **Superseded by DEC-23** | Free, 24/7, safe onboarding. Superseded because the major perpetuals venues exclude US persons | US equities first via Interactive Brokers |
| DEC-14 | Build Phase 0 core from first principles, piece by piece | Accepted | Founder direction; deep understanding of trading mechanics | Start directly on NautilusTrader |
| DEC-15 | Working name "Mandate" | Accepted | Matches the core concept; finance-native | Delegate, Autopilot, Principal |
| DEC-21 | Build with the founder plus AI coding agents; no hires planned for v1. The founder approves all merges and holds all production secrets | Accepted | Founder direction; speed and cost | Hire a core engineering team |
| DEC-22 | Serve the United States first | Accepted | Founder direction; largest market | Global crypto venues first |
| DEC-23 | First connector: **Alpaca** (US stocks, ETFs, crypto spot; paper trading; OAuth connections). Second: **Kraken Derivatives US** (CFTC-regulated crypto perpetuals). Later: Coinbase US futures, Interactive Brokers | Accepted (connector order amended by DEC-98) | Alpaca: free paper trading on the same API as live, four asset classes in one integration, OAuth for third-party apps, market data through each user's own account. Kraken: the CFTC-regulated perpetuals venue for US users with a self-service demo environment | Kraken perpetuals first; Coinbase first (static sandbox only); Interactive Brokers first (heavier onboarding) |
| DEC-24 | v1 asset scope on Alpaca: US stocks, ETFs, and crypto spot; options later | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-27) | Keeps v1 accounting and risk to spot instruments | Include options in v1 |
| DEC-25 | Trading domain conventions: average-cost P&L; fees recorded separately in USD (asset-denominated fees valued at fill price); backtests on raw prices plus explicit corporate actions; cash accounts use settled cash only; v1 leverage capped at 1× | Accepted (refined by DEC-27; shorting superseded by DEC-32, no short sales; margin buying power by DEC-34) | Simple, auditable, and cannot create account violations by construction ([trading domain spec](../specs/trading-domain.md)) | FIFO P&L; adjusted-price backtests; margin in v1 |
| DEC-26 | One serialized account ledger per broker account; one agent per instrument per account; activity not originated by Mandate switches all agents on the account to exits-only | Accepted | Buying power, orders, and positions are account-level; prevents agents overspending or crossing each other | Per-agent ledgers without coordination |
| DEC-27 | Store signed cost basis, not average cost (average derived); reductions rounded half-even to 12 places | Accepted | Exact conservation; identical results across Rust and Python | Stored average cost |
| DEC-28 | Protective exits via OCO and bracket orders (tranche model), with defined exit and kill-switch sequences | Accepted | Alpaca rejects self-crossing orders but exempts OCO and bracket orders; plain stops block other exits | Plain stop orders with cancel-and-replace only |
| DEC-29 | Opening and increasing orders are limit orders only, within a price collar; market orders only for risk-reducing exits in the regular session | Accepted | Bounds fill prices, so buying power and no-debit rules hold | Market orders for entries |
| DEC-30 | Overnight session disabled for v1 agents | Accepted | Thin liquidity, delayed data on free plans, next-day trade dates, no stop protection | Enable overnight trading |
| DEC-31 | Instrument eligibility floor (exchange-listed, no OTC or IPO-day, price and liquidity floors, leveraged/inverse ETPs only with opt-in) and gate-enforced market-conduct controls | Accepted | Prevents manipulation-like patterns and outsized losses from autonomous flow | Monitoring only |
| DEC-32 | No short sales in v1 | Accepted | Removes Reg SHO, borrow, and margin-deficit exposure | Shorting with safeguards |
| DEC-33 | Mandate never originates trade ideas (inferred fields inactive until confirmed; approvals show the mandate basis, not platform-authored alternatives); trading records retained at least 6 years | **Superseded by DEC-97** (the platform now originates ideas); the retention rule stays | Protects the software-only posture; supports users' recordkeeping and the platform's defense | Configurable retention without a floor |
| DEC-34 | Margin-account buying power uses total cash (settled + unsettled) ≥ 0; settled-only applies to cash accounts; backtests use the same rule | Accepted | Matches Alpaca at 1× (buying power = cash); using unsettled proceeds is permitted in margin accounts | Settled-only for all accounts |
| DEC-35 | Paper trading uses a defined IEX data profile; live equity agents require consolidated (SIP) data on the user's Alpaca account | Accepted | Paper and free plans receive IEX only; collars and risk marks need a sound reference quote | IEX for live; SIP everywhere |
| DEC-36 | Crypto protection is one simple GTC stop-limit for the whole position; take-profit handled by the runtime; adds and exits use cancel → confirm → act → re-place | Accepted | Alpaca crypto supports only simple orders (no OCO or bracket) | No crypto protection |
| DEC-37 | No opening orders outside the regular session in v1; exits allowed | Accepted | Bracket orders do not support extended hours; stops do not trigger outside regular hours | Extended-hours openings |
| DEC-38 | Principle wording: "Mandate does not choose instruments, strategy, sizing, or limits; these come from user-confirmed mandate fields; advisors are tools the user selects." Templates never ship with platform-chosen instruments or parameters | **Superseded by DEC-97** | The earlier "never originates a trade idea" overstated, given platform advisors and templates | Keep DEC-33 wording |
| DEC-39 | Each agent has a capital allocation (Σ ≤ account equity); agent equity = allocation + agent P&L at risk marks; allocation changes adjust equity, high-water mark, and day-start equity so they never count as P&L | Accepted (allocation-change rule superseded by DEC-50) | Limits need a per-agent base when several agents share one account; risk marks keep limits conservative | Limits on account equity only; allocation as a hard cash reservation |
| DEC-40 | Risk day starts 00:00 America/New_York for all asset classes; a daily-loss breach puts the agent in `exits_only` until the next risk day, lifted automatically | Accepted (confirmation and lift delay added by DEC-49) | One boundary for equities and 24/7 crypto; automatic lift matches the limit's "daily" meaning | Per-session day; owner acknowledgment to lift |
| DEC-41 | Drawdown from the high-water mark with a ladder (`scale_sizes` with hysteresis, `exits_only`, `flatten_and_pause` at `max_drawdown`); `exits_only` and `flatten_and_pause` lift only by owner acknowledgment with step-up | Accepted (acknowledgment and reset defined by DEC-44) | Graduated response before a hard stop; hysteresis prevents flapping; severe states need a human | Single hard stop; drawdown from allocation instead of the high-water mark |
| DEC-42 | Autonomy: built-in AUTO for reduce, close, and protective actions; then ordered first-match rules; then the default (`ask` unless the user confirmed `auto`); approval timeout always skips | Accepted (exit purposes refined by DEC-48) | Enforces DEC-05 structurally; first-match is predictable; skipping on timeout is the safe default | Scored rules; timeout executes; per-rule timeouts |
| DEC-43 | Mandate version = SHA-256 of the canonical JSON; every change is classified risk-increasing, risk-reducing, or neutral (unknown changes are risk-increasing); risk-increasing changes need step-up | Accepted (the universe moved out of the hashed document by DEC-97) | Deployments pin exact contracts; fail-safe classification keeps loosening deliberate | Semantic version numbers; step-up for every change |
| DEC-44 | Acknowledging a drawdown `exits_only` or `flatten_and_pause` (step-up) resets the high-water mark to current equity and restores sizes gradually; a lifetime loss floor (`max_loss_from_allocation` of contributed capital) can never be acknowledged or reset; risk state belongs to the agent and survives restarts and version changes | Accepted | After a flatten the agent holds cash, so drawdown never recovers on its own; unlimited resets left cumulative loss unbounded | Unlimited resets; re-arm rungs without a reset |
| DEC-45 | The user enters and confirms every judgment field; the compiler only extracts values the user stated (with the quoted span) and never proposes instruments, signal models, numbers, or `auto`; platform defaults only for listed non-judgment fields | **Superseded by DEC-97** | Required risk fields filled by the platform would be the platform choosing limits (DEC-38) | "Conservative" platform defaults for unstated limits |
| DEC-46 | Goals in v1: `continuous`, `accumulate`, and `profit_stop` (a level at which the agent flattens and stops, not a target); `distribute` removed | Accepted | An agent cannot sell shares its sub-ledger never bought; avoids an unpaced selling program and any implied expected return | Position adoption for `distribute`; `return_target` |
| DEC-47 | Order builder: the user selects the sizing method (v1: `conviction_linear`) and fixed model weights; no calibration in v1; separate entry and exit thresholds; rebalance band; no trims; missing models count as zero; proposals clipped to limits before autonomy | Accepted (missing-model rule refined by DEC-60) | Removes platform discretion over size and approval routing; a model outage can no longer enlarge orders; no churn or non-monotonic holds | Weight calibration within bounds; single threshold; dividing by fresh weights only |
| DEC-48 | Exits are typed by origin: `risk_exit` (limits, flatten, kill switch, stop watchdog) is exempt from everything; `discretionary_exit` (signal, goal, owner close) is AUTO and never denied but paced by conduct controls, the close window, and (equities) the regular session; the gate assigns purpose from side and position | Accepted | A bad model could otherwise dump and rebuy the book at extended-hours prices, exempt from all controls | All exits exempt (DEC-05 as first written) |
| DEC-49 | `exits_only`, `flatten_and_pause`, daily-loss, and lifetime-floor breaches must persist for the user-set `breach_confirm_s`; equity risk state uses regular-session marks only; a failed mark means `exits_only`, never flatten; equity flatten sells outside the regular session wait for the open; daily-loss lift waits at least `daily_breach_min_s` | Accepted (confirmation refined by DEC-54) | A single bad bid or flash print could otherwise trigger an irreversible flatten into the worst liquidity | Immediate triggers; extended-hours marks |
| DEC-50 | An allocation change scales the high-water mark and day-start equity by E′/E, preserving drawdown and the daily fraction; increases are rejected while a latched limit is active; decreases may not push equity below gross exposure | Accepted (extended to the capital base by DEC-53) | Additive adjustment let deposits lift ladder rungs and cuts trigger flattens | Additive adjustment (DEC-39 as first written) |
| DEC-51 | Policy hierarchy platform → organization → workspace → mandate, with maximums, minimums, permissions (`auto_allowed`, `leveraged_etps_allowed`), requirements (`protection_required`, `independent_approval_required`), and sets (asset classes, signal-model types, goal types, channels); ladder policy by action, not index; a retail profile at platform level (values pending counsel) | Accepted | Funds need to restrict autonomy and model types; platform minimums need a home; retail needs a stricter default | Org-level only |
| DEC-52 | Terminology: "signal model" (not "advisor"), "order builder" (not "decider"), "combined score" (not "confidence"), "profit stop" (not "return target"); signal models pin a content hash, have no parameter defaults, and document methodology only | Accepted (output limits lifted for the research agent by DEC-97) | Avoids holding-out and advice connotations; pins exactly what the user selected | Keep the original terms |
| DEC-53 | An allocation change scales the high-water mark, day-start equity, capital base, and inherited loss by E′/E (rounded up); it is applied after time is settled and rejected if any limit would become newly true | Accepted | Additive capital let a withdrawal trip the floor or a false profit stop; found by review, then by the invariant fuzz (MI-2) | Additive capital base |
| DEC-54 | Breach confirmation counts time in breach, resetting only after the condition is false for `breach_confirm_s`; a loss of 1.25 × the limit triggers immediately; `breach_confirm_s` ≤ 300 s; a pending daily breach is latched at the rollover; confirmation continues on clock ticks | Accepted | A bounce every 59 s prevented a flatten; the last hour of a session was effectively unenforced | Continuous-input confirmation |
| DEC-55 | Platform maximum on `max_loss_from_allocation` (0.5; retail 0.2 pending counsel); loosening a latched floor needs independent approval, or one full risk day in a single-user workspace; a connection's 90-day loss carry is inherited by new agents, and deployment is rejected when the carry reaches the floor budget | Accepted | A single step-up change, or retiring and redeploying, escaped the floor | Floor only per agent |
| DEC-56 | `risk.scale_action`: `limit_buys` (targets only) or `trim_to_target` (sell down to the scaled cap as a risk exit), chosen by the user | Accepted | A "halve sizes" rung did not reduce what was held | Always trim; never trim |
| DEC-57 | After a high-water-mark reset, scale rungs lift one at a time, highest first, each after `scale_lift_after_s` of regular-session time | Accepted | All rungs lifted together after 10 minutes, restoring full size at once | Post-reset factor held for N days |
| DEC-58 | `owner_exit` purpose: the owner's close or kill switch may sell equities outside the regular session through the exit price ladder after confirming the displayed bid; automated flattens still wait for the open | Accepted | After bad after-hours news the owner's only way out was trading at the broker directly | All kill switches wait for the open |
| DEC-59 | `on_complete` for `continuous` and `accumulate`: `hold_protected`, `release`, or `disarm_ladder`, chosen at confirmation; `profit_stop` always exits and retires (`profit_stop_reached`) | Accepted | A completed accumulation could be sold at the next ordinary dip | Always hold with every limit armed |
| DEC-60 | `unusual_input` cannot be used in v1 rules (V-018) until the drift detector ships. For buys a missing model output counts as fully bearish (conviction −1); for exits it counts as zero | Accepted | "Always true" made every trade ask; the invariant fuzz (MI-10) showed "missing counts as zero" enlarges buys when the missing model is bearish | Missing counts as zero for both |
| DEC-61 | Retail profile placeholders until counsel answers: `auto_allowed: false`, quant models only, leveraged ETPs off, protection required, approval timeout ≥ 120 s, lifetime loss ≤ 0.2; retail assignment journaled | **Superseded by DEC-98** | Autonomous execution and personalized LLM output are the main open retail posture questions (counsel 3, 20) | Same defaults as business workspaces |
| DEC-62 | The shared plane carries market data and factual classifications of public events only, never directional views; agent memory does not feed signal models in v1 | **Superseded by DEC-97** for the agent's own memory; the shared-plane rule stays | A platform-computed signal feed sold by subscription would be a signal service; memory "lessons" would be calibration by another name | Shared "intelligence" subscription |
| DEC-63 | A hard breach (1.25 × a limit's loss level) on a sane quote applies a temporary `exits_only` (`hard_breach`); the limit latches only if the hard level holds on a second sane quote at least min(`breach_confirm_s`, 10 s) later (refines DEC-54) | Accepted | One bad print could flatten an agent irreversibly into the worst liquidity | Immediate hard latch |
| DEC-64 | A daily breach still confirming at the rollover keeps confirming against the previous day's E₀ (at most 300 s) and is discarded if it clears (refines DEC-54) | Accepted | Latching at midnight however briefly the breach lasted flattened on a flash print | Latch any pending breach at midnight |
| DEC-65 | `trim_to_target` trims only on a rung active for `breach_confirm_s`, only when the excess is at least the rebalance band and the order meets the minimum, equities in the regular session only, never while Holding; not allowed with `accumulate` (V-033) (refines DEC-56) | Accepted | Unconfirmed rungs caused round trips on one quote, dust trims, extended-hours trims, and goal-spend erosion | Trim on every rung trigger |
| DEC-66 | An owner exit outside the regular session confirms the displayed bid, bid size, and a floor price (default: bid × (1 − the ladder's maximum offset)); the exit price ladder never prices below it; any remainder rests at the floor, then waits for the session (refines DEC-58) | Accepted | In thin after-hours markets the ladder could fill far below the bid the owner saw | No floor |
| DEC-67 | A signal model's content hash includes the underlying model identity; the gateway never substitutes a model; withdrawals are journaled `PlatformOperatorAction` events and outputs then count as missing (refines DEC-52) | Accepted | A gateway fallback or provider update would change behavior without a new version, which is the platform choosing strategy | Pin code and prompt only |
| DEC-68 | Retail status depends on who the investor is, not the deployment mode: a workspace is retail unless owned by a verified entity other than an individual's personal vehicle (or per counsel's investor-status test); unassigned means retail (refines DEC-61) | Accepted | Choosing hybrid or on-prem, or a single-member entity, escaped every retail protection | Managed-mode individuals only |
| DEC-69 | The loss carry is each retired agent's net dollar loss (net contributed − equity), not the capital-base loss (refines DEC-55) | Accepted | Withdrawing before retiring shrank the carry to almost nothing, reopening the floor escape; found by review and confirmed by the fuzz (MI-14) | Accrue scaled losses on each withdrawal |
| DEC-70 | Discretionary exits are allowed in the close window as marketable limit orders within the price collar and participation caps; opening and increasing orders stay blocked there and MOC/LOC orders stay banned (resolves OD-10) | Accepted | Deferring a 15:51 exit held the position through the overnight gap, and the exit might not recur at the open; exits paced by participation caps carry little marking-the-close risk (counsel question 29) | Defer and execute at the open |
| DEC-71 | Tier 1 specs approved: trading domain spec v0.8, journal spec v0.2, and mandate spec v0.5, with their schemas, reference cases, and the mandate reference implementation; agents may begin engineering setup and E1 | Accepted | Founder sign-off after three review rounds and the invariant, fixture, and mutation checks | Further review rounds before implementation |
| DEC-72 | Engineering setup per [ADR-0001](../adr/0001-engineering-setup.md) (ES-01 to ES-25): monorepo; pure core crates with enforced dependency directions; Rust 1.98.1, edition 2024; exact-or-error decimals over `rust_decimal`; own time types and RFC 8785 canonicalizer; tokio 1.53 LTS in shell crates only; sqlx 0.9 with owner-role migrations; Python 3.14 with uv and no PyO3 yet; PyYAML-exported fixtures; Depot CI on Origin; squash merges with a two-PR flow for safety-critical work; founder-owned dependency registry | Accepted | Decided by a three-engineer panel under founder delegation (unanimous or accepted majority on every point) | See ADR-0001, "Alternatives rejected" |
| DEC-73 | The journal is the audit record; telemetry never is. Metrics via the OpenTelemetry API with a Prometheus pull exporter at M6; push export only under a data policy at M8 (amends HLD §11) | Accepted | Traces are lossy and an exfiltration path; audit needs the hash-chained journal | Traces as part of the audit record |
| DEC-74 | Latency budget: "under 1 ms" means p99 in-process time from input to order bytes ready (gate, order builder, draft canonicalization and hash), excluding the durable journal append (p99 under 5 ms, journal spec §5.3) and the broker round trip | Accepted | "Journal before acting" makes an end-to-end 1 ms impossible; all three panelists raised it independently | End-to-end 1 ms (impossible); dropping journal-before-acting (unsafe) |
| DEC-75 | GitHub (`kunwarshivam/mandate`) is the repository's home; CI is GitHub Actions, and `main` is protected by a GitHub ruleset requiring a PR, the six CI checks, and CODEOWNERS review (amends ADR-0001 ES-01, ES-12, ES-13; Depot dropped) | Accepted | Founder choice; GitHub Actions gates merges natively, with no bridge or extra vendor | Origin as home with Depot CI |
| DEC-76 | CI uses fewer Actions minutes: two required checks, `fast` (lint, test, spec guard) and `full` (fixture drift, reference, supply chain), instead of six; CI runs on pull requests and on demand, not on pushes to `main`; the nightly covers `main` and skips when unchanged; uv caching and per-job timeouts (amends ADR-0001 ES-12 and the checks named in DEC-75) | Accepted | Each job repeated the full setup and GitHub rounds every job up to a minute; the private-repo minute allowance was exhausted | Six parallel checks on every push and PR |
| DEC-77 | Two-PR mechanics for safety-critical stories: (1) the tests PR adds tests and API stubs; tests that cannot pass yet carry `#[ignore = "pending <story>"]`, and reference cases not listed in `status.toml` are pending (they run with `--include-ignored`); (2) the implementation PR changes code and, in test files, may only delete those `#[ignore = "pending <story>"]` lines; (3) a status-only PR, citing the story and this DEC, marks the cases `passing` in `status.toml` (amends ADR-0001 ES-15, which had the implementation PR flip `status.toml`). **Amendment (2026-09-27, the coordinator's ruling on [#174](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/174)).** A test that a review ruling requires in an implementation PR goes in-module, under `#[cfg(test)]` in the crate's `src/`, because item (2) lets an implementation PR change files under `tests/` only by deleting `#[ignore = "pending <story>"]` lines, unless the ruling explicitly names a `tests/` file, in which case the ruling cites DEC-77 and says why. The same ruling accepted where they stand the `tests/coverage.rs` additions merged with [#194](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/194), [#196](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/196), and [#198](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/198), which predate this note | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | ES-15 has the implementation PR flip `status.toml`, but ES-22 and the spec guard forbid changing `status.toml` together with code, and a tests PR whose tests fail cannot pass the required checks. This order satisfies both rules without weakening either | Exempt `status.toml` from the spec guard; merge tests PRs with failing checks through the admin bypass |
| DEC-78 | Agent workflow: the trust ladder (unrepresentable, checked, guided, reviewed) in `AGENTS.md`; a repo entry skill `mandate-mode` with playbooks and founder gates; a `verify-mandate` skill whose feature map CI keeps in sync; seven pstack skills (`how`, `why`, `tdd`, `blast-radius`, `interrogate`, `unslop`, `technical-writing`) and cursor-team-kit's `deslop` vendored unchanged under `.cursor/skills/` (MIT, pinned upstream commit), below `AGENTS.md` in precedence; new checks for debt markers, `#[allow]` without a reason, and `#[ignore]` without a pending story; mutants on the diff of safety-critical crates in the `full` check (completes ADR-0001 ES-12) | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | Agents can run unsupervised only in a repository whose structure and checks make the wrong change fail, with verification they run themselves; a correction written only as prose is followed sometimes ([talk](https://x.com/poteto/status/2102050467505430555)) | Adopt pstack's `poteto-mode` unmodified (built for another team's defaults); enable the plugins through `.cursor/settings.json` (tried: they did not load for cloud agents, and upstream text would change unreviewed) |
| DEC-79 | The founder is not a gate on routine work. Agents merge their own PRs (squash, into `main` only, never stacked on another PR's branch) when both required checks are green on the final head and an independent review agent on a different model has re-run `cargo xtask check` on that head and passed the diff against its story, specs, and `AGENTS.md`; safety-critical changes also need zero missed mutants and an adversarial review (pstack `interrogate`). Agents accept reversible engineering and process decisions themselves, recording them as "Accepted (agent)". Reserved for the founder: anything involving live trading, live credentials, or real money; spending or new paid services; legal and compliance text (terms, disclosures, anything counsel must confirm); and weakening an approved safety invariant or an `AGENTS.md` non-negotiable. The founder reviews after the fact and can revert or reverse any agent decision (amends ADR-0001 ES-13 and ES-15 and the "founder approves every merge" rule) | Accepted | Founder instruction, 2026-09-25: "i need you to not make me bottleneck". Review by a second model, CI, mutation testing, and the reference cases replace line-by-line human review | Founder reviews every PR (the bottleneck); agents merge on green CI alone, with no independent review |
| DEC-80 | No plain comments (`//`, `/* */`) in Rust code in `crates/` and `xtask/`; doc comments (`///`, `//!`) stay as each item's contract. The reason for a constraint goes in a name, a type, a test, an assertion message, or the item's doc comment. `cargo xtask markers` enforces it with a comment-aware scan that ignores string, raw-string, and char literals (resolves OD-11) | Accepted | Founder, 2026-09-25. Agents cite nearby comments to justify workarounds and copy them as precedent; the talk behind DEC-78 reports the same from a codebase that bans comments | Keep comments that state constraints (the old rule, followed only sometimes); ban doc comments too (loses the per-item contract and spec anchors) |
| DEC-81 | `risk_clock` is enforced, not optional. Every risk input of mandate spec §5.2 except the copied `ClockAdvanced` carries a required whole-second `risk_clock` payload field from its first schema version, and `append` rejects one lower than the stream's last (`risk_clock_regressed`). The journal vectors move to version 3: the `FillApplied` vector (seq 5) gains `risk_clock` `2026-09-21T14:00:01.000000000Z`, and the hashes that depend on it are regenerated. `OwnerAcknowledged` is recorded in the control stream and copied by the executor into the account stream, where it is a risk input (journal spec §2, §9; aligns the trading domain spec §12 and mandate spec §5.2). Delivered in three changes so `main` stays green: code that accepts the field, the version 3 vectors and spec text, then code that requires it | Accepted | Founder, 2026-09-25 ("do what is best for risk_clock"). A risk input without `risk_clock` cannot be placed on the risk clock, so replay could not reproduce limit timing (MI-13); both specs already required the field and only the vector omitted it | Keep `FillApplied` v1 without `risk_clock` and add it in a v2 schema at E6 (leaves a weaker path open and a schema version with no stored data behind it); make the field optional (unenforced) |
| DEC-82 | E3-1 crate shapes. `mandate-num` holds `Qty`, `SignedQty`, and `Price` (at most 9 fractional digits; `Price` positive), `Usd` and `CostBasis` (full precision), and `FeeRate`, `FeePerShare`, and `Bps` (non-negative), each a private `rust_decimal` field; every operation computes on 256-bit `ruint` intermediates and is exact or an error, and a formula with a rounding takes its scale and mode and rounds once. Parsing accepts only canonical text, so `mandate-num` takes `&str` and callers pass `DecStr::as_str()`: `mandate-num` and `mandate-canon` are both layer 0 and may not depend on each other. `FeePerShare` is added to the ES-04 list (TAF and CAT are USD per share, not a rate on money); `Ratio` waits for the story that needs it. `mandate-time` gains `TradingCalendar` (versioned range; weekdays not listed as holidays trade; settlement days also skip settlement holidays; any date outside the range is an error) and New York conversions through jiff with the bundled tzdb. No `mandate-domain` yet: `InstrumentId`, `Side`, and `AssetClass` live in `mandate-accounting` until a second crate needs them. `Price` holds 9 places, so the 12-place adjusted marks of §8.5 need their own type in E3-2 | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | ES-04 and ES-05 as written, within the layer rule. Canonical text is already exact, so no rounding can enter at the boundary | `mandate-num` depends on `mandate-canon` (breaks the layer rule); a new `mandate-domain` for three small types (a crate with no second user) |
| DEC-83 | The diff mutation gate (`cargo xtask ci mutants`) skips a safety-critical crate while its tests carry `#[ignore = "pending <story>"]` markers, and says so in its output. In a DEC-77 tests PR that crate's code is stubs that no live test runs, so every mutant would survive by construction; the implementation PR deletes the markers and replaces every stub body, so its diff puts all of that code under the gate. Tests PRs therefore contain stubs only, not logic | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79); founder to confirm | ADR-0001 ES-15 already assigns the mutation gate to the implementation PR ("an implementation PR that ... passes the mutation gate"); the gate joined the `full` check after the E5-1 tests PRs, so the conflict first appears in E3-1 | Mark stubs `#[cfg_attr(test, mutants::skip)]` (does not compile under `cfg(test)` without a new `mutants` dependency); founder-approved exclusions in `.cargo/mutants.toml` (those are for equivalent mutants); merge tests PRs through the admin bypass |
| DEC-84 | E3-1 accounting where the trading domain spec leaves a choice. (1) The fold is `Account::apply(input, config) -> Result<Applied>`, pure; a rejected input leaves the account unchanged; a `fill_id` already applied is `duplicate_fill` (order-level dedupe stays with E7-2). (2) Inputs come from journal events `FillApplied` and `LateFillApplied` (fill), `MarkUpdated` (reporting mark), `FeesCharged`, and `SettlementPosted` (§12). (3) The reporting mark is the latest `MarkUpdated`; with none, the last fill price, and a later fill never replaces a mark (§8.2); a position with neither makes unrealized P&L and equity an error (`no_mark`), never a guess. (4) Equity trade date: the New York date of the execution, the next day from 20:00 ET, then the first trading day on or after it; settlement is the first settlement day after the trade date (§2.2, §8.4). (5) `SettlementPosted` for D moves every unsettled bucket dated on or before D (I4). (6) Crypto USD fees accrue on the UTC date, since their charge is at 00:00 UTC (§6.3); they are already whole cents, so the charge equals the accrual; the equities charge is `round(daily total, 2, ceiling)` per trade date (§6.2). (7) Net realized = gross realized − all fees: accrued, charged, and asset fees at their USD value (§8.1). (8) A fill that crosses zero is split at the same price into a close and an open (§8.1) | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | Each is the reading the spec text and the reference cases support; the reference cases (RC-01, RC-02, RC-03, RC-07, RC-11, RC-13) agree wherever they exercise one | Reject crossing fills (the spec requires the split); round crypto charges again (no-op on whole cents but a second rounding in the code) |
| DEC-85 | The `trading_domain` reference-case harness interprets `fill`, `mark`, `fees_charged`, and `advance_clock` (settlement) steps and the accounting expectation keys (positions, cash, fees, realized, unrealized, equity, marks, conservation, trade and settlement dates). Every other step type, case key, initial-state field, instrument field, and expectation key fails the case with "not interpreted until <story>" naming the owning story (E3-2 corporate actions and income, E3-3 cash accounts, E4-1 backtests, E6-3 gate decisions, E6-5 kill switch, E6-6 buying power and day trades, E6-7 eligibility, E6-8 conduct, E6-9 account status and agent mode, E7-2 orders, E7-3 reconciliation, E7-4 order actions and quotes, E7-5 agents and external fills); an unknown key is a failure. `conservation` keys are changes since the case start, and the I1 identity is checked with them; `avg_cost` is compared as `round(B ÷ Q, 12, half_even)`; a step's fill ID is `step_<n>` | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | Every expectation key is checked or fails loudly, so no case passes on a key nobody read | Skip unknown keys (a case could pass unread); leave the suite unharnessed until every story lands |
| DEC-86 | A position's cost basis has the sign of its quantity: a long's basis is ≥ 0, a short's ≤ 0, and a flat position's is 0. `Position::new` rejects any other pair (`invalid_position`), so initial positions and the reference-case harness's fixture loading cannot build one, and every fold transition builds its result through the same check. A zero basis on an open position is allowed: corporate actions can create zero-cost shares, and a reduction can leave one. A reduction by \|q\| of \|Q\| first computes the rounded result U = B − R, R = `round(B × \|q\| ÷ \|Q\|, 12, half_even)` (spec §8.1, I2). The reduced basis is exactly U whenever U is on the position's side of zero or zero (U ≥ 0 for a long, U ≤ 0 for a short). Otherwise \|R\| > \|B\|: the removal is limited to B, and the position ends with zero basis. The limit binds only when the rounded removal exceeds the basis held, which requires B to have nonzero digits below the 12th decimal place: if B is a multiple of 10⁻¹², then \|B × \|q\| ÷ \|Q\|\| < \|B\|, and rounding to the 12-place grid cannot pass the grid point B. Such bases occur because B = q × p is exact to 18 places. Binding is a condition on the rounded removal, not on the exact formula: B 0.000000000000527 reduced by 19 of 20 has exact remaining basis 0.00000000000002635 (on the long side), yet R = 0.000000000001 > B, so the result is 0 rather than U = −0.000000000000473. Realized P&L is the proceeds less the basis actually removed, so total realized over a round trip is still cash received less cash paid. Property: `a_reduction_matches_the_rounded_formula_unless_the_removal_exceeds_the_basis_held` | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79); founder to confirm. Refines DEC-27 in the sub-grid case; the spec §8.1 and I2 wording follows in a spec-only change | Review of the E3-1 fold: a long of 1 with basis −100 was accepted, and closing it at 100 reported 200 realized. With the rule in the type's constructor, no caller can build that state; the limit makes every transition keep it | Check only flat positions (the defect); require a nonzero basis on open positions (a sub-grid reduction legitimately leaves 0); let a reduction push the basis past zero (a long with a credit basis) |
| DEC-87 | Fee configuration amounts and rates are non-negative by type. `FeeRate`, `FeePerShare`, and `Bps` already were; the TAF cap becomes `mandate_num::FeeCap`, which parses canonical USD text and rejects a negative value (`negative`). Each fill is charged under the fee configuration in force for it (spec §6.1 records the fee-configuration version per fill; §6.2 rates are effective-dated), so the cap and the cap mode can change between executions of one order. TAF = min(rate × shares, room). In `per_order` mode with a `client_order_id`, room = max(0, cap − TAF already charged on that order), where "already charged" counts every earlier execution of the order, in either mode. Otherwise room = cap (`per_execution`, or a fill without a client order ID, which is its own order). TAF already charged is never refunded when a later cap is lower. Consequences: every TAF is ≥ 0, so every fee on a fill is ≥ 0, accrued fees never decrease except by a charge, and a daily charge (`round(total, 2, ceiling)` of a non-negative total) is never a cash credit. A `per_order` execution leaves the order's TAF at most the larger of its total before and the cap in force, so an order charged only per order never exceeds the highest cap in force at any of its executions. (Refined in review round 3: without the floor, lowering the cap from 0.015 to 0 between two executions of one order charged a TAF of −0.015.) | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | Review of the E3-1 fold: a negative `Usd` cap gave a negative TAF per execution but 0 per order. Parsing at the boundary removes the state; one formula removes the inconsistency | Clamp a negative cap to 0 in both modes (keeps an invalid configuration representable); validate in `Config` construction (fields are public, so it could be bypassed) |
| DEC-88 | E2-1 dependencies: `reqwest` 0.13 with `default-features = false` and `rustls-no-provider`, plus `rustls` 0.23 with the `ring` provider (no aws-lc, OpenSSL, or native-tls); `parquet` 60 with only `arrow` and `snap`, and `arrow-array` and `arrow-schema` 60 to build columns; `clap` 4 with derive and without color or suggestions; `tokio` `~1.53` (ES-06) with `time` in `mandate-marketdata` and `rt` in `mandate-cli`; `secrecy` (ES-09); `serde` and `serde_json` with `raw_value` (ES-23). cargo-deny checks the targets we build and develop on (x86_64 and aarch64 Linux, aarch64 and x86_64 macOS) instead of every target | Accepted (agent) | reqwest's `rustls` feature pulls aws-lc-sys, whose license set includes OpenSSL, which ES-14 does not allow; ring is Apache-2.0 AND ISC. Parquet's default features add four C compression libraries; snap is pure Rust. The only crate outside the allowed licenses, `webpki-root-certs` (CDLA-Permissive-2.0), is a wasm32-only dependency of the platform verifier, so scoping cargo-deny to real targets keeps the license list unchanged | `ureq` (blocking; ES-06 already fixes tokio); aws-lc-rs with an OpenSSL license exception; `arrow` umbrella crate (more transitive crates); `lexopt` (no help or validation, and the CLI will grow `inspect`, `db migrate`, `oracle`); adding CDLA-Permissive-2.0 to the allowed licenses |
| DEC-89 | Market-data dataset format (E2-1): one Parquet file per symbol per UTC day under `<out>/alpaca/<feed>/<bars-<timeframe>\|trades>/<symbol>/`, with a canonical-JSON `manifest.json` recording the dataset, each decimal column's scale, and each fetched day (rows, bytes, SHA-256; empty days with rows 0 and no file). Ranges are whole, completed UTC days, half-open per day. Decimal columns are `Decimal128(38, s)`: trade prices and sizes at scale 9 (trading domain spec §2.1), and every bar column (open, high, low, close, volume, VWAP) at 18, because bars are vendor aggregates the spec does not bound and real BTC/USD 1-minute bars carry ten fractional digits in prices and VWAP and nine in volume (2026-09-21 to 25); a value needing more digits is rejected. Times are Parquet `Timestamp(ns, UTC)`, converted from `UtcNanos` with overflow checked. Records are stored raw (`adjustment=raw`, vendor order, no de-duplication). Stocks require `--feed sip` or `iex`; crypto is `crypto-us`. Every run re-fetches and compares: identical bytes are left untouched, new partitions are written atomically, and different bytes for a stored partition fail the run without touching the file. `Bar` and `Trade` stay in `mandate-marketdata` with `canon::DecStr` numbers until `mandate-domain` exists, then move there; `mandate_num::Price` and `Qty` hold at most 9 fractional digits, so they cannot carry bar values. Days come from `mandate_time::Date::next`; E3-1's `TradingCalendar` is not used, because `main` has no holiday table outside tests and closed days are stored as empty days. Recorded fixtures keep Alpaca's page tokens, so `.gitleaks.toml` allows the generic API-key rule's page-token matches only in the recorded fixture pages (`tests/fixtures/alpaca/*/page-<n>.json` and `requests.txt`), and the alpaca-key-id rule's literal sentinel only in the redaction test (`tests/http.rs`). Each exception needs both its path and its text to match (`condition = "AND"`, gitleaks 8.30.1), so the same text anywhere else is reported and every other rule still applies in those files. `cargo xtask ci supply-chain` plants each case in a temporary directory and fails if a planted finding is not reported or an allowed one is | Accepted (agent) | Per-day partitions make each file one query and one comparison, so idempotency is a byte comparison and an interrupted run resumes; fixed scales give every file of a dataset one schema; refusing to replace data keeps backtests reproducible from a snapshot (FR-4.5); an open day would change on the next run, so only completed days are fetched. `Timestamp(ns)` is what research tools read, and market data ends well before 2262 | Per-request files (a re-run with another range re-writes history); scale inferred from the data (schemas differ between days); `Decimal128(38, 9)` for bar prices, VWAP, or volume (rejects real BTC/USD bars, and volume would sit at the limit); scale 18 for trades too (trade prints are bounded by the spec); two integer time columns (unreadable by research tools); an overwrite flag (silently changes past backtests); `mandate_num::Price` and `Qty` for trade prices and sizes (no integer-unit accessor, so filling a `Decimal128` column goes back through text, and the scale check already rejects a tenth digit); `mandate_accounting::AssetClass` (market data would depend on accounting); creating `mandate-domain` here to share `AssetClass` (moves a type out of a safety-critical crate, outside this story) |
| DEC-90 | Research basket for M1 and later backtests: ETFs SPY, QQQ, IWM, TLT, GLD and stocks AAPL, MSFT, NVDA, AMZN, JPM, plus BTC/USD, in `config/research-basket.toml` | Accepted (agent) | Liquid names across equity indexes, small caps, Treasuries, gold, large technology, and a bank, with splits (AAPL, NVDA) and dividends (SPY, JPM) for E2-4. This is the team's internal research data, not an instrument choice for any user (AGENTS.md rule 11 governs the product) | A single index ETF (too little variety for gap and corporate-action tests); a broad universe (downloads and fixtures too large for M1) |
| DEC-91 | Corporate-action types and fold inputs (E3-2). `mandate_num::MarkPrice` is a positive price with at most 12 fractional digits (canonical text; `too_precise` beyond 12 places, `not_positive` at or below zero); every `Price` converts to one, and every mark and last fill price the fold stores is a `MarkPrice`, valued exactly by `SignedQty::value_at_mark`. `SplitRatio` is two positive integers new:old (`not_positive` otherwise); `ShareIncrement` is `Fractional` (10⁻⁹) or `Whole`. `SplitRatio::split` returns a `SplitQty`, the only holder of Q and Q' = Q × new ÷ old truncated toward zero to the increment, so the residual formulas can only be given a matching pair; `SplitRatio::mark`, `SplitQty::residual_basis`, and `SplitQty::cash_in_lieu` each round once. Journal events to `mandate_accounting::Input`: `CorporateActionApplied` → `CorporateAction(Split \| CashDividend)`; `DividendPaid` → `DividendPaid { instrument, ex_date }`; `CashInLieuPosted` → `CashInLieuPosted { instrument, amount }`. `CorporateActionPrepared` has no accounting effect. `CashDividend::new` rejects a pay date before the ex-date (`pay_date_before_ex_date`); per-share amounts and cash-in-lieu prices are positive `Price`s. The fold also reports `income`, `receivables` (signed: a short's dividend or cash in lieu is a payable), `net_receivables`, `total_pnl`, and `due(at)`, the settlements and dividends payable by an instant | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | DEC-82 reserved the 12-place mark type for E3-2. With the invariants in the types, a zero ratio, a payment before the ex-date, or a Q' that is not the truncation of Q cannot reach the fold through any public entry point | Widen `Price` to 12 places (order limits would accept 12-place prices); marks as `Usd` (18 places, no positivity); split inputs as raw integers and a free-standing Q' |
| DEC-92 | A split that leaves no share removes the whole basis. When Q' = 0 (a holding smaller than one post-split increment), R = B, B' = 0, the position is removed, and realized += cash in lieu − B. Otherwise R = round(B × (Q·new − Q'·old) ÷ (Q·new), 12, half_even) as spec §8.5 states, and B' = B − R keeps the sign of Q' (DEC-86) without any limit: Q' ≠ 0 means \|f\| < increment ≤ \|Q'\|, so \|Q_raw\| > 2\|f\| and \|B × f ÷ Q_raw\| < \|B\| ÷ 2; if \|B\| < 10⁻¹² that rounds to 0, and otherwise \|R\| ≤ \|B\| ÷ 2 + 5 × 10⁻¹³ ≤ \|B\|. With Q' = 0 the formula is R = round(B, 12, half_even), which is wrong for bases with digits below the 12th place: B = 5.27 × 10⁻¹⁶ gives R = 0 and a flat position holding basis; B = 5.27 × 10⁻¹³ gives R = 10⁻¹² > B. Tests: `a_split_leaving_no_share_removes_the_whole_basis`, `dust_splits_remove_exactly_the_basis_held`, property `a_split_removes_the_residuals_basis_and_never_more_than_the_basis_held` | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79); founder to confirm. Extends DEC-86 to splits; the spec §8.5 wording follows in a spec-only change | A position whose residual is all of it has nothing left to carry basis, so the removal is exactly what is held. The proof shows the §8.5 formula is already safe whenever a share remains, so only the Q' = 0 case changes | Apply DEC-86's limit (the Q' = 0, B = 5.27 × 10⁻¹⁶ case still leaves a flat position with basis, which `Position::new` rejects); reject such splits (the broker applies them regardless) |
| DEC-93 | Cash in lieu is round(f × p, 2, half_even), signed like Q (a short owes it), where p is the broker's price per post-split share, and 0 when none is posted. A nonzero amount is a receivable (payable for a short) keyed by instrument and ex-date. `CashInLieuPosted { instrument, amount }` settles the outstanding cash-in-lieu receivable of that instrument whose amount is exactly the posted amount, earliest ex-date first; if there is none it is `cash_in_lieu_mismatch` and changes nothing. A broker amount that differs is a reconciliation difference (spec §11, E7-3), corrected by a compensating event rather than by this input | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79); founder to confirm. The spec §2.1 table and §8.5 follow in a spec-only change | §8.5 writes "cash in lieu = f × broker price" without a rounding, but cash moves in cents and §2.1 rounds every other cash amount; half-even matches dividends. Matching exactly keeps a posting from settling an amount the fold did not expect | Keep full precision (a receivable no broker can pay exactly); round half-up; settle any posting and book the difference as P&L (hides a broker discrepancy from reconciliation) |
| DEC-94 | I3's market-value bound uses the raw quantity. For a split, ΔMV + f × mark' = Q'·mark' + f·mark' − Q·mark = Q_raw × (mark' − mark × old ÷ new) exactly, and \|mark' − mark × old ÷ new\| ≤ 5 × 10⁻¹³, so \|ΔMV + f × mark'\| ≤ \|Q_raw\| × 5 × 10⁻¹³, and it is 0 when mark × old ÷ new terminates within 12 places. The spec's \|Q'\| × 5 × 10⁻¹³ is smaller whenever f ≠ 0 and does not hold: Q 1 marked 1.000000001, split 16:3, gives 8 × 10⁻¹² ÷ 3 against a stated bound of 7.9999999995 × 10⁻¹² ÷ 3 (`i3_is_bounded_by_the_raw_quantity_not_the_split_quantity`). The fold is unchanged: the mark is rounded once as §8.5 states; only the stated bound moves | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-26) | The identity is exact, and the example meets the corrected bound with equality, so no rounding of the adjusted mark to 12 places meets the old one | Keep \|Q'\| and test with it (fails on the example); round the adjusted mark to more places (breaks the 12-place mark of DEC-82 and §2.1) |
| DEC-95 | Fold interpretations for corporate actions (E3-2). (1) An action is `duplicate_corporate_action` if one of the same kind was already applied to the instrument on that ex-date; otherwise `corporate_action_out_of_order` if its ex-date is on or before the trade date of a fill already applied in the instrument, or before an ex-date already applied there. A fill whose trade date is before an applied ex-date is `fill_before_corporate_action`. A split and a dividend may share an ex-date. A rejection changes nothing. (2) A dividend is round(Q × d, 2, half_even) on the position when it is applied (negative for a short: a payable); income moves by that amount on the ex-date; a nonzero amount is a receivable due at 00:00 New York on the pay date, and a zero amount (flat, or rounding to 0) leaves none, though the action still counts as applied. `DividendPaid` settles the dividend of that instrument and ex-date, else `no_dividend_due`. The broker's amount is authoritative through reconciliation (§11), not through `DividendPaid`. (3) `due(at)` lists the settlement dates with a bucket and the dividends whose pay-date midnight is at or before `at`, ordered by date, settlements before dividends on one date, then by instrument and ex-date. (4) Every stored mark, the latest `MarkUpdated` and the last fill price, for held and flat instruments, is replaced by round(mark × old ÷ new, 12, half_even); one that rounds to 0 is rejected (`not_positive`). (5) Equity = settled + Σ unsettled + net receivables − accrued fees + Σ market value, and total P&L = realized (gross) + unrealized + income − fees (§8.2); I1 holds exactly for every input, splits included. (6) Tax lots (§8.5) are out of scope: the fold keeps one average-cost position per instrument | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | Entitlement is the position after the fills traded before the ex-date (§8.5); applying actions in ex-date order after exactly those fills makes the position at application the entitlement, and the checks reject any journal order in which it would not be. Duplicates are errors as fills are (DEC-84) | Ignore a duplicate action (hides a redelivery bug); reorder inputs inside the fold (it is a pure function of journal order); entitle on settlement date; round dividends half-up |
| DEC-96 | The `trading_domain` harness interprets `corporate_action_applied` (split and cash_dividend; a split needs the instrument's `fractionable`), `broker_cash_posting` of type `cash_in_lieu`, `advance_clock` emitting `SettlementPosted` and `DividendPaid` in `due` order, and the expectation keys `receivables` (the net amount), `income`, and `conservation.income`. The conservation baseline is the first state at which every compared value is defined (a case that opens without a mark counts from its first mark). `corporate_action_prepare` is owned by E7-4: it cancels orders and has no accounting effect. Everything else still fails as "not interpreted until <story>" (DEC-85) | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | Every key of an interpreted expectation is checked, so RC-05, RC-06, and RC-23 pass only on values the fold computed | Interpret `corporate_action_prepare` as a no-op now (RC-04 would pass without its order actions being checked) |
| DEC-97 | Autonomous ideation. The mandate is a risk envelope and a goal, not a strategy. Envelope fields (every field of today's mandate except `universe.instruments`, plus a new `universe.max_instruments`; this includes the signal-model set with fixed user weights, DEC-47, thresholds, band, protection distances, and cadence) stay user-confirmed under every existing rule, including change classification, and the compiler and templates may propose values for them (provenance `platform_proposed`, shown as proposed). Strategy fields (the working universe and its theses) are produced at runtime by a new **research agent**, one signal model with a user-confirmed weight, journaled (`ThesisProposed` in the agent stream; `UniverseChanged` in the account stream as a risk input), bounded by the envelope, and leave the hashed document. An instrument is admitted only through the eligibility floor, the policy's asset classes, `max_instruments`, the instrument-group claim, and the autonomy rules (new condition fields `new_instrument` and `thesis_confidence`; platform default for admissions `ask`). The agent's own memory feeds the research agent. Bring-your-own-strategy stays as a mode that pins the universe. The order builder, gate, ladder, floor, journal, and kill switches are unchanged: the research agent produces opinions, never orders (DEC-04). Design in [ADR-0002](../adr/0002-autonomous-ideation-and-retail.md) | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-26); supersedes DEC-33, DEC-38, DEC-45, and DEC-62 (agent memory); amends DEC-43 (`universe.instruments` is runtime state; every other classification row stands) and DEC-52 (directional outputs allowed for the research agent); `AGENTS.md` rule 11 restated. The mandate spec, schemas, reference implementation, and cases follow in spec-change PRs before M5 | Founder direction: the product is a fully autonomous agent that brings its own ideas. A platform that forbids itself ideas serves only users who already have strategies, and the safety architecture never depended on who chose the instrument | An LLM signal model over a user-chosen universe; the LLM drafts the whole mandate and the user confirms, with no runtime changes; keep the software-only posture |
| DEC-98 | Retail from the start, and the software-only posture is withdrawn. Retail workspaces stay the default (DEC-68); the retail profile becomes `auto_allowed: true`, `signal_model_types` including `llm`, `protection_required: true`, leveraged ETPs off, with the lifetime-loss ceiling and approval-timeout minimum set with counsel. Working assumption: Mandate may be an investment adviser under the Advisers Act and plans for registration or a counsel-approved structure; counsel engagement moves from M13 to before the Phase 1 exit; no live trading for any user until counsel signs off (`AGENTS.md` rule 8 unchanged, so retail users trade paper until then). Connectors: Alpaca first (unchanged), **Robinhood Agentic Trading** second (retail US equities and crypto spot over MCP into the customer's dedicated, customer-funded agentic account; options out of scope, DEC-24), Kraken Derivatives US third; Fidelity deferred until it offers an official trading API; no aggregator in the order path. Design in [ADR-0002](../adr/0002-autonomous-ideation-and-retail.md) | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-26); supersedes DEC-61; amends DEC-23 (connector order) and the compliance document's posture (v0.6) | Founder direction: adoption through consumer brokers rather than funds. Robinhood Agentic Trading (beta since 2026-05-27; options from 2026-07-06 and crypto from 2026-08-17) is an official path for third-party agents; Fidelity has no public trading API and aggregators cannot trade on either | Retail after design partners (the original plan); Fidelity first; trade through an aggregator |
| DEC-99 | Evidence loop. Historical backtests of LLM-originated theses are not evidence of thesis quality, because the model may have been trained on what happened after the test period (lookahead through training data). They stay valid for testing mechanics (sizing, the gate, accounting). Evidence of thesis quality comes only from forward paper trading: every thesis is scored after its horizon against pre-registered baselines (buy-and-hold of the eligible basket, and a broad index ETF), net of modeled costs. Signal-model scorecards (E15-3) move to Phase 1 as Must. The Phase 1 exit adds a quality gate: research-agent theses beat the baselines on forward paper over a pre-registered evaluation window and metric. The window, the metric, and the pass threshold are set before the evaluation starts and recorded as a decision (open for the founder, [design questions](09-mandate-rewrite-questions.md) 6) | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-26); amends the Phase 1 exit of [ADR-0002](../adr/0002-autonomous-ideation-and-retail.md) part 9 | A backtest over a period the model has read about scores hindsight, so thesis quality looks better than it is (R-27). Forward paper trading is the only test whose outcomes the model cannot have seen. Setting the window, metric, and threshold in advance keeps the result from being chosen after the fact | Historical backtests as evidence of thesis quality (contaminated by training data); ship the research agent without a quality gate |
| DEC-100 | Correlated flow across accounts. One research-agent version running in many workspaces can send correlated orders into one instrument. Each workspace's risk gate stays the only binding control and decides from that workspace's own mandate, policy, state, and market data, including its participation cap (trading domain spec §9.6); no other workspace's state is ever a gate input (DEC-09). On top of that, and never loosening any workspace's own limits, three controls apply to research-agent theses: (1) **aggregate-flow monitoring**, an operator service in the workspace deployment (a managed cell, or the customer's hybrid or on-prem site), outside the trade path, sums research-agent exposure and order flow per instrument over the workspaces that deployment hosts, in dollars and as a share of the instrument's average daily dollar volume, and alerts the deployment's operator above thresholds set before live trading; it reads only its own deployment's workspaces, writes to none of them, and sends nothing to the global control plane (DEC-10, HLD §4); (2) an **operator per-thesis halt**, a kill switch at the operator level of HLD §8: the operator names an instrument and optionally the research-agent version (its pinned content hash, DEC-67), and every workspace the operator's deployment hosts stops research-agent admissions and openings that match, while exits and protection continue (DEC-05 unchanged). The halt only removes permissions, is journaled in each workspace as a `PlatformOperatorAction` (the action kind is added by E17-6's spec change), and is the only way one workspace's situation changes another's decisions. The managed operator's halt reaches managed cells only; hybrid and on-prem operators hold their own (HLD §8); (3) **staggered execution**: each workspace delays research-agent openings on a new thesis by a deterministic offset derived from its own workspace ID and the thesis, spread over a window inside the regular session and within the conduct controls, so accounts do not act at once without sharing any state. Bring-your-own-strategy agents keep per-account controls only | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-26; narrowed in review of #52 to keep DEC-09, DEC-10, and HLD §4: the aggregate cap became monitoring plus an operator halt); thresholds and the stagger window open for the founder ([design questions](09-mandate-rewrite-questions.md) 7) | Per-account limits bound each account, not the sum. One thesis admitted in many accounts at once concentrates flow and market impact in one instrument, and the accounts compete for the same liquidity (R-26). A faulty thesis has to stop in every account at once. A binding cross-workspace cap would make one tenant's positions an input to another tenant's gate and put a shared service in the trade path; monitoring plus an explicit, risk-reducing operator halt gives the aggregate view and the stop without either. Until the operator halts, aggregate flow is bounded by the sum of the per-account caps | Per-account limits only (misses herding and market impact); one thesis per account (does not bound aggregate flow); a binding platform-wide cap in every workspace's gate (cross-tenant gate input, a shared service in the trade path, and no place for hybrid and on-prem workspaces) |
| DEC-101 | Research inputs and admission hardening. The research agent reads only sources on a vetted allowlist, kept as versioned configuration. An admission requires corroboration by at least one independent source, or by market data consistent with the thesis, and the corroboration is recorded in `ThesisProposed`. The input-drift detector (E17-5, `unusual_input`, V-018) becomes Must in Phase 1. Simulation fuzzing includes prompt-injection fixtures across every input source | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-26) | The research agent chooses instruments from public text, so one planted article could otherwise admit an instrument (R-05). The allowlist limits who can write the agent's inputs, corroboration stops a single source from admitting alone, and the drift detector escalates unusual inputs before the agent acts on them | Open web inputs; rely on the eligibility floor and `ask` alone |
| DEC-102 | Counsel now. Securities counsel is engaged during Phase 0, in parallel with engineering, not only before the Phase 1 exit. No live trading until counsel signs off (unchanged) | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-26); amends the counsel timing of DEC-98 and [ADR-0002](../adr/0002-autonomous-ideation-and-retail.md) part 7 | Counsel's answers shape the research agent, the retail profile, onboarding, and the go-live screen (compliance questions 31 to 35). Answers that arrive at the Phase 1 exit would force rework of what Phase 1 built | Counsel before the Phase 1 exit (ADR-0002's original timing) |
| DEC-103 | Phase 1 thin slice. The first research-agent slice runs only in the team's internal paper workspaces, never on a user's agent. Its data universe is fixed to the research basket (DEC-90), which stays the team's internal research and test data: the research agent reads and proposes only for basket instruments, and each admission still passes that workspace's own envelope (the eligibility floor, the policy's asset classes, `max_instruments`, and the autonomy rules) with every admission `ask`, paper only, and scorecards on. The basket is never offered to a user as an instrument choice: users' agents get the research agent with the full E17-3, only after the DEC-99 evaluation passes on the thin slice, and then each user's envelope alone governs which instruments may be admitted. The evaluation scores the team's own paper results, so no user's results are aggregated (mandate spec §8.1) | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-26; worded in review of #52 so that DEC-90 stands unchanged) | A fixed, liquid test universe takes eligibility and injection risk out of admission, so the evaluation measures the theses alone. The admission machinery grows only after thesis quality is shown | Full dynamic admission first; offer the basket to users as a starter universe (contradicts DEC-90 and `AGENTS.md` rule 11) |
| DEC-104 | E3-3 settlement and buying power in the fold where the trading domain spec leaves a choice. (1) The account type (`AccountType`, cash or margin; spec §7.2) is state of the broker account, not pinned configuration: `Account::opening(account_type, settled, positions)` sets it with no default, `Account::account_type` reports it, and it does not change within a fold (a change arrives as account state, E6-9). (2) `Account::buying_power(reservations)` is the model buying power of §7.2: settled cash, plus Σ unsettled in a margin account, less reservations, less the charges the accrued fees will post. The accrued-fee term is Σ over open (family, day) buckets of round(bucket, 2, ceiling), the amount `FeesCharged` will debit (§6.2), not round(Σ accrued, 2, ceiling): the two agree while one bucket is open (every reference case), otherwise the per-bucket sum is at most a cent per bucket more conservative, and it makes settled cash after every charge exactly the buying power reported before them. (3) Reservations are the account ledger's (§9.5, E6-6); the fold takes their total as a `Reservations` value that rejects a negative amount (`negative`) and is `Reservations::NONE` until E6-6. The broker-side minimum of §7.2 (`non_marginable_buying_power`; the lower of model and broker) is the gate's (E6-6, E6-9). (4) The fold records every fill the broker reports, including one that creates a debit, and buying power then reports the shortfall; the no-debit rule (§8.3, I4) is the gate's promise, tested as a property whose generator approves buys with an independent buying-power check. (5) Under §8.3's cash movements a cash account's settled cash is not ≥ 0 after every approved order: the charge on a sale is debited from settled cash before the proceeds settle, so a debit of at most those charges exists while a bucket is unsettled. What holds, and what the property asserts: after every approved buy, settled ≥ 0 (cash) and buying power ≥ 0 (both types); after every event, settled + Σ unsettled − accrued ≥ 0 (both); a cash account's settled cash is ≥ 0 whenever nothing is unsettled and, while something is, never below minus the charges posted since then. A spec-only change follows for the §8.3 and I4 wording for cash accounts and for §7.2's "round(accrued, 2, ceiling)", which item 2's per-bucket rounding contradicts | Items 1 to 4: Accepted (agent, under DEC-79). Item 5: Proposed (founder), since it reads a safety rule more loosely than its text (AGENTS.md rule 9); until decided, the I4 property asserts the strictest reading the fee model's rounded charges allow, listed in item 5, and the hand test `a_cash_account_sale_can_leave_a_fee_debit_until_its_proceeds_settle` shows the debit the text does not cover | Each is the reading the spec text and RC-08 and RC-18 support. (2) is the safe reading of a formula the spec leaves ambiguous (AGENTS.md rule 3) and the only one under which a charge never takes settled cash below the buying power reported before it. (5) was found by the I4 property while writing this story's tests | The account type in `Config` (it is observed broker state, and accounts sharing a fee schedule can differ); a default of margin (a cash account read as margin overstates buying power); rejecting debit-creating fills in the fold (fills are broker facts, and external fills and fees create debits); netting fees from sale proceeds (contradicts §8.3's cash movements) |
| DEC-105 | The `trading_domain` harness interprets `initial.account.type` (the alpaca profile defaults to margin and has no cash accounts; a generic broker's case must state its type) and the `buying_power` expectation as the fold's buying power with no reservations, which is honest only while no `propose_order` step is interpreted: if E6-3 interprets `propose_order` before E6-6 tracks reservations, RC-18 fails loudly rather than passing. `propose_order` and `decision` stay E6-3's: a verdict's reason code is the first failing check in §9.1 order (RC-08's `insufficient_settled_buying_power` asserts that every earlier check passed), which an accounting-only comparison cannot establish, so no case passes on a key nobody checked (DEC-85). RC-08 and RC-18 `generic_cash_account` stay pending on E6-3 and E6-6, with their accounting steps reproduced by hand tests (`settlement::rc_08_…`, `settlement::rc_18_…`) and by a harness test that runs the two cases without their gate step (`harness::rc_08_…`, `harness::rc_18_…`). E3-3 moves no case to passing, so its DEC-77 sequence is a tests PR and an implementation PR with no status PR | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | The buying-power arithmetic behind RC-08's deny and RC-18's allow is checked where it is decidable, in hand tests, and the verdict is left to the gate that decides it | Interpreting `propose_order` for the buying-power check alone (RC-08 would pass with its verdict unverified); running a case's steps up to its first pending item (the case still fails, and the report loses the full list of owning stories) |
| DEC-106 | E4-1 backtest fill model where trading domain spec §6.4 leaves a choice ([task brief](tasks/E4-1-simulated-execution.md) "Interpretations"). (1) `mandate-sim` is safety-critical (lint header, mutation gate, DEC-77 sequence), because a flattering fill model misleads the owner at go-live (DEC-97). (2) A fill price with more than 9 places (§2.1) rounds adversely at 9 places: buys up, sells down; fills are still not tick-rounded. (3) `sqrt` impact is `coefficient_bps × √(fill qty ÷ reference volume)` in basis points; the root is taken at 18 places, rounded up. (4) The previous bar in the same session is the most recent earlier bar of that session and trading day; a bar with no earlier bar is the session's first bar only if its session started at or after the data's coverage start (then the 20-session median, a missing minute counting as volume 0, unavailable with fewer than 20 prior sessions); otherwise the reference is unavailable and the cap is 0. (5) Auction bars are an input flag (the first regular-session bar of a covered trading day, or the first bar after a halt reopens); a resting limit gapped through at an auction fills at the open with no slippage and no liquidity flag. (6) Market, stop, triggered stop-limit, and marketable-limit fills are taker; resting-limit fills are maker. (7) A stop-limit whose trigger bar opens beyond its limit does not fill in that bar ("no fill", rule 7) and rests as a limit from the next bar. (8) The first fill of either OCO leg, partial or full, cancels the other leg; a take-profit remainder keeps resting at its limit and a stop remainder is a market order (item 10). (9) Orders trade the regular session, plus pre-market and after-hours when `extended_hours` is set, and never the overnight session (DEC-30); equity stops trigger only on regular-session bars; a day order's remainder is canceled after its last eligible session. (10) A trigger or phase change happens on the bar that causes it even when the cap allows no fill; a triggered stop's remainder, and an OCO's remainder after its stop leg fills, is a market order; an OCO leg is canceled only by a fill of the other leg. (11) The `trading_domain` harness interprets `bars`, `orders`, `isolation: per_order`, `first_bar_reference_volume`, `resting_since_bar`, and `resting_since: previous_trading_day` (already resting, never marketable on arrival) for RC-10, RC-12, and RC-19, and compares liquidity only where a case states it | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | Each choice is the one that never makes a backtest look better than the market allowed, and each reproduces all 15 expected fills of RC-10, RC-12, and RC-19 (recomputed by hand in the brief) | Round fill prices half-even; keep a triggered stop's remainder under the stop rule (it would not fill once the price recovers above the stop, which a real market order would); treat the `sqrt` coefficient as a fraction; take the reference volume from the last bar in the data whatever its session; mark auction fills maker; let an OCO partial fill leave the other leg working |
| DEC-107 | E5-2 artifact store. (1) The pure core in `mandate-journal` owns `ArtifactRef` (`sha256:{hex}` of the exact stored bytes, journal spec §6.3, never a re-encoding: callers storing JSON pass canonical bytes), the `ArtifactSource` (read) and `ArtifactStore` (put) traits, and the checked read `get_artifact`; a map implements both for pure code and tests. (2) The filesystem backend is a new safety-critical adapter crate, `mandate-artifacts-fs` (layer 6, impure, no external dependencies), because the pure core has no filesystem I/O; this adds it to the ADR-0001 ES-02 crate list. Object storage with object lock (§6.2) is a later backend behind the same traits. (3) A put is idempotent and write-once: an object is never replaced, and an existing object that no longer re-hashes makes the put `Corrupt` and stays as evidence (§11: nothing is repaired in place). A read is `Missing`, `Corrupt`, or `Unavailable` (retry); verification counts `Unavailable` as `artifact_missing`, so an unreachable store never passes. (4) On disk, `<root>/sha256/<first two hex>/<64 hex>`, read-only, written under `<root>/tmp/`, flushed, hard-linked into place (a link never replaces a name), and the directory flushed | Accepted (agent) | ES-02 keeps the core free of I/O, and Phase 0 needs a store that persists across processes; content addressing makes idempotency and corruption detection the same check; a hard link publishes atomically without ever replacing an object | Filesystem code in `mandate-journal` (breaks ES-02); in `mandate-journal-pg` (E5-3 is the Postgres journal); publishing by rename (silently replaces an existing, possibly corrupt, object); addressing a canonical re-encoding (§6.3 hashes the stored bytes, and artifacts include non-JSON data) |
| DEC-109 | E5-3 Postgres journal where journal spec §6.1 and ADR-0001 ES-08 leave a choice ([task brief](tasks/E5-3-postgres-journal.md) "Interpretations"). (1) Client: sqlx 0.9 with `postgres`, `runtime-tokio`, `migrate`, and `tls-rustls-ring-native-roots`, without query macros, so builds need no database and no committed `.sqlx` metadata; this amends ES-08's "macros … offline query metadata committed". (2) Schema: §6.1's tables and checks; `seq`, `schema_version`, and `writer_epoch` as `bigint`; `recorded_at` as the canonical nanosecond text; `events` references `event_ids` through a foreign key deferred to commit; `stream_heads` adds `risk_clock`, the stream's latest, NULL until the first. (3) The database enforces the chain (`seq` = last + 1, `prev_hash` = the last hash, 64 zeros at `seq` 1), heads that only advance to a stored event (epoch and `risk_clock` never lowered or cleared), and append-only tables: `BEFORE UPDATE`, `DELETE`, and `TRUNCATE` triggers raise SQLSTATE 23001 for every role, and the application role has only INSERT and SELECT on `events` and `event_ids` and the head updates the trigger allows. (4) Append is one transaction holding the head row (`SELECT … FOR UPDATE`) after drafts are validated, rerun whole on a concurrent `event_id` clash or serialization failure. (5) An error before or in response to COMMIT is `Unavailable` (rolled back); a connection lost during COMMIT is `Ambiguous`. (6) `rows` re-runs §11 checks 1 to 5 from `seq` 1; `event`, each event a retry returns, and the head event an append chains onto re-run checks 1, 2, and 4 on that row, and the head event's hash must be the head row's; a failure is an `IntegrityError`, never a result; checks 6 and 7 are the artifact store's, so reads pass over them. (7) Tests skip without `MANDATE_PG_URL` and fail without it when `MANDATE_PG_REQUIRED` is set; `cargo xtask ci postgres` runs them in `full` against PostgreSQL 18 and nightly against 17, never in `fast` | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79); amends ES-08 on query macros | (1) Query macros would need a live database or committed metadata in every build and `sqlx-cli` in CI, for a crate with a handful of statements that the per-PR Postgres tests execute anyway, so a SQL mistake still fails CI before merge. (2) Postgres timestamps hold microseconds, and `column_mismatch` compares `recorded_at` with the body exactly; the head's `risk_clock` makes §2's check one row read under the lock. (3) Privileges alone do not bind the owner or a superuser; triggers do, and a 23001 names the rule. (5) An error response to COMMIT means Postgres rolled back, so only a lost connection leaves the outcome unknown (§5.3). (6) §6.1 forbids re-serializing to verify, and a read that trusted the table would return tampered bytes. (7) The `full` job already pays the setup; `fast` stays database-free | Query macros with committed `.sqlx` metadata (ES-08 as written); `tokio-postgres` (ES-08 rejected it); `timestamptz` for `recorded_at` (loses nanoseconds); scanning the stream for the last `risk_clock` on every append; `SERIALIZABLE` without the row lock (retries under contention instead of queueing); Postgres tests in `fast` (a service container on every push of every PR) |
| DEC-110 | The `fast` check runs every test in the workspace marked `#[ignore = "pending <story>"]` in one nextest invocation (`cargo xtask ci pending`, after `test`) and fails if one passes on the change's code or does not run; a failure by panic, `todo!()` included, counts. The gate needs no base commit, so it runs the same on a PR, on `main`, and locally (amends ADR-0001 ES-12, which listed no such job) | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79); founder to confirm | A DEC-77 tests PR marks a test pending because it cannot pass on the stubs; one that passes anyway pins nothing and turns green in the implementation PR with no behaviour behind it, which review alone let through in #66. The check only adds a failure mode and weakens nothing; on the open tests PRs it runs 13 to 53 tests in under a second | Run only tests newly marked pending against the merge base (a pending test whose body a later PR edits would go unchecked); leave it to review (the rung-4 check #66 already passed) |
| DEC-111 | Thesis revision loop. A research-agent thesis that fails on forward paper is neither discarded silently nor rewritten in place: the research agent may propose a *revised* thesis, a new version in the same lineage, that names what the autopsy found and what changed, journaled as `ThesisRevised` with a link to its predecessor.
