(1) A revision is scored only by the forward-paper evaluator of DEC-99 (E17-8); a backtest may check a revision's mechanics, never score it. (2) A revised thesis starts with no track record: scorecards never carry a predecessor's score forward, and every report shows the lineage's revision count. (3) A revision is a proposal like any thesis: it passes the eligibility floor, corroboration (DEC-101), and the autonomy rules, and it can never loosen an envelope field. (4) A lineage is capped by `max_revisions_per_lineage`, an envelope field (default 3 in the thin slice); past the cap the lineage is retired and the owner is told. (5) No revision loop ships before E17-8 exists and the DEC-99 evaluation has run once on the DEC-103 thin slice. The `ThesisRevised` event and the envelope field are recorded for the Track C rewrite (journal spec event catalogue, mandate spec; the original row said "journal spec §12", corrected in #109 because the events live in the catalogue at §9, not in §12 Export — a cross-reference fix, not a change of decision) | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-26) | The loop the industry is racing to build, a strategy that reads its own autopsy and rewrites itself, compounds overfitting exactly as fast as it compounds returns when the evaluator can be gamed (R-27, R-28). A forward-paper evaluator that the model cannot have seen is the only thing that makes the loop safe, so it is built first and the loop is gated on it; journaled lineage keeps the failures visible instead of buried in a rewrite | Self-rewriting strategies scored on backtests (the machine the trade press describes); no revision at all (loses the mechanism a fund uses to improve, the gap this decision closes); revisions exempt from admission rules because the predecessor was admitted |
| DEC-112 | Documentation-only changes take a short path through the two required checks. `.github/scripts/docs-only.sh` classifies the PR from its merge base: Markdown anywhere, `docs/` (except the reference-case YAML), `CODEOWNERS`, and `LICENSE*` are documentation; the feature map and everything else are code. A documentation-only change runs `typos`, the spec guard's protected-path rule, and the trailer check in shell (`.github/scripts/docs-checks.sh`, mirroring `cargo xtask ci spec-guard`) with no Rust toolchain, cache, or Postgres; every other change runs the unchanged `cargo xtask ci fast` and `full`. The Postgres container starts as a step on that path only | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79; founder asked for it on 2026-09-26 with 90 percent of the month's Actions minutes used) | A docs PR cost about six runner minutes for checks that build nothing it changed, and the up-to-date rule re-runs every PR after each merge; the short path costs under two. The two required checks keep their names, so the ruleset and DEC-76 are unchanged; the shell mirror of the spec guard is small and its rules are listed in the script | Skipping CI for docs paths with `paths-ignore` (the required checks would never report and every docs PR would be unmergeable); building xtask on the short path (cuts only half the cost); making the checks optional (DEC-76 requires them) |
| DEC-113 | The ruleset's up-to-date rule is off while the month's GitHub Actions budget is nearly spent: the two required checks (`fast`, `full`) stay required, but a PR whose checks passed against an older `main` may merge without a re-run. Compensations: one merge queue, run serially by the coordinating session, which runs `cargo xtask check` on `main` locally after each code merge and reverts a merge that breaks it; the nightly workflow covers `main` daily; the rule is restored when the budget resets (2026-10-01) unless the queue has shown it unnecessary | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79; the founder delegated the day's engineering on 2026-09-26 with 90 percent of the budget used) | With the rule on, every merge re-runs every open PR's CI (about a third of the day's 155 runs); with four builders in parallel that would exhaust the budget within hours. The risk is a semantic conflict between two PRs that each passed alone; the local check on `main` after each merge catches it at zero CI cost | Keep the rule and stop parallel work; make the checks optional (DEC-76 forbids it); pay for more minutes (the founder's call, not taken today) |
| DEC-114 | E4-1 implementation readings, where trading domain spec §6.4 and §5.1 leave a choice that neither DEC-106 nor DEC-108 covers. (1) **Crossed prices are strictly crossed.** `StopLimitCrossed` and `OcoLegsCrossed` reject a limit or a take-profit that lies *strictly* on the far side of its stop; a stop-limit whose limit equals its stop, and a protective pair whose legs sit at one price, are accepted and simulated. Neither puts a leg on the wrong side of the market, which is what §5.1 and §5.4 forbid, and a stop-limit at its stop is an ordinary order the builder may produce. (2) **§6.4 rule 6's regular-session condition is enforced by the v1 order policy, not by a second test inside the walk.** A stop leg triggers on any bar the walk reaches it on, and a triggered stop's remainder is a market order (DEC-106 item 10). Rule 6 still holds, because the walk reaches a leg only on a bar the order may trade (rule 2) and only a limit order may carry `extended_hours` (spec §5.2, DEC-108 item 7): a stop order's tradable bars therefore *are* its triggerable bars — the regular session for an equity, the continuous session for crypto (DEC-106 item 9). The one input this reading would part company with the spec on is an equity bar labelled `Continuous`, which contradicts spec §4.3 and which the market-data layer never produces | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | Both are the literal reading of the rule and of the error's own wording, neither can make a backtest look better than the market allowed, and neither changes a fill in RC-10, RC-12, or RC-19. (2) puts rule 6 on the trust ladder's checked rung — `ExtendedHoursNeedsALimit`, a typed error with a test — instead of a runtime branch that no accepted input can reach and that the mutation gate therefore reports as untested | (1) Reject an equal limit and stop as crossed (it rejects an order the order builder may legitimately place); (2) keep an asset-class session test inside the walk (no input the model accepts can make it bind, so the mutation gate reports it as equivalent to `true`, and a check nothing exercises is the kind of code the next agent copies) |
| DEC-116 | `inspect` reports a quotes dataset's prices over the *quoted* sides of each quote. Alpaca sends a zero price, a zero size, and a blank exchange for the side holding no order, so a zero is an absent order and not a price of zero: it is left out of the bid and ask extremes, out of the spread statistics, and out of the split-adjusted prices, and counted instead. (1) The summary gives each side's lowest and highest price with the number of rows quoting it, and the classes of every row: two-sided (of which locked, bid = ask, and crossed, bid > ask), one-sided (exactly one side quoted), and unquoted (neither); the three classes cover every row exactly once, which a named test asserts on every recorded and scripted fixture. (2) The spread is the ask minus the bid over two-sided rows and keeps its sign, so a crossed quote's spread is negative and the narrowest spread may be below zero. (3) The summary reports the narrowest spread, the widest, and the total with its row count, never a mean: the quotient of two exact decimals is not one (ES-23, DEC-89), and the total with the count gives a reader the mean exactly. (4) Quote sizes are not totalled: the top of the book is a standing offer, not traded volume. (5) Storage is unchanged and still raw (DEC-89): nothing is dropped or normalized, and only the summary judges; a price that is neither zero nor positive is summarized as stored rather than hidden. (6) Quotes sharing a time and both exchange codes are one duplicate, as a trade's key is its time, ID, exchange, and tape | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | [The E2-3 brief](tasks/E2-3-quotes.md) leaves the shape of the quote statistics to the CLI slice, and spec §8.2 puts the sanity rule (0 < bid ≤ ask) in the readers that mark with a quote, not in storage or in a report. Including a zero side would make "lowest bid 0" the answer for almost every equity dataset, which is the recorded CPHC day exactly: one of its 147 quotes is one-sided. An unsigned spread would hide the crossed quotes a slippage model must see, which is what the story asks the data for. Neither choice touches a stored byte, weakens a safety rule, or reaches a trading path | Include a zero side raw (the extremes stop telling a researcher anything); drop one-sided rows from `rows` as well (the count would stop matching the manifest, which DEC-89 makes the record); report an absolute spread or only two-sided quotes' presence (loses the crossed quotes the story wants); report a mean spread (not an exact decimal); total the bid and ask sizes (a number with no meaning, since the same standing offer is counted on every tick) |
| DEC-117 | `universe.max_instruments` bounds the working universe: the platform proposes a default of 5 and enforces a ceiling of 20, the same for retail. Organizations and workspaces may set a lower maximum through the policy hierarchy (DEC-51). Recorded in the mandate spec §3, §4.3, and the schema ([design questions](09-mandate-rewrite-questions.md) 1) | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-26; the recommended answer to rewrite question 1, confirmed after the spec PR #109 merged) | At a 1,000 USD allocation five positions are 200 USD each, and five admissions stay reviewable by one owner. More instruments mean more approvals, smaller positions, and more research cost | Default 10; platform ceiling 50 (v0.5's `universe.instruments` bound) |
| DEC-118 | Thesis lifetime. Every thesis expires at its horizon (`expires_at = as_of + horizon_s`). The research agent may renew it only through a new `ThesisProposed` with fresh evidence before expiry, which passes the admission checks and the autonomy rules again. A thesis that expires without renewal makes its instrument a removed instrument: exits only, protection stays, and the discretionary exit follows mandate spec §2.2. The horizon is also when DEC-99 scores the thesis, so the position and its score end together ([design questions](09-mandate-rewrite-questions.md) 2) | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-26; the recommended answer to rewrite question 2, confirmed after the spec PR #109 merged) | A position held on an expired thesis is held for no stated reason. Ending the position and the score together means every thesis is scored on the window it claimed | A thesis stands until invalidated (an open-ended hold); automatic renewal while no invalidation condition holds (the thesis never has to be re-argued); exit at once at the horizon (a forced sale on a timer, ignoring the conduct controls) |
| DEC-119 | Bounds on the research agent's weight. Organizations and workspaces may cap it through the policy hierarchy (policy key `research_weight`, a maximum). There is no platform minimum or maximum until the DEC-99 evaluation gives evidence for one. DEC-47 stands: the weight is an envelope field the owner confirms, and there is no calibration ([design questions](09-mandate-rewrite-questions.md) 3) | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-26; the recommended answer to rewrite question 3, confirmed after the spec PR #109 merged) | A platform bound would be the platform choosing how much its own ideas count, with no evidence for the number. Funds that want a cap have the policy hierarchy | No bound at all beyond the owner's confirmation; a platform minimum, maximum, or both |
| DEC-120 | Research-agent cost cap. `behavior.research.cost_cap_usd_per_day` is an envelope field the owner confirms, in dollars per agent per risk day. It is enforced by deterministic code, not by the model: once the day's spend reaches the cap, a proposed thesis is journaled and refused (mandate spec §8.5, check 7). Existing positions are managed normally, their theses stay valid until they expire or are invalidated, and exits, protection, and kill switches are unaffected ([design questions](09-mandate-rewrite-questions.md) 4) | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-26; the recommended answer to rewrite question 4, confirmed after the spec PR #109 merged) | Model cost grows with agents, instruments, and sources, and an owner who cannot bound it cannot bound the bill. Dollars are the unit the owner understands; enforcing the cap outside the model keeps DEC-04's boundary | No cap, metered and billed; a cap in tokens per day (not a unit an owner can reason about) |
| DEC-121 | Switching bring-your-own-strategy. Both directions are mandate versions. **Pinning** (`universe.pinned` false to true, from a version that had an admitting model, together with clearing `behavior.research` and every `admits_instruments`, not raising `max_instruments`, and changing nothing else) is **one risk-reducing change** and applies at once; pinning a version that had no admitting model is risk-increasing, because such a version has an empty working universe and pinning hands the agent instruments it could not trade before; instruments the research agent had admitted that are not in the pinned list become removed instruments (exits only, protection stays, discretionary exit under mandate spec §2.2). **Unpinning** is risk-increasing and needs step-up. Any other combination of those fields follows the per-path rules, which are fail safe ([design questions](09-mandate-rewrite-questions.md) 5) | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-26; the recommended answer to rewrite question 5, confirmed after the spec PR #109 merged) | Pinning removes the platform's discretion, so it cannot need an approval that reducing risk never needs (DEC-05). Unpinning lets the platform admit instruments the owner did not choose, which is the largest single loosening in the mandate. Treating pinning field by field would classify it increasing, because the signal-model row is fail safe | Both directions under the per-path classification of the fields they touch (pinning would then need step-up) |
| DEC-122 | The DEC-99 evaluation window, metric, and threshold, fixed before the evaluation starts. **Window:** the later of three months of forward paper trading and 100 closed theses. **Metric:** mean excess return per closed thesis, net of modeled costs, against each pre-registered baseline (buy-and-hold of the eligible basket, and a broad index ETF) over the same holding window. **Threshold:** a one-sided 95% lower confidence bound above zero against both baselines. The E17-0 spike report gives the spread of outcomes needed to check that 100 theses can show a difference ([design questions](09-mandate-rewrite-questions.md) 6) | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-26; the recommended answer to rewrite question 6, confirmed after the spec PR #109 merged) | A point estimate above zero on a small sample is noise. A lower confidence bound above both baselines is the weakest claim that still means the theses beat the alternatives. Fixing all three in advance stops the result being chosen after the fact | A fixed calendar period alone, or a thesis count alone (either can end with too little evidence); hit rate (says nothing about size); risk-adjusted portfolio return (confounds sizing with thesis quality); a positive point estimate as the threshold |
| DEC-123 | The DEC-100 monitoring thresholds and stagger window. The aggregate-flow monitor alerts the deployment's operator at 1% of an instrument's 20-day average daily dollar volume or 1,000,000 USD per instrument per deployment, whichever is lower. Openings on a new thesis are staggered over 900 s inside the regular session, by a deterministic offset from the workspace id and the thesis id (mandate spec §8.4), within the conduct controls; `stagger_window_s` is a policy minimum. The operator issues the halt and the monitor never halts by itself, so no workspace's state changes another's decisions without an operator's action. Hybrid and on-prem deployments run the same monitor and halt over their own workspaces. Revisit the values before live trading ([design questions](09-mandate-rewrite-questions.md) 7) | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-26; the recommended answer to rewrite question 7, confirmed after the spec PR #109 merged) | 1% of average daily volume is small enough that the platform's aggregate flow is not the instrument's price, and the dollar floor catches thin names where 1% is still large. Fifteen minutes spreads the flow without leaving a thesis unacted on for a session. An operator-issued halt keeps DEC-09 and DEC-10 intact | 0.5% or 5% of average daily volume; no dollar threshold; a 5-minute or 30-minute stagger; letting the monitor halt by itself (one tenant's state would then change another's decisions) |
| DEC-124 | The Robinhood paper stage. The simulated broker configured with Robinhood's rules (order types, sessions, fractional shares, fees) is the required paper stage for Robinhood users; Alpaca paper is optional for users who also hold an Alpaca account. Whether any live Robinhood path is possible still depends on the OD-12 answers on client order IDs ([design questions](09-mandate-rewrite-questions.md) 8) | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-26; the recommended answer to rewrite question 8, confirmed after the spec PR #109 merged) | Robinhood has no paper environment, and E10-4 requires a paper run. Requiring Alpaca paper would make every Robinhood user open a second brokerage account before they could try the product | Alpaca paper as the required stage; both required |
| DEC-125 | `auto` for retail on paper. DEC-98 stands: the retail profile has `auto_allowed: true` and every retail user trades paper until counsel signs off. The retail profile screen and the go-live screen state that live `auto` depends on counsel's answer to compliance question 33 and may come with lower ceilings or be unavailable, so a paper run sets no expectation for live. The wording is compliance text, so the founder accepts it (DEC-79); the requirement to show it is recorded in mandate spec §4.3 ([design questions](09-mandate-rewrite-questions.md) 9) | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-26; the recommended answer to rewrite question 9, confirmed after the spec PR #109 merged) | A paper run under `auto` teaches the user what live will look like. Saying in advance that live may differ costs nothing and prevents a promise the platform cannot keep. Restricting retail to `ask` would need an entry superseding that part of DEC-98 | No extra wording; restrict retail to `ask` until counsel answers |
| DEC-126 | How theses are shown. The approval screen for an admission shows the full thesis in the mandate spec §8.2 and §8.4 shape: instrument, direction, horizon, evidence and corroboration as links to the allowlisted sources (DEC-101), invalidation conditions, and confidence labeled self-reported and uncalibrated; for a revision, the lineage's revision count and what the revision changed (DEC-111). No price targets and no profit estimates. The research agent's scorecard stays off approval screens until counsel answers compliance question 35, because it may count as hypothetical performance. Notification payloads carry opaque IDs and generic text only (`AGENTS.md` rule 6) ([design questions](09-mandate-rewrite-questions.md) 10) | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-26; the recommended answer to rewrite question 10, confirmed after the spec PR #109 merged) | An owner approving a platform-originated idea needs the whole reason for it, with its sources, to judge it. A summary hides exactly what an injected article would have planted. Labeling confidence as self-reported keeps it from reading as a probability of profit | A summary with the full thesis behind "View model output"; either form plus the scorecard (hypothetical performance before counsel has answered) |
| DEC-108 | E4-1 tests-PR shapes for the backtest fill model, where its [task brief](tasks/E4-1-simulated-execution.md) and DEC-106 leave the code open. (1) `mandate-sim` reuses `mandate_accounting::{Side, AssetClass, Liquidity}` instead of creating `mandate-domain`, which DEC-82 foresaw "until a second crate needs them": ES-02's chain already puts accounting below sim, a fill maps one to one onto an `Execution`, and the extraction would edit `mandate-accounting` while E3-3 holds it (#48). It moves to the story that creates `mandate-spec` and `mandate-risk` (M5). (2) The model's arithmetic goes to `mandate-num` (claim #62), whose exact 256-bit arithmetic is crate-private and which ES-04 makes the home of money and quantity operations: `Fraction` (non-negative, at most one, 9 places; `above_one` beyond), `Qty::checked_add`, `Qty::portion` (truncate(fraction × qty, increment)), `Bps::checked_add`, `Bps::sqrt_impact` (DEC-106 item 3's 18-place ceiling root) and `Price::slipped(Bps, Adverse)`. DEC-106 item 2 needs no new rounding mode: rounding the slippage **amount** up once at 9 places moves a buy up and a sell down, and equals ceil(p × (1 + s), 9) and floor(p × (1 − s), 9) exactly, because a price already sits on the 9-place grid. (3) A `SimBar` carries `trade_date` as well as `session` and `session_start`: the cap's reference is the previous bar of the same trading day and session, and a day order's last eligible session is the last of its trade date, neither of which a session start alone decides. (4) `OrderRef` is the order's index in the submitted slice, assigned by `simulate`, because rule 3's priority *is* submission order and the sim cannot check a caller's own identifiers against it. (5) `Eligibility::Resting { from_bar }` takes a bar index, so `resting_since_bar: n` is expressible for any n, and such an order never takes the marketable-on-arrival test. (6) The harness labels each bar's session, session start, trade date, and auction flag from the bar's instant: the New York hour comes from `mandate-time`, and an instant's minute is its UTC minute because every New York offset is a whole number of hours, so 09:30 is told from 09:31 without a second time-zone conversion (ES-02 keeps jiff inside `mandate-time`); the regular session ends at 13:00 ET on a calendar early close; `coverage_start` is the case's first bar; `first_bar_reference_volume` is the median for every bar that opens a session; a backtest order is a day order with `extended_hours` false unless the case says otherwise; and a sell above `initial_position` fails the case, so no case can describe a short sale (DEC-32). (7) The v1 order policy of §5.1 and §5.2 is checked before anything is simulated, each cause with its own error and stable code: `ZeroQuantity`, `QuantityOffIncrement` (`Qty::portion(Fraction::ONE, increment)` is the truncation it compares against), `ExtendedHoursNeedsALimit` (only a limit order may carry `extended_hours`, since the extended sessions take limit orders alone), `StopLimitCrossed`, `OcoLegsCrossed`, `OcoOnAFractionalInstrument` (crypto protection is a stop-limit, DEC-36), `DayOrderOnAContinuousInstrument`, and `RestingBarOutOfRange`. (8) Neither leg of an OCO is ever marketable on arrival: a protective pair's legs are working orders away from the market, and DEC-106 item 8 has the take-profit remainder resting at its limit, which a marketable leg would not do. A marketable sell take-profit would also take the slipped open above its limit, the one path in the model that could beat the limit it was given. (9) The harness interprets neither `time_in_force` nor `extended_hours`, which no backtest case states (DEC-85): every case's order is a day order trading the regular session alone, the most restrictive reading, and a case stating either key fails as unknown | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | Each keeps the fill model pure, keeps its arithmetic in one place, and takes the reading that cannot make a backtest look better than the market allowed. The adverse-rounding identity in (2) is checked by hand and by property (`slippage_moves_a_price_against_the_order_by_a_rounded_up_amount`); (3) to (9) were each shown to matter by a planted bug, (7) to (9) after the first independent review of the tests PR | Create `mandate-domain` now (it edits a crate another claim holds and needs its own claim); duplicate `Side` and `AssetClass` in `mandate-sim` (two spellings of one domain type); add a `Rounding::Floor` mode (a mode nothing else needs, and logic a tests PR cannot put under the mutation gate); derive sessions inside `mandate-sim` (it would need jiff, which ES-02 keeps in `mandate-time`); trust each bar's stated `session` (a mislabelled bar could widen what an order may trade); let a backtest order default to GTC and extended hours (the least restrictive reading) |
| DEC-115 | E5-4 verification command inputs, where journal spec §11 and §12 leave a choice ([task brief](tasks/E5-4-verification-cli.md) "Interpretations"). (1) **No new export format:** §6.2 already defines the segment file and `mandate_journal::export_line` already writes it, so E5-4 adds only the reader, which strips the `{"body":` prefix and the `,"hash":"<64 hex>"}` suffix and takes the body as an exact byte slice, never re-serializing it. (2) **The reader lives in `mandate-cli`,** not beside `export_line`, so the safety-critical crate needs no change; it moves next to it if the cold-store exporter needs it too. (3) **Check 2's columns come from the body,** because an export stores none: a column the body cannot supply is left at a value the body does not hold, so a body missing an envelope field fails `column_mismatch` instead of being accepted. Two vector cases (`column_altered`, `seq_values_swapped`) therefore cannot be expressed by an export, and the tests assert that rather than skipping them. (4) **A line no canonical body can be read from fails check 1** (`non_canonical`) at the `seq` it should have held, including a final line without the line feed §6.2 requires, so every failure the checks report is one of the spec's codes. (5) **The trusted start is an argument** (`--from-seq` with `--trusted-prev-hash`, together or not at all; default the genesis start), never read from the export, since manifests are deferred to the cold-store story. (6) **The anchor file** is JSON of §4's grammar holding what `AnchorComputed` records, `{"leaves":[{"hash","seq","stream_id"}],"root"}`, leaves sorted by `stream_id`; a file that is not one is refused before anything is reported. The format is not yet in the spec (a protected path): a spec-only change follows. (7) **Without `--store` every reference is missing,** so an event naming an artifact fails `artifact_missing`; a `--store` that is not a directory is refused rather than created, so a typo cannot report a verified export. (8) **A segment covers one stream:** once the per-event checks pass, an export whose lines do not all carry one `stream_id` is refused as malformed input, because §11's per-range vocabulary for it (`segment_manifest_mismatch`) belongs to the deferred manifest work. (9) Range checks run only after every per-event check passes, which is what the vectors' `per_event: pass` expects. (10) **An anchor never vouches for an export it cannot cover** (both halves found by the independent review of #108, each a false pass). An export holding no event holds no anchored head, so with `--anchor` it is `anchor_head_mismatch`, never `verified, no events`; and an anchor naming no leaf for the export's stream is refused, because `verify_anchor` rightly skips the head check there and the command would otherwise print `verified` with the anchor path as though it had been checked. (11) **Every input refusal carries a stable code first** (`Refusal::code`: `trusted_start_invalid`, `artifact_store_unusable`, `anchor_invalid`, `anchor_covers_another_stream`, `export_mixes_streams`, `export_stream_id_invalid`), as the artifact errors of check 6 already did, so a script reads the same word an auditor does (ADR-0001 ES-09). | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | An auditor's tool that reports anything other than the spec's first failing check, or that passes an input it did not fully read, is worse than no tool; each choice resolves a gap in the spec in the direction that cannot produce a false pass, which items 10 and 11 record after the review found the two places the first draft resolved it the other way. The export format is the spec's, so nothing new is minted where the spec already decides | A new JSON-lines export format of `StoredEvent` rows with their columns (it would make check 2 expressible but contradict §6.2, which the journal already implements); reading the trusted start from the export (lets a rewritten segment vouch for itself); reporting a malformed line under a new CLI code instead of `non_canonical` (an auditor would have to learn two vocabularies); creating a missing `--store` directory (a typo would read as a verified export); reporting `verified, no events` for an empty export under an anchor, or letting an anchor about other streams stand as a check of this one (the two the review rejected) |
| DEC-127 | E4-2 baseline backtest and metrics report, where no spec section defines the figures ([task brief](tasks/E4-2-backtest-baseline.md)). (1) The loop, the baseline strategy, and the metrics go in a new safety-critical `mandate-backtest` (layer 7, pure, `allowed_external = ["thiserror"]`), not inside `mandate-sim`, because the report is the evidence an owner reads before go-live (DEC-97) and a separate crate leaves E4-1's model unedited; `xtask/layers.toml` and CODEOWNERS are founder-owned, so the entry is the one item this decision cannot take alone. (2) The loop's order at each bar is fixed: due cash movements, the bar's fills, the bar-close mark (spec §8.2), fee charges for days that have ended, the period observation, then the strategy's decision; so a settlement dated before the bar lands before its fills and a fill in a period's last bar is inside that period's equity. Every instant the loop passes on is a bar start, because a bar carries no duration: a decision taken on bar i's close is timed at bar i + 1's start, the earliest instant the input proves is at or after that close, so with zero latency the order is eligible from bar i + 1 and never from the bar that decided it (no look-ahead), and a decision on the last bar is never acted on. (3) One period per trade date, observed at that period's closing bar — the last regular-session bar of the date for an equity, §8.2's official close, and the last bar of the UTC date for crypto, a date covered only outside the regular session being no period — with E0 the equity before the first bar; `periods_per_year` is configuration (252 for equity trade dates, 365 for crypto) and the report states it. (4) A period return and the total return are round((E − E') ÷ E', 12, half_even); an opening equity that is not positive is the error `equity_not_positive`, never a substituted number. (5) Variance is the sample variance, divisor n − 1, as one rounding of (n × Σr² − (Σr)²) ÷ (n(n − 1)) on 256-bit intermediates, absent below two periods; the report shows n and both sums, so a reader recomputes it with the stated divisor. (6) Volatility is the least 12-place value whose square reaches the variance (a ceiling root, so dispersion is never understated), and annualization multiplies the **variance** by the integer period count exactly before one root is taken, never the root by an irrational factor. (7) Sharpe is reported as `sharpe_squared` with `sharpe_sign`, which annualizes by the same exact multiplication, plus a `sharpe` whose 12-place root is rounded towards −∞, so it never reads better than the truth; an absent figure is `Null` and an `absent` field says why, `fewer_than_two_periods` or `zero_variance` (which covers a positive exact variance that rounds to zero at 12 places). (8) No annualized (CAGR) return: a fractional power is not an exact decimal, so the report gives the total return with the period count, the first and last dates, and `periods_per_year` (the DEC-116 item 3 rule: the parts, never a hidden quotient). (9) Maximum drawdown is peak to trough over the period closes including E0 as the first peak, each rung rounded **up** at 12 places, reported with the exact USD amount and the earliest peak and trough periods; intra-bar lows are not used. (10) Turnover is round(min(buy notional, sell notional) ÷ E0, 12, half_even), with both sides, the total, and the fill count reported exactly: the minimum measures what went round, so a position still open is not turnover and a pure buy-and-hold run reads 0 beside its buy notional, where the half-sum would call it half a turn and both sides would call one round trip two; E0 is the denominator because the envelope fixes the owner's starting capital (DEC-97). (11) Fees come from the accounting fold and are never applied to the return a second time, because §8.2's equity already subtracts accrued fees. (12) The baseline strategy is a moving-average crossover on period closes compared without division (F × slow > S × fast; a tie is flat), long only (DEC-32), at most one working order (§5.3 rule 6), sized from `Account::cash_total` so a margin account's unsettled sale proceeds count as §7.2 makes them, entering with a tick-rounded day limit buy in the regular session and exiting with a tick-rounded limit sell of the whole position, which §5.3 rule 2 exempts from the increment because a crypto asset fee leaves the received quantity off it; no adds and no protective legs; the time in force is `Day` on an equity and `Gtc` on a continuous instrument (§5.2, which `simulate` enforces), and a GTC order is never re-submitted. (13) The buy-and-hold benchmark is the same loop, bars, fill model, fees, and starting cash with one GTC limit buy at the first bar's close plus the collar, decided at bar 0's close rather than at a period close, the single exception to item 2's decision rule, because a benchmark that sat out its first period would not be the comparison FR-4.2 asks for (its decision is still timed at the next bar's start, so it cannot fill in the bar that decided it), sized truncate(starting cash ÷ limit, increment), reporting the submitted and filled quantities; the comparison is a full metric block plus the exact difference of the two total returns. (14) The metric arithmetic is added to `mandate-num` under a shared-crate claim, because ES-04 keeps exact arithmetic there: `Ratio` (signed, at most 24 places so an exact sum of squares fits, with every reported rounding at 12; ES-04 already names the type), `Usd::ratio_to`, `Usd::shares_at`, `Ratio::{checked_add, checked_sub, negated, is_negative, times_int, sum, sum_of_squares, mean, sample_variance, squared_quotient, root_floor, root_ceiling}` (the two sums are the ones the report shows, and `sample_variance` takes them with the period count so the figure recomputes from the report), and `Price::on_tick` with §2.1's Reg NMS tick table. (15) The report is self-checking: every derived figure is computed from figures the report itself shows with one stated rounding, and it serializes to canonical JSON (decimals as text, no floats) whose SHA-256 is the run digest, with the bars' and configuration's digests embedded (FR-4.5) and a `report_version` that rises whenever a definition changes. (16) The metrics get no spec text in this story: the definitions live in the brief and here, and a spec section is **proposed** for the pre-M5 spec window, because the presentation of owner-facing performance evidence is the founder's call. (17) Fees are charged at the instants the spec names, not at one of the loop's own, and each instant is a **point in time the bars pass**, not an hour a bar must itself fall in: equity fees for trade date D at the first bar at or after 20:00 ET on D (§6.2), so a 09:30 bar of the next trade date has passed that instant and charges D, and crypto fees for UTC date D at the first bar whose UTC date is past D, which is 00:00 UTC (§6.3); nothing is swept at the end of a run, so an accrual whose instant no bar reaches stays accrued, exactly as the fold would hold it. The point-in-time reading is the coordinator's ruling on the independent review of the implementation PR (#163 review round 1, finding 2): the hour-of-the-bar reading left a regular-session-only week charging nothing, which lets a return read better than it is, while §6.2 fixes an instant; the implementation PR carries the loop change and this wording, and the tests-correction PR recomputes the three hand cases and the ledger oracle that encoded the other reading. (18) One `simulate` call per submitted order over the **whole** bar slice, never a slice trimmed to the order's life and never the loop re-deriving an order's last eligible session: a bar with no earlier bar of its session in the slice given is that session's first covered bar (DEC-106 item 4), so a trimmed slice would cap a mid-session order on the 20-session median instead of the previous bar's volume and stop agreeing with the whole-series model and with RC-10, RC-12, and RC-19, while rule 2's cancellation is read back from the `OrderEnd` that `SimOutcome::end_of` returns. An order works through the end of the bar that ends it, its last fill's bar or an `Expired` bar, so that bar submits no replacement and the next submission comes from a later period close (§5.3 rule 6). One call is enough because the baseline keeps at most one order working; multi-order cap sharing stays E4-1's tested behaviour. (19) The backtest account is a margin account with no reservations and no buying-power check, because the risk gate is not in this loop and every Alpaca account is a margin account (OD-08). (20) A bar's `trade_date` is checked, not trusted: recomputed with the trading calendar for an equity, compared with the UTC date of the bar's start for crypto, which has no trade date (§2.2); either disagreement fails the run, so a mislabelled bar cannot move a period boundary. (21) A fill's identifiers are load-bearing, so they are fixed: orders are numbered from 0 in submission order within a run, and a fill becomes an `Execution` with `client_order_id = Some("o<index>")`, `fill_id = "o<index>-f<ordinal within the order>"`, and `executed_at` the fill bar's start; a repeated fill ID is `duplicate_fill` in the fold and the per-order TAF cap is counted against the client order ID (§6.2, DEC-87), which a `None` would turn into a cap per execution. (22) `submitted_qty` and `filled_qty` are defined for every run, strategy or benchmark: the sum of submitted order quantities and the sum of fill quantities as `simulate` reports them, which is gross, a crypto buy's asset fee leaving the position below `filled_qty` (§6.3) (23) The tests PR's own inputs are corrected in a separate DEC-77 tests-correction PR, which changes fixture inputs and generator domains only: its decimal literals were written as non-canonical text (`100.00`, `104.50`, `1.00`), which §2.1's grammar and `mandate_num`'s parsers reject as `not_canonical`, so 133 of them are rewritten to the same numbers canonically (`100`, `104.5`, `1`) and the generated bars' prices are formatted the same way; and the sample-variance property drew returns up to 10⁶ in magnitude, whose exact sum of squares does not fit the 96-bit significand a 24-place `Ratio` holds (the largest is 79228.162514264337593543950335), so about three series in four would have been item 14's `overflow` rather than the exact sum the property asserts — the generator is bounded to ±10 a period, whose seven-term worst case is 700, and the documented overflow stays covered by `adding_and_subtracting_ratios_is_exact` (24) A **second** tests-correction PR follows the implementation PR's review, and again changes fixture inputs and expected values only. Its four corrections: the TAF-cap fixture, whose 2,000-share median capped the *entry* at 200 of 479 so the exit was one fill and the per-order cap could not be told from the per-execution one — the entry day's second bar now reaches the entry limit and the exit day's second bar the exit limit, so the exit is the whole 479 and splits 200 then 279; the three hand cases that encoded the hour-of-the-bar reading of item 17, which this decision now settles as the instant — an equity trade date's fees are charged at the first bar **at or after 20:00 ET on that date**, so a 09:30 bar of the next trade date charges the previous one — (`an_accrual_the_bars_never_reach_stays_accrued` now stops before its accrual day's 20:00 ET instead of relying on a week of regular bars, `the_total_return_is_net_of_accrued_and_charged_fees` reads 1.6 where charging rounds 0.00479 and 1.580255967 up to the cent, and `a_settlement_posts_before_the_bars_fills` pins period 7's equity as period 6's less exactly 0.009744033, the rounding and nothing else, which proves the bucket transfer moved no equity more tightly than equality did); the golden report the brief asks for, committed as canonical bytes whose SHA-256 is the run digest, so a reader outside the crate can check both; and three cases for what closing the implementation PR's mutation survivors by code shape left untested — a bar later than the charge hour still charging its day, a crypto fee accrued on a run's last UTC date staying accrued (pinned against the same bars plus one, so the case carries no fee figure of its own), and a bar's session alone changing the input digest. The ledger oracle needs no change: `sequencer::charges_due` already read the instant as a point in time, which is why the property that uses it passes on the implementation. (25) The implementation PR exceeds ES-13's 400-line budget (1,159 `src` lines) and is not split into a loop PR and a metrics PR as the brief allows, because `run` is what all but a handful of the 65 frozen tests call, so either half alone would leave a stub behind and the pending gate red; the coordinator's review accepts the exception for that PR rather than stalling the story, and the runner story splits the crate. (26) A **third** tests-correction PR follows the implementation PR's review round 2 (verdict approve) and **adds cases only**: it changes no existing assertion and no `src` file. Two of that round's three minors land in it. A Sharpe case with a non-zero `risk_free_per_period` and one with a rate above the mean, because every hand case and every generator used a zero rate, so item 6's subtraction of the risk-free rate was pinned by nothing and the plant `excess = mean_return` passed the whole suite; on the series 100000 → 102000 → 103020 the returns are exactly 0.02 and 0.01, so a rate of 0.005 makes the squared Sharpe 0.01² ÷ 0.00005 = 2 where ignoring it would report 4.5, and a rate of 0.02 turns the sign negative on a run whose every period gained. And a **standard-time** charging case, because every fixture in the suite sits in daylight time, where a fixed −04:00 offset and the America/New_York rules agree, so item 17's instant could have been written as a fixed offset and passed: the new case runs a buy-and-hold day on 2026-11-16, where 20:00 New York is 01:00 UTC of the next date, and asserts that a 19:30 EST bar — 00:30 UTC, which the fixed offset would read as 20:30 and charge — charges nothing and stays accrued, while a bar at exactly 20:00 EST charges the day. The round's third minor, dropping `NumError::Unimplemented` together with its row in `error_codes_are_stable`, is **not** done and is backlogged: the variant is returned by the fourteen `mandate-num::sizing` stubs E6-2 still owes, so it is live code rather than a leftover, and dropping it would leave those stubs with no typed error to return. | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79); item 1 needs the founder's `xtask/layers.toml` and CODEOWNERS entry, and item 16 is proposed | PRD FR-4.2 names seven figures but no spec section defines them, and ES-23, DEC-89, and DEC-116 item 3 forbid floating point and hidden quotients, so every figure needed an exact definition with a stated rounding direction. Reporting the squared statistics makes annualization an exact integer multiplication and leaves the roots as the only chosen roundings, each moved against the run; a division-free crossover keeps the strategy from adding roundings of its own; and running the benchmark through the same loop means the comparison cannot be flattered by a different fill model or a different fee schedule | Report a standard deviation and Sharpe as decimals with no stated direction (an irrational number silently truncated, and annualization no longer exact); annualize by multiplying the rooted figures by √252 (not an exact decimal); use the population variance (a reader comparing Sharpe ratios assumes the sample divisor); measure drawdown from the starting equity or from intra-bar lows (the first understates a recovery's drawdown, the second needs data the loop does not have); count both sides in turnover (a round trip would read as two); define the benchmark arithmetically from the first and last closes (it would pay no slippage or fees, flattering the benchmark and diverging from the loop's definitions); put the loop in `mandate-sim` (it edits the crate stream A holds and mixes the model with its runner); a fixed-weight periodic rebalance as the baseline (a division per rebalance and a signal coupled to the mark) |
| DEC-129 | `mandate-risk`, the independent risk gate, where trading-domain spec §9 and mandate spec §5.3 leave the code shape open ([task brief](tasks/E6-3-risk-gate.md)). (1) The gate is a new safety-critical `mandate-risk` (layer 4, pure, `allowed_external = ["thiserror"]`), a single pure function over a mandate, a risk state, an account snapshot, a working universe, a market context and one proposed order: no clock, no I/O, no state between calls, and no journal handle, because a limit enforced anywhere an agent can reach is not an independent gate; `xtask/layers.toml` and CODEOWNERS are founder-owned, so those two entries are the one item this decision cannot take alone. (2) §9.1's evaluation order is normative and the first failing check's code is reported; `reference/mandate/ref.py`'s `gate` is the mandate-limit subset of checks 2, 6 and 7 in that same order and the Rust reproduces it exactly, `computed` blocks included, while its `order_decision` is documented in `ref.py` as the composition the fuzz uses for MI-1 and puts checks 1, 5, 3 and 6 before check 2, which changes no verdict and only which code is reported when two checks fail at once, so where the two disagree §9.1 wins, the fuzz oracle compares verdicts with `order_decision` and codes with §9.1, and no reference case exercises the disagreement. (3) An absent working universe is an error, never an allow: `ref.py` indexes `st["working_universe"]` and raises, so `WorkingUniverse` has no `Default` and no guessing constructor and its `Unavailable` variant returns `GateError::WorkingUniverseUnavailable`; an empty universe is a different thing, known and denying every opening (MC-G16); a `GateError` is neither an allow nor a deny but a refusal to decide, and the executor's safe default applies. (4) Purpose is assigned by the gate from side, the agent's position and an `Origin` that carries the originating component and nothing else, because §9.1 types an exit by its originating component and no side can say which: a proposer that lies about its origin can only make its own exit stricter or claim a `risk_exit` its component identity contradicts, and a sell above the position is `would_cross_zero`. (5) Every §9.1 check lives in this one function — eligibility floor, sessions, order constraints, collar, conduct controls, buying power and the day-trade budget beside the mandate limits — because two gates would eventually disagree and one of them would be the one that allowed the order. (6) The §9.2 day-trade ledger is a pure fold in this crate (window = today plus the four prior trading days from the committed calendar, shares held overnight sold first, each same-day open-then-close once, crypto never, fractional counted; `remaining` from the prior-close equity and the flag; `required = 1 + sold earlier today + open same-day positions + working same-day opening orders elsewhere`), since no other crate has a reason to know the rule and the harness's `day_trade_count` reads it. (7) Under `intraday_margin` check 8 denies nothing: §9.2 says a 1× long-only approved order cannot create a deficit and check 7 already enforces 1×, so a broker-reported deficit is an account state (`closing_only`, `exits_only`) set at check 1, not a per-order denial. (8) Check 7 compares `qty × limit_price + round(estimated fees, 2, ceiling) ≤ min(model, broker)` with every term exact and subtracts reservations **once**, because `Account::buying_power(reservations)` already nets them and the pending fee charges out (DEC-34, DEC-104); §9.5's "≤ buying power − existing reservations" is the same figure written before DEC-104 moved the subtraction into the fold, and the gate's account snapshot carries no separate reservations field, so the double subtraction is unrepresentable rather than merely avoided; a cash account's failure is `insufficient_settled_buying_power` and a margin account's `insufficient_buying_power`; crypto uses `min(model, broker non-marginable)` with a fee reservation of 0. (9) The gate derives the session, the auction windows and the close window from `now` and `mandate-time`'s committed NYSE calendar rather than taking a caller's label, because a check the caller can defeat is not independent; a date outside the calendar's range is `ConfigOutOfRange`, never a guessed session. (10) An unclassified ETP behaves exactly as complex and a classification older than `etp_classification_max_age_s` denies ETP openings, which is §3.2 item 6's fail-closed rule; `test_default` carries no such field, so the crate default serves the harness and the config field is proposed. (11) Pacing is an output, not a second call: a discretionary exit's allow carries the sliced quantity and the collar-bounded limit price the executor sends, because §9.6 says such exits are paced and never denied, and the remainder is re-proposed rather than stored, as §9.1 already rules for `defer`; an opening or increasing order is never paced, only allowed as proposed or denied. (12) `hold` is a fourth verdict beside allow, deny and defer, as `ref.py`'s `order_decision` already returns, because MI-1 says an exit may be held only by `paused`, `stopped`, an `Unknown` order or the broker, and calling a hold a denial would make MI-1 false in the code while true in the spec; schema v3's trading-domain vocabulary has only three verdicts (`allow`, `deny`, `defer`), so that harness compares a `Hold` against a case's `deny` carrying the same reason code, which no case exercises today; the `kind: gate` harness needs no mapping, `ref.py` having the same four verdicts. MI-1's own words are "never denied by a mandate limit, conduct control, session rule, or instrument restriction", and its list ends "or the broker": an account the broker has blocked is that broker arm, so check 1 denies **every** purpose with `account_trading_blocked`, a risk exit included (RC-15's `status_not_active`), while `closing_only` still allows every exit (RC-15 step 4), and the MI-1 property is scoped to MI-1's own words rather than to "never denied by anything". (13) The §9.6 daily surveillance report is a pure function that states figures and flags thresholds and makes no judgement, because §9.6 is explicit that the platform does not supervise users' trading; routing it and journaling the acknowledgment belong to the runtime. (14) The gate never journals: rule 5's "journal before acting" is the executor's obligation and a pure function cannot hold a journal handle, so `Decision::checks` carries every check reached, in order, with the rest marked `NotReached` so a reader cannot mistake "we did not look" for "it passed". (15) Every limit comparison is `>` against the limit, so a value exactly at it passes (MC-G02), nothing rounds before a comparison, and a bound that must be rounded to the price increment is rounded against the order. (16) Fee reservations for buying power (the work tracker's E6-6 follow-up) are `round(estimated fees, 2, ceiling)` per order computed through `mandate-accounting`'s fee rules rather than a second copy, 0 for a crypto buy, and an input to check 7 only. (17) A protective order is never held by a mode and a risk or owner exit is held by `paused` unless a kill switch sent it, which is `ref.py`'s `order_decision` exactly, and which is why `Origin` separates `AutomatedKillSwitch` from the other risk origins and `OwnerKillSwitch` from `OwnerClose`: an owner closing one position under `paused` is not a kill switch and is held, so one `Owner` variant could not express the rule. Further: trading spec §7.4's prose is narrower for the protective case ("no new orders except re-placing protection before expiry"), but nothing in the gate's input distinguishes a re-placement from a first placement, `ref.py` draws no such line, and holding protection under `paused` would leave a position unprotected, the opposite of rule 3's safe default; MI-1 is satisfied either way, since it says an exit *may* be held only by those four things, not that each always holds one. (18) `auction_window` covers the 09:28 to 09:30 opening auction and market orders in either window, and the closing ten minutes deny an opening or increasing order as `close_window`: §4.3's closing auction window and §9.6's close window are the same interval and both bar openings, and check 3 runs before check 6, so without this the first code would always win and RC-25 step 2 could not pass; nothing is lost, because an opening order at 09:28 is in pre-market and check 3's session rule already denies it. (19) The `kind: gate` harness runs `ref.py`'s `gate` rather than the whole of §9.1 (DEC-85 style): those cases carry no session, quote or account status, so the harness passes the case's `purpose` through as already assigned and runs only the mandate-limit checks `gate` implements, which is why MC-G15 allows a `discretionary_exit` in an instrument with no position; purpose assignment and check 4's `would_cross_zero` are exercised by the trading-domain cases (RC-03's `gate_rejects_zero_crossing_order`) and by hand tests, and anything a mandate case states that this path does not read stays "not interpreted until <story>". (20) A cancel is a gate decision too, so the crate exposes `evaluate_cancel` beside `evaluate`: §9.6's minimum resting time is a rule about canceling a non-marketable opening order and its exemption for a cancel that precedes a risk-reducing order is a risk judgement, so leaving it to the executor would put a conduct control outside the independent gate, which item 5 rules out for every other control. (21) An agent flatten prices every sell it emits by the session alone, `market_or_ladder` in the regular session and `exit_price_ladder` outside it, which is `ref.py`'s `agent_flatten` exactly and is what separates MC-F01's crypto sell from MC-F02's and MC-F03's. (22) An `Unknown` order in the instrument denies an opening or increasing order and **holds** a reduction, under the one code `unknown_order_in_flight`: §5.3 rule 9 says "no new orders in that instrument until resolved" while MI-1 lists an `Unknown` order among the things that may hold an exit beside `paused` and `stopped`, and both are true at once only if the verdict depends on the purpose; denying the exit would contradict MI-1's own wording and letting it through would risk doubling a position whose true size is unknown, so the fuzz's hold codes are `agent_paused`, `agent_stopped` and `unknown_order_in_flight` while `account_trading_blocked` stays the only denial a reducing purpose may carry. `ref.py` is not the oracle here: `order_decision` models neither rule 9 nor an `Unknown` order, and for a reducing purpose it falls through to `gate`, whose first line allows anything in `REDUCING`, so it answers allow where this item requires hold; for an input with an `Unknown` order in the proposed instrument the fuzz's oracle is rule 9 and MI-1 directly, and item 2's bound on `order_decision` says so. (23) A `removed_instrument` restriction is the working-universe check rather than a separate one, reported at check 2 as `not_in_working_universe`: an instrument becomes `removed_instrument` exactly when it leaves the working universe (mandate §2.3 and §5.9) so one fold produces both, and `removed_instrument` is **not** in the trading-domain reason registry, so reporting it would mint an unregistered code and break ES-09's stability promise; `stale_mark`, the other instrument restriction, is registered and is evaluated at check 5 with mark freshness where §9.1 puts it, and `ref.py`'s `order_decision` returns the restriction's own name only because it models the two as one parameter, which changes no verdict. (24) A real halt and a presumed halt are different denials under different codes: §4.4 gives a halted or paused instrument "no new opening orders" while a stale quote or dropped status feed is "treated as a presumed halt: no market orders", so a halt is `instrument_halted` at check 3 and bars the opening outright while a presumed halt leaves openings alone and refuses only market orders as check 4's `market_order_not_allowed`; one code for both would either bar openings a presumed halt permits or permit market orders a halt does not. (25) **Proposed (founder):** `not_in_universe` and `not_in_working_universe` are two registered codes for one condition — mandate §5.3 and MC-G14/MC-G16 expect the second, trading-domain §3.2 item 1 and RC-16 step 7 the first, and both fixtures are pinned universes, so no implementation satisfies both; the recommendation is one code, `not_in_working_universe`, with §3.2 item 1 dropped (the working-universe check at the head of check 2 covers it) and RC-16 step 7 updated, and until then the Rust emits `not_in_working_universe` and RC-16 stays pending. (26) **Proposed (founder):** §3.2 item 6's "configured age" for ETP classification data has no field in `configs.test_default.gate`, so the rule has no value to compare against; the recommendation is `etp_classification_max_age_s` (proposal: 7 days) in the next spec window. (27) **Proposed (founder):** §5.3 rule 2 (quantity ≥ `min_order_size` and a multiple of the increment) is a check-4 denial with no code in the trading-domain `reason_codes` registry, so the gate would have to mint one and ES-09's "stable reason code" would not be stable; no case exercises it today, and the recommendation is to register `below_min_order_size` and `quantity_off_increment` under order constraints, with the tests PR asserting the verdict and leaving the code unasserted until then; also **Proposed (founder)** on the same path: register `protection_unattributed` (an opening held while a protective leg in its instrument is unattributed, checked before `add_blocked_by_protective_order`), which DEC-160 journals as a hold until then (28) A presumed halt re-prices an exit; it never denies one. §4.4 ends "no market orders; exits use marketable limit orders" and §5.6 lists presumed halts among the conditions where an exit that must be marketable takes the exit price ladder, so the gate allows a risk or owner exit proposed as a market order under a dropped status feed or a stale quote and sets `Pacing::marketable_limit_required`, while a market order to open or increase is denied at check 4 as `market_order_not_allowed`. Denying the exit would read §4.4's first clause without its second and would put an instrument restriction in front of a risk exit, which is the one thing MI-1 names outright; it is numbered after the proposed items rather than beside item 24 because renumbering accepted items invalidates every reference already written against them. (29) **A partial gate fails closed for adding risk** (the coordinator's ruling on #157): while any check, or part of one, is still owed by a later PR or story, an opening or increasing order that every implemented check would allow returns `GateError::Unimplemented("evaluate", <story>)`, naming the story that completes the first owed check, and never an allow; a denial or hold from an implemented check still reports first, and an owed check is listed `NotReached` for every purpose, a reduction's included, since the gate did not look. A reducing purpose passes a check that does not exist yet, as `AGENTS.md` rule 13 requires of most of them anyway, the broker being the backstop. Each implementation PR narrows the owed set, and a test that needs an allowed opening stays pending until the PR that completes the last check it passes through; the public API is unchanged, the variant existing since #136. (30) Two rows of §9.1's purpose table are settled by v1's long-only book (DEC-32): every buy is `open` or `increase`, a protective leg's included, since there is no short for a buy to protect; and every sell above the agent's position is typed `open`, a protective leg's included, since it would open a short if it filled or triggered, so check 4 denies it `would_cross_zero`, which is §9.1's own rule ("a sell above the position is denied"); §5.3's "bracket protective legs are checked against position + entry quantity" sits with rules 4 to 6 and is rule 4's (`sell_exceeds_available`, E6-6). Only a protective sell within the position is `protective`, so no reducing purpose is ever what a zero crossing is denied under, and MI-1 stays true as stated. A zero-crossing sell under `paused` or `stopped` is therefore held at check 1 before check 4 is reached, which is §9.1's order applied as written. (31) **An exit is re-priced under a real halt as well as a presumed one** (the coordinator's ruling on #186): §4.4, §5.6 and item 28 attach the marketable-limit re-pricing to the presumed halt, and §5.1 permits a market exit "with current status data", which a real halt has; the gate nonetheless re-prices a market-order exit in a halted instrument too, setting `Pacing::marketable_limit_required`, because a market order into a halt reopens at an unknown price and a marketable limit bounds it. It is the safe direction of rule 3 and never a denial: the exit is allowed, only its form is constrained, so MI-1 and rule 13 hold. (32) **The gate applies §3.2's platform minimums itself, rather than trusting `GateConfig`** (#193 review): items 4, 5 and 7 give each floor as an organization setting with a platform minimum (1.00 USD for the price floor, 1,000,000 USD for both the 20-day equity and 30-day crypto liquidity floors), and `GateConfig`'s fields are public with nothing in the repo validating them, so a setting below a minimum is representable today. The floor therefore compares against `max(setting, platform minimum)`: a gate that accepts a weakened floor on trust is not the independent gate rule 1 requires. `max` rather than a `ConfigOutOfRange` refusal, because raising the floor to the minimum enforces the spec exactly while a refusal would decline to decide, and the check is one a misconfigured organization should not be able to turn off at all. (33) **A stale or absent ETP classification denies every US-equity opening, not only an ETP's** (#193 review): §3.2 item 6 says "ETP openings are denied", but `EtpClass::Plain` covers a plain ETF and a common stock alike, so once the classification is stale the gate cannot tell which it is holding — the instrument that was `Plain` a year ago may be a leveraged ETP today. Denying only what is already classified complex would fail open on exactly the data the rule exists to distrust, so the wider reading is the one rule 3's safe default picks. The cost is that a reference case must supply a fresh classification date for an ordinary stock, RC-16 and DEC-138 item 10's tracer included. (34) **A crypto opening stays owed at check 2 until the quote currency is an input** (#193 review): §3.2 item 7 admits USD pairs only, and nothing in `InstrumentSnapshot` says what a pair is quoted in — `AssetId` is a UUID, so BTC/USD and BTC/USDT are indistinguishable. Calling check 2 whole for crypto would let a non-USD pair open the moment E6-6 and E6-8 land, so `owed` keeps a crypto opening owed to **E6-10**, which adds the field, and the fail-closed rule refuses it meanwhile (item 29). A crypto exit is untouched, since `first_owed` accrues only for an opening. | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79); item 1 needs the founder's `xtask/layers.toml` and CODEOWNERS entry, and items 25, 26 and 27 are Proposed (founder) | AGENTS.md rules 1, 2, 3 and 13 all reduce to one claim — that no code path lets an agent exceed its mandate and that reducing risk is never denied — and that claim is only true if the enforcement is one pure function the agent cannot reach, with an order of checks fixed in advance and a reason code that names the first failure. Writing the checks down in §9.1's order before any code also surfaced the two places where the mandate spec and the trading-domain spec disagree on the same input, which no implementation could have satisfied silently | Split the checks across the six stories' own modules (the evaluation order would live nowhere and drift); let the caller supply the session and the purpose (a mislabelled session opens in an auction window, and a proposer could claim its own exemptions); treat an absent working universe as empty (a fold that has not caught up would silently deny rather than refuse, hiding a broken input); return only allow and deny (a deferred discretionary exit becomes a denial, contradicting §9.6 and MI-1); fold the day-trade ledger in the executor (the rule and its state would sit in different crates); make the gate journal its own decision (impure, and the executor must journal before acting anyway); pick one of the two conflicting reason codes ourselves (a reference case is founder-owned, ES-22) |
| DEC-130 | E6-2 autonomy classification and the order builder of mandate spec §8, as code ([task brief](tasks/E6-2-autonomy-and-order-builder.md)). (1) Both go in a new safety-critical `mandate-builder` (layer 5 per ES-02's plan, pure, `allowed_external = ["thiserror"]`), together rather than in two crates, because an autonomy rule compares the combined score the builder produces and splitting them would put one crate's output in the other's fixtures; `xtask/layers.toml` and CODEOWNERS are founder-owned, so those two entries are the one item this decision cannot take alone. (2) **The builder proposes, the gate decides, never the reverse:** `mandate-builder` does not depend on `mandate-risk`, and the §6.2 step 2 dry run reaches it as a `GateVerdict` **value**, so no defect in the builder can turn a verdict into an allow or be the reason a limit goes unenforced (`AGENTS.md` rule 1). The `Purpose` on a proposal is a label; the gate assigns purpose from side and position (§6.1, trading spec §9.1) and re-derives it. (3) The §5.5 `trim_to_target` risk exit is **not** the builder's: its quantity, its four guards (`rung_not_confirmed`, `holding`, `regular_session_only`, `below_minimum_order`), and the ladder state they read are `mandate-risk`'s. The runtime asks the risk engine first and calls the builder only when there is no trim to place, so the builder can never suppress a risk exit — a stronger property than sequencing the two inside it. `MC-B17`, `MC-B30`, `MC-B31`, and `MC-B32` therefore belong to stream G. (4) `RC-08` and `RC-18` are the gate's, not the builder's: both supply an explicit instrument, side, quantity, limit price, and purpose and expect a gate verdict — `RC-08` and `RC-18`'s `generic_cash_account` variant a deny with reason code `insufficient_settled_buying_power`, `RC-18`'s main path an `allow` with the reservation's effect on buying power (`449.97`) — which either way is trading spec §9.5 through §9.1's ordered checks, and §12 maps `propose_order` to `IntentProposed` and `GateDecided`; neither combines outputs or sizes anything. The work-tracker M2 row is corrected in the same change. (5) The mandate arrives as narrow views, `BuilderMandate` (§8 fields) and `AutonomyPolicy` (§6 fields), not as the whole document, so the story is testable before `mandate-spec` exists and `mandate-spec` later supplies a conversion without changing a signature; `Session` and `Purpose` live here until stream F creates `mandate-domain`, beside the `AssetClass` move already recorded. (6) Market value is at the **risk mark**, taken as its own input, not at the quote: §8.3 step 3 says "MV (at the risk mark)" and trading spec §8.2 makes it the bid for a long, which is why the cases supply the two as one number; a stale or non-sane mark can then never be silently replaced by a quote. (7) **Exactness.** §5.2 says comparisons are exact and §8.3 states exactly three roundings, so the three `round₁₂` of step 1 are the only ones and every other value is exact. `mandate-num` gains, under a shared-crate claim (ES-04 keeps exact arithmetic there): `SizeFraction` (non-negative, at most one, **12** places: a signal model's `weight`, `max_position_fraction`, the two thresholds, `rebalance_band`, and the ladder size factor, whose 12-place bound is what keeps the sizing chain inside 256 bits and is therefore load-bearing; not `Fraction`, whose 9-place ceiling is part of the fill model's contract), `Unit` (non-negative, at most one, **18** places: a model output's `confidence`, the combined score, `thesis_confidence`, `drawdown`, and the unit-typed condition values — 18 because `MC-B13` already carries a 13-place confidence and a confidence enters only the combine step, where the budget affords it), `Conviction` (signed, in [−1, 1], 18 places, the bound checked rather than assumed; the two combined convictions are `round₁₂` results and use 12 of them), `Signed` (18 places, for the two P&L fractions §5.2 defines at 12 and their unbounded condition values), `Unit::weighted_ratio` and `Conviction::weighted_ratio` (each of step 1's quotients as **one** formula on 256-bit intermediates, with the missing models' weights counted as −1 for the buy conviction), and `UsdExact` (a signed USD amount carried exactly wider than `Usd`, with `checked_add`, `checked_sub`, `min`, `times_size_fraction`, `times_conviction`, `is_positive`, `round`, and a wide `shares_at` beside the existing `Usd::shares_at`) for cap, the target, and Delta: `Usd` holds 28 places on a 96-bit significand and `Ratio` 24, while cap alone reaches 33, a 12-place fraction times an equity that holds 21 from a 9-place quantity at a 12-place mark, so rounding them would be a rounding the spec does not state. The brief states the digit budget per step that keeps the whole chain inside 256 bits and the property test that keeps it true. (8) An input too wide **refuses the order**: a weight, fraction, threshold, conviction, confidence, or condition value beyond the places its type holds is `too_precise`, an intermediate beyond 256 bits is `overflow`, and either way nothing is proposed. Refusing adds no risk, which is what `AGENTS.md` rule 3 and DEC-06 require of an ambiguous input, whereas an approximated size is an order nobody specified. (9) The three combined figures are computed and reported **before** a hold: §8.3 step 1's "no fresh outputs: hold" still reports c = 0, b = −1, and s = 0 (MC-B20), so an outage is visible as an outage on the journal and the approval screen rather than as a blank. (10) §8.2's "ties by journal sequence" is the index in the caller's `outputs` slice, supplied in `seq` order, because the crate cannot verify a sequence number it is handed; `outputs_used` is a `BTreeSet<ModelId>`, so it is sorted as the cases expect and two runs cannot differ by iteration order (ES-21). (11) A pinned triple is compared whole: an output is ignored unless id, version, **and** content hash all match (§8.1, DEC-67), and an ignored output counts as missing, which lowers the buy conviction and never raises it. (12) Condition values are typed **at load**, so V-023's type rules and V-017's four-level depth limit are checked once and every comparison is numeric on two exact decimals, never lexical; a rule naming `unusual_input` is refused at load (V-018), so the field cannot be reached. (13) `by` names what decided: `admission_ceiling` only when the ceiling actually changed the decision (MC-A12, MC-A14) and the matching rule's id otherwise (MC-A13, MC-A15); the ceiling is applied after the rules and only tightens, so an owner's `deny` survives `admission: auto` (MI-17, DEC-05). (14) `skipped` and `deferred` are `Outcome` variants, not `Decision` variants, so a gate verdict can never be mistaken for an autonomy decision in a `match`; the reference-case harness maps the two shapes. (15) A discretionary exit is paced, never denied, and the two pacing rules have different owners. The **session** rule is the gate's: an equity exit outside the regular session is verdict `defer` (trading spec §9.6, §9.1, DEC-48), and `decide` maps `GateVerdict::Defer` to `Outcome::Deferred` with nothing stored. The builder does not derive it — `decide` takes no `Market` and so cannot see the session, which is item 2 holding rather than an omission; `ref.py` composes the two inside its own `builder`, which is why MC-B22 reads as a builder case, and in Rust the harness composes propose → gate → `decide`. The **close-window** rule is the builder's, because it changes the order rather than the verdict: `propose` has `Market`, so an equity discretionary exit with `in_close_window` set carries `OrderShape::MarketableLimit` (DEC-70). `Session::Crypto` is never deferred. (16) No approval is ever requested for an action the gate would deny, and `decide` is the only path to an `Autonomy`, so §6.2 step 2's rule holds by construction rather than by discipline. (17) Limit prices are the quote: a buy at the ask, an exit at the bid, neither collared nor tick-rounded, because the collar (trading spec §9.6) and the tick (§2.1) are the gate's and the executor's and applying them here would price an order twice; a crossed quote is `CrossedQuote`, the one refusal in `BuilderError` that `ref.py` would not produce and that no committed case reaches, taken because sizing off a crossed quote prices an order against a market that does not exist. Two refusals an earlier draft carried are dropped for the same test: an output whose `expires_at` is at or before its `as_of` is simply never fresh, and `Direction` has one variant, `Long`, so an unsupported direction is unrepresentable rather than rejected (the trust ladder's first rung). (18) Protection is not the builder's: `protection` is an envelope field the executor uses to place the bracket or crypto stop-limit (trading spec §5.4), and the builder proposes the entry only. (19) **No defaults** (DEC-85): every field the cases treat as optional (`size_factor`, `gross_usd`, `bought_today_usd`, `has_prior_fill`, `goal_spent_usd`, `cost_basis`) is required, and `has_prior_fill` in particular is never inferred from the position, because a re-entry after a round trip is not a first trade (MC-B25) and inferring it would mislabel exactly that case. (20) **Journal events: none.** The crate is a pure function; the runtime appends `ModelOutputRecorded` (with the `ignored` reason for an output this crate rejected), `IntentProposed`, `GateDecided`, and the approval events, and §6.2 step 2's skipped and deferred journaling is the runtime's. (21) Two orderings the spec leaves implicit are fixed: in step 2 the `accumulate` check comes **before** the flat-position check, as `ref.py` has it, so a flat `accumulate` agent under c ≤ −`exit_threshold` holds `discretionary_exits_disabled` rather than `no_position`, which would read as though a position would have been sold; and the step-5 guard is `n ≤ 0` **or** the value below the minimum order, not the minimum alone, so a zero-quantity buy is impossible even where `min_order_usd` and `rebalance_band` are both zero (22) **Five shapes the tests PR corrected, and one deviation it records** ([#175](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/175), on the coordinator's ruling in review round 1). `Sizes::current_mv` is a `UsdExact` and not a `Usd`: a 9-place quantity at a 12-place mark carries 21 fractional places and the digit budget's 10¹² ceiling puts 12 digits in front of them, which is 33 significant digits where `Usd` holds 28 on a 96-bit significand. `Market::increment` is a `Qty` and not a `ShareIncrement`, the same correction item 27 of DEC-128 made for a goal's increment, because `MC-B28`'s expected `0.010025` is a 6-place truncation that nine places would leave at `0.010025062`. `combine` takes the instrument being sized and **refuses** an output naming another (`output_instrument_mismatch`): §8.3 sizes one instrument at a time, nothing else in the chain notices the mix-up, and ignoring a mismatched output would make a caller's mistake indistinguishable from a model that did not answer (DEC-85). §6.2 step 3 is reached **before** the order path re-checks the rules, and `UntradableSession` is reached on the buy path only, both because `AGENTS.md` rule 13 forbids a malformed rule set or an untradable session being the reason a risk-reducing action is refused; an overnight equity exit is still proposed here and deferred by the gate (item 15). And `UsdExact::shares_at` clamps a negative amount to `Qty::ZERO` as `truncated_quotient` does, so the two cannot disagree about which hold a zero band reaches. **The deviation:** `ConditionTooDeep`, `ConditionTypeMismatch` and `ReservedField` are variants of `BuilderError` although DEC-128 item 18 puts those three errors in `mandate-spec`, which does not carry them yet while `Condition::matches` returns `SpecError`. They are the **order path's** re-check of V-017, V-018 and V-023, which `classify` does because it is handed a §6 block and cannot assume validation ran, and they carry the codes item 18 names, so the implementation PR maps `SpecError` onto them and no caller sees a different code; stream F is asked to acknowledge on claim #124. | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79); item 1 needs the founder's `xtask/layers.toml` and CODEOWNERS entry, and the brief's Decisions needed carry four proposals to the founder: bounding the schema's decimal scales (12 places on the sizing and limit fractions, 18 on a model output's `conviction` and `confidence`, since `MC-B13` already carries 13, and a condition value to the places its field's type holds) so an exact chain always fits; treating the Rust crate, not `ref.py`, as the oracle beyond the 298 committed cases, because `ref.py` runs at `getcontext().prec = 60` and so is not exact either; amending ES-04's 96-bit engineering note to name one wide reported type for the figures §5.2 forbids rounding; and updating E6-2's backlog acceptance line for the cases spec v0.6 added | The whole point of the crate is that the owner's rules, not agent logic, decide what happens without them, so every choice takes the reading that cannot widen autonomy or enlarge an order: the ceiling only tightens, a missing model is bearish for buys and neutral for exits, an over-precise input refuses rather than approximates, and the gate's verdict arrives as data the builder cannot influence. Keeping the trim and the gate out of the crate makes "the builder can never suppress a risk exit" a structural property instead of a tested one.
