Typing condition values at load turns V-017, V-018, and V-023 into load-time errors instead of evaluation-time surprises | Two crates, one for autonomy and one for sizing (one crate's output becomes the other's fixtures); let the builder call the gate (the builder would then be on the path a limit depends on); sequence the `trim_to_target` exit inside the builder, as `reference/mandate/ref.py` composes it for the cases (a builder defect could then swallow a risk exit); take the whole mandate document as input (the story would wait on stream F and the crate's inputs would stop being reviewable); round cap, the target, and Delta to 12 places so `Usd` suffices (a rounding the spec does not state, in a sizing path, and a silent divergence from the reference implementation); widen `Fraction` to 12 places instead of adding `SizeFraction` (a volume cap would then carry 12 places, changing E4-1's contract); hold every unit value in one type (at 18 places the target reaches 69 and the share division passes 256 bits, and at 12 it rejects `MC-B13`'s 13-place confidence); approximate an over-precise input instead of refusing it (an order nobody specified); infer `has_prior_fill` from the position (MC-B25 is exactly the case it would get wrong) |
| DEC-131 | E6-1 and E6-5, the agent runtime skeleton and kill switches, where the specs state the rules and no document says how code realises them ([task brief](tasks/E6-1-agent-runtime-and-kill-switches.md)). (1) A new safety-critical `mandate-runtime`, `pure = true`, layer 6, `allowed_external = ["thiserror"]`, with a CODEOWNERS line: layer 6 is the lowest layer that can see `mandate-journal` (2) and the M5 crates `mandate-spec` (3), `mandate-risk` (4), and `mandate-builder` (5), and it is `mandate-executor`'s own layer, which is why the executor arrives behind a trait rather than as a dependency; ADR-0001 ES-02's Phase 1 list names no runtime crate because it assumed the runtime was the executor's process, and this decision separates the two writers of the two streams (journal spec section 2). `xtask/layers.toml` and `CODEOWNERS` are founder-owned, so this item is the one the decision cannot take alone. (2) Two entry points, and recovery is an input: `fold` replays a journaled event and produces no effects, and `handle` is the only producer of effects (ES-06), taking the injected pure ports; recovery is `Input::Started`, whose only permitted draft is the startup hold's `AgentModeChanged` and which never re-journals an intent, so a replay cannot re-send and recovery cannot re-journal. `handle` acts on folded state and never folds what `Input::Journal` carries: the tailer folds first and then hands the event over, which is what lets replay and live input share one fold with no double application; and the shell folds each committed append back, which is how a single writer learns its own head and clears an unresolved batch. (3) Streams F, G, H, and J arrive behind injected ports (the mandate view, the gate, the order plan, the agent flatten of family F, the research outputs), so the four M5 briefs run in parallel and none of them edits this core; the runtime also takes its own `Deployment` (agent, connection, and workspace ids) rather than inferring them from the first event folded, because a kill switch addressed to a sibling must reach nothing and a fresh state that inferred its identity would accept any stream. (4) The runtime's gate call is a dry run that can only narrow: the binding gate runs on the account stream, whose owner is the executor (`GateDecided` is an account-stream event; AGENTS.md rules 1 and 12), so a dry-run deny skips a proposal and a dry-run allow authorises nothing. (5) The journal is the channel and a notification is only a hint: every core input is an event tailed from a stream in `seq` order, a risk-clock tick, or a command, so no transport payload reaches the core, a lost notification costs latency rather than correctness, and the founder's DEC-17 answer changes only the shell. (6) Event identity is derived, not random: the injected `IdGen` derives the `event_id` from the stream id, the `writer_epoch`, the expected head, and the draft's ordinal, so a retry after `Unavailable` or `Ambiguous` re-derives the same id and the append answers `AlreadyCommitted` (journal spec section 5.1); the epoch is in the derivation so a fenced writer's retry cannot collide with the new writer's id and turn a `Fenced` into an `IdempotencyConflict`; a ULID's time component carries no meaning, so a derived ULID conforms; and the derivation puts one obligation on the shell, that a batch whose outcome is in doubt is retried with the same drafts and no new input is handled at an unresolved head, because a recomputed batch at the same head would collide with its own derived ids and answer `IdempotencyConflict` for ever. (7) Effects are ordered and write-before-acting is structural: every intent effect follows the draft that records it in the same list, except for `Input::Started`, whose handoffs re-send intents whose `IntentProposed` committed in an earlier run, so for that input alone the rule reads "preceded by the draft that records it, or by a draft the fold saw committed before the call"; asserting the unqualified form over every input would make correct recovery fail the property, and the shell runs the list in order and stops at the first append that is neither `Committed` nor `AlreadyCommitted`, discarding the rest, which the next start re-derives from the fold. (8) A tick that changes nothing emits nothing: durations are sums of whole seconds over the intervals between inputs, each credited by the state at the interval's start (mandate spec section 5.2), so inserted ticks never change a journaled draft (MI-13), and the risk-clock second on the input is the only time the core knows. (9) Mode is copied, never derived from limits: `AgentModeApplied` originates on the account stream and the runtime copies it as `AgentModeChanged` with `causation_id`, its own restrictions being the owner's commands and `recovering`, and the effective mode is the strictest of the two, so the runtime is never less strict than the account stream and journals a change only when the mode changes (MI-6). (10) The kill switch's order is fixed and its scope exact: the final mode first (`stopped` for an owner switch or stop, `paused` for an automated flatten), then `KillSwitchActivated`, then every pending approval cancelled, then timers disarmed, which includes the deadline of every approval it cancels, then exactly one flatten handed to the sink; a deployment outside the scope emits nothing; an owner `Stop` is not a kill switch and hands no flatten, because trading domain spec section 5.5 gives the flatten to the switch and stopping an agent leaves its positions where they are; the runtime computes no plan, cancels nothing, sells nothing, and has no path to cancel-all or close-position (trading domain spec section 5.5, AGENTS.md rule 13). (11) Cancelling pending approvals is part of the kill switch, not a tidy-up, because an approval that outlives it is an order after the stop; a later response is dropped with a journaled refusal. (12) `stopped` is terminal: the core keeps folding so the stream stays readable and proposes nothing ever again, a restart resumes as stopped, and only a new deployment (a new agent stream) starts fresh. (13) Recovery holds `paused`: the hold is taken at every `Started` unless the fold proves the broker's truth is known, which needs both a clean reconciliation at or after the last observed submission and nothing still outstanding, an outstanding intent being exactly the case where the runtime cannot know what the broker did with it; the runtime carries its own `awaiting_reconciliation` until the account stream shows a `ReconciliationRun` at or after its last observed submission, which is the reconciliation trading domain spec section 11 runs at startup and the reconciliation restriction mandate spec section 5.9 names; on a mismatch section 11's rule takes over and only owner acknowledgment with step-up lifts the pause, never the runtime; `Input::Started` journals the hold's `AgentModeChanged` as its first effect, so the hold exists in the journal before anything is re-handed. (14) Approvals live in the core and delivery does not: the core creates requests, binds quantity, limit price, and mandate version, applies responses, and applies the always-`skip` timeout (mandate spec section 6.4), because the timeout is what makes an unattended loop safe; channels, quiet hours, step-up, and two-approver routing are M7's, and a response under a changed version is skipped rather than re-priced. (15) A risk-increasing version applies at the next evaluation with no `Unknown` orders; a reducing or neutral one applies at once, cancels pending approvals, and re-proposes under the new version, and latched limits are never cleared (mandate spec section 2.2). (16) An uninterpreted input fails loudly (DEC-85): an event type, payload field, or command the core does not interpret returns `NotInterpreted` naming the owning story, never a silent no-op. (17) Read positions are derived, not stored: the folded position of every followed stream is part of the folded state and is re-derived by replay, so no durable state lives outside the journal and snapshots remain a later optimisation that changes no result. (18) Fencing at startup, and a `Fenced` append is fatal: the process increments `writer_epoch` before it appends anything and exits rather than retrying when a newer epoch owns the stream. (19) Notification payloads carry opaque ids and a message key only, never an instrument, a quantity, a price, or thesis content (AGENTS.md rule 6), and a test walks every payload the core can build. (20) Determinism is asserted, not assumed: `BTreeMap` and `BTreeSet` only, no floats, no clock, no randomness, exact `mandate-num` arithmetic, `fold_version` 1 with a committed golden journal, and a property that two runs of the same inputs give equal effect lists (ES-21). (21) Every kill-switch initiator has a final mode, because a kill switch that errors instead of stopping would be this crate's worst defect: `Owner` and `PlatformOperator` give `stopped` and `RiskLimit` gives `paused` (trading domain spec section 5.5, mandate spec section 5.5, DEC-100); `Broker` is not an initiator, because a broker-driven restriction is account state (trading domain spec section 7.3) arriving as a copied `AgentModeApplied`; a risk-limit flatten's restriction lifts only through the account stream and never by the runtime, each limit differently (`drawdown_flatten` by owner acknowledgment once flat per section 5.8, `daily_loss` automatically once a new risk day has started with `daily_breach_min_s` passed and the owner's acknowledgment of a `flatten_and_pause` per section 5.4, and `lifetime_floor` only when a version raising `max_loss_from_allocation` applies with equity strictly above the new floor, never by acknowledgment, per section 5.7), and `stopped` lifts for no one. (22) `Input::Started` re-hands only what the folded mode permits, per mode rather than as one list: every outstanding intent in `normal`; exits, protective intents, and an unfinished flatten in `exits_only`; protection re-placement and an unfinished flatten in `paused`; and in `stopped` an unfinished flatten alone, which survives because the kill switch is always available and `stopped` is the mode a kill switch sets. The startup hold is excluded from the re-hand gate and from it alone, because `awaiting_reconciliation` is a reason not to decide rather than a reason to drop an exit already journaled, which trading domain spec section 5.5 lets only `paused`, `stopped`, an `Unknown` order, or the broker hold; the re-hand gate is therefore the strictest of the copied account mode and the lifecycle state, while the hold governs new proposals. The throwaway implementation written for the planted-bug run is what found this: with the hold inside the gate, a restart could never re-hand an exit. `Started` also re-arms every timer from the deadlines the fold carries, so an opening intent still outstanding at a restart under `exits_only` or stricter is dropped rather than re-sent, whatever already reached the executor being covered by the flatten plan's cancel list and the executor's binding gate; an intent has two states rather than one, because conflating them loses one of them: it is `Handoff::Pending` from the commit of its `IntentProposed` until `IntentReceived` reaches the account stream, which is what decides whether a restart hands it again, and it is a live order in either handoff state until a terminal outcome (a `GateDecided` denial, an `OrderAbandoned`, or a terminal order state), which is what decides whether it belongs in a flatten's `working_orders`. With one set, either a restart re-hands an intent the executor already holds or an order it took drops out of the cancel hint. A flatten handoff is identified by its `KillSwitchActivated` draft, since a flatten is never proposed and journal spec section 9 fixes an intent id only for `IntentProposed`; a skipped decision emits the story's one owner alert, carrying the decision's id and a message key; and `Command::OwnerExit` is accepted by the type but answers `NotInterpreted` naming E7-2, whose exit ladder it needs. (23) Entering `exits_only` or stricter cancels every pending approval, whatever put the runtime there (a copied `AgentModeApplied`, an owner pause, or a kill switch), as mandate spec section 5.9 requires; the agent stream has a single writer, so the runtime journals `ApprovalCanceled`, and anything still wanted is re-proposed at the next evaluation. (24) An owner-initiated stop journals `OwnerExitRequested` before the handoff, carrying the scope or instrument, the bid and bid size shown, whether the owner confirmed them, the floor price, the opaque user, and the step-up evidence, because without it the executor cannot price an owner exit outside the regular session. (25) Readings the implementation forced, each the conservative one, recorded here because the DEC-77 tests are frozen and the suite bounds what the core may claim. (a) An approved ASK is **recorded, not acted on** in this story: `PendingApproval` binds the instrument, the mandate version, the deadline, and whether the action adds risk, and carries no order body, so nothing in this core can rebuild the order a grant would place; `ApprovalResponded` records the response, a response for an approval the fold no longer knows is recorded as refused, and anything still wanted is re-proposed at the next evaluation, which is mandate spec section 2.2's own pattern. Acting on a grant needs the bound content the M7 escalation story carries. (b) **Any** `MandateVersionApplied` cancels every pending approval, not only a risk-reducing one: section 2.2 requires it of a reducing version and section 6.4 skips a response bound to a superseded version, so cancelling in both directions is the stricter of the two readings and can only skip an action. (c) The approval window is one crate constant of 300 s until `MandateView` carries the mandate's `autonomy.approval.timeout_s` (stream F): above DEC-61's `approval_timeout_s >= 120` placeholder, inside the schema's 30 s to 86400 s, and, because `on_timeout` is always `skip`, a window that is too short only skips an action. (d) An account-stream terminal outcome (`GateDecided` denial, `OrderAbandoned`, a terminal order state) folds as interpreted and inert rather than removing the intent from `outstanding`: keeping it leaves the order in a flatten's cancel list, which is the safe direction, and item 22's two-state rule is unaffected because `IntentReceived` still moves the handoff to `Taken`. The startup hold is therefore **not** gated on `outstanding` being empty: taking the hold wants nothing outstanding, but lifting it wants only a clean `ReconciliationRun` at or after the last observed submission, which is what item 13 and trading-domain spec section 11 actually say. One predicate for both would hold an agent `paused` for ever after its first order and stop even a discretionary exit (round-1 review finding 4). (e) A tick at a risk-clock second the runtime has already evaluated is inert, and a tick that arrives out of order is read at the second the state already holds rather than refused, which makes MI-13's "waking twice for one second journals nothing" structural and never moves a deadline backwards. (f) A batch whose append is unresolved is retried with the **same drafts and the same handoffs**: re-deriving the ids is what makes the append answer `AlreadyCommitted`, and re-emitting the handoffs is what stops a batch that resolves on its retry from losing the intent its draft authorised. Emitting the drafts alone would have deferred every such handoff to some later restart, and for a kill switch's flatten that is the one handoff `AGENTS.md` rule 13 says must always get through (round-1 review findings 1 and 5). Write-before-acting is preserved: each re-emitted handoff still follows its own re-emitted draft in the list. (g) `Input::Started` hands an unfinished flatten again, rebuilt through the `FlattenPlanner` port from the initiator the `KillSwitchActivated` carries and the confirmation the `OwnerExitRequested` before it recorded, under the `KillSwitchActivated`'s own id. The sink is at-least-once by design, so handing one twice costs nothing while handing none at all is a switch that never reached the executor. Only approval deadlines are armed as timers in this story, since an evaluation timer and a protection-renewal timer have no deadline in folded state yet. (h) The deployment's **lifecycle** (an owner pause, resume, or stop) is folded as a `Mode` carried on the `AgentModeChanged` the command writes, because the agent-stream catalogue records it nowhere else, and that draft is written when the lifecycle moves **even if the effective mode does not**, because a stricter restriction is already in force; a command that moves neither journals nothing, so MI-6 still holds for every mode a reader can observe. Without that draft the lifecycle would live only in memory and a pause taken while the account stream or the startup hold already paused the agent would be lost the moment that other restriction lifted, which is less strict than the owner asked for. The `lifecycle` field is an addition to `AgentModeChanged`'s payload; journal spec section 9 lists that event's *key* fields rather than closing its schema, and the runtime is the agent stream's single writer, so every `AgentModeChanged` a fold can meet carries it. An `AgentStopped` on the workspace control stream, whose owner the runtime is not, is where a richer record belongs. (i) A copied `AgentModeApplied` is **agent-scoped**: mandate spec section 5.10 gives it an agent field and the account stream carries every agent on the connection, so one naming a sibling is ignored, and a sibling's `normal` can never lift this agent's restriction. For the same reason the fold follows this deployment's own agent stream and not every `agent:{workspace}:*`, or a sibling's `IntentProposed` would join this runtime's outstanding set and a sibling's `AgentModeChanged` would overwrite this one's lifecycle (round-1 review findings 2 and 3). A copied `AgentModeApplied` can also retire a kill switch whose final mode is not terminal, because a `RiskLimit` flatten's restriction lifts through the account stream and item 12 makes `stopped` lift for no one (finding 6) — but the rule is keyed on **event identity**, never on what folded first, and three rounds of review were needed to get it there. A switch is **confirmed** only by the copy whose `causation_id` is that switch's own `AgentModeChanged`, which journal spec section 2 makes the executor write; it is **lifted** only by a later copy to a looser mode whose `causation_id` names an `OwnerAcknowledged` the fold has seen on the account stream, which is brief item 21's "by owner acknowledgment". Four things on that stream must therefore not lift a switch, and each was a real defect: the switch's own `paused` returning as the executor's copy, which would retire it the instant it was pulled and leave a restart with no flatten to hand (round-2 finding 1); a `normal` written before the breach, since the streams have no global order (round-2 finding 2); a pause and clearance pair that both predate the breach, which an "at or above my mode" test confirms and then lifts (round-3 finding 2); and, worst, **any** rule that reads arrival order, because a replay folds the agent stream ahead of the account stream (journal spec section 8), so one switch's echo would confirm a later switch and one switch's acknowledgment lift it — the live run and its own replay then disagree, which is the ES-21 invariant this crate exists to hold (round-3 finding 1). The coordinator's ruling also asked the lifting copy's `seq` to exceed the confirming one; that comparison is omitted because a stream folds gaplessly in `seq` order, so it can never be false where it would be read, and an unreachable branch is one the mutation gate cannot cover. `Switch` still records the confirming `seq`. The ruling's alternative, a switch confirmed by any copy at or above its mode, is what rounds 2 and 3 disproved; an earlier version of this item called that reading "strictly more conservative", which was wrong and is withdrawn. (j) A pending approval that would add risk holds back a risk-adding proposal and nothing else: holding a discretionary or protective exit behind an opening ASK is a hold trading-domain spec section 5.5 gives only `paused`, `stopped`, an `Unknown` order, or the broker (finding 7). One approval cannot be both cancelled and timed out by a single step (finding 9). The residue of finding 9 is unfixed and stated rather than hidden: an approval response that arrives in the same step as the tightening that cancels its approval is recorded as `recorded` rather than `refused`, because the response is read against the state before the batch is applied. Nothing is handed either way, so it is a record-accuracy defect and not a safety one; it is on the backlog. (k) The twelve cases the implementation PR added under the reviewers' rulings are the DEC-77 exception that ruling created: DEC-77 freezes the existing tests and does not forbid adding one that catches a real defect. Eleven fail on the head they were written against and pass on its fix; the twelfth, `an_owner_pause_taken_under_the_startup_hold_outlives_it`, passes on both, because the pre-fix code journaled every lifecycle command unconditionally and so was already right on that case — it guards item (h)'s new guard against being widened into the defect the guard could otherwise introduce, and it is the case that catches it. They are `a_kill_switch_flatten_survives_an_unresolved_append_and_a_restart`, `a_retried_append_hands_the_exit_its_draft_authorises`, `another_agents_mode_change_does_not_lift_this_agents_restriction`, `a_sibling_agents_stream_is_not_one_this_runtime_follows`, `a_clean_reconciliation_lifts_the_startup_hold_with_an_intent_still_outstanding`, `an_account_mode_change_retires_a_risk_limit_switch_but_never_a_stop`, and `an_exit_is_not_held_behind_a_pending_opening_approval`, and `an_owner_pause_taken_under_the_startup_hold_outlives_it`, and, for the round-2 findings, `an_echo_of_its_own_pause_does_not_retire_the_switch` and `a_stale_account_normal_does_not_lift_an_unconfirmed_switch`, and, for the round-3 findings, `a_second_switch_is_not_confirmed_by_the_first_ones_echo` and `a_pre_breach_pause_and_clearance_pair_does_not_lift_a_switch`, beside the round-3 sanctioned `a_taken_and_working_order_is_in_the_flatten_requests_working_orders`. No DEC-77 **frozen** test changed: no test that #134 merged, and no fixture, oracle, or golden file, has been touched. Three of this PR's *own* added cases were later amended, twice because a subsequent review round showed the sequence they folded was one the executor cannot produce (an uncaused `AgentModeApplied`, where journal spec section 2 always writes the echo first) and once to reach code a mutant survived in. An earlier version of this item said no existing test body changed at all, which was inaccurate once those amendments landed; the round-3 review caught it. | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79); item 25 likewise, with (a) and (g) closing in M7 and M6 respectively; item 1 `Proposed (founder)` because `xtask/layers.toml` and `CODEOWNERS` are founder-owned. DEC-17, which this decision rests on, was accepted by the founder on 2026-09-26 (#133) exactly as recommended. Four readings are `Proposed (founder)` as spec questions, recorded under Decisions needed in the PR rather than fixed by editing a spec, with the conservative reading taken meanwhile: that mandate spec section 5.9's restriction list stays the executor's risk restrictions while an owner pause is the HLD deployment lifecycle and the startup hold is section 5.9's reconciliation restriction held from startup; that the executor cancels working opening orders while the runtime cancels its own pending approvals, since a second writer to the agent stream would contradict journal spec section 2; that a platform-operator flatten takes `stopped` with the automated pricing schedule, both the safer direction, since trading domain spec section 5.5 prices only owner and mandate-limit flattens; and that an intent the runtime abandons is recorded by its own draft plus the preceding mode change, the agent-stream catalogue having no event for it | The specs fix the rules; these fix the shape the four other M5 streams plug into, and fixing it before they are written is what lets them run in parallel. Splitting `fold` from `handle` is the whole of crash safety; deriving event ids makes a retry idempotent without a round trip; keeping the transport out of the core makes the DEC-17 answer cheap; copying the mode rather than deriving it keeps the account stream's owner the only author of a risk-driven mode | One crate with the executor (rejected: the broker's client and the agent's loop under one lint header); `handle` used for replay too (rejected: a replayed input re-emits effects the run already ran); random ULIDs with a re-query on `Ambiguous` (rejected: a round trip on the crash path, and a window in which a second `IntentProposed` is possible); the transport as a core input, so a NATS or Postgres choice would reach the state machine (rejected: DEC-17 is still open); waiting for streams F, G, and H before writing the runtime (rejected: it serialises M5's largest block for no design benefit); resuming `normal` after a replay (rejected: HLD's lifecycle pauses on anything unexplained) |
| DEC-128 | Stream F, the mandate document as code, where the mandate spec v0.6 and its 298 reference cases leave the Rust shape open ([task brief](tasks/M5-F-mandate-spec.md)). (1) **Two crates, both safety-critical.** `mandate-spec` (layer 3) holds the document, validation, the policy hierarchy, the risk state, risk days, goals, the condition language, and change classification; `mandate-domain` (layer 1) holds the vocabulary streams G, H, and J share (`AssetClass`, `AssetId`, `Environment`, `Side`, `Purpose`, `AutonomyDecision`, `AgentMode`, `Session`). Both are `pure = true`, `safety_critical = true`, `allowed_external = ["thiserror"]`. The rules sit below the gate because the gate enforces them (AGENTS.md rule 1), and the vocabulary sits below both so no stream depends on the mandate rules to name an asset class. `mandate-spec` does **not** depend on `mandate-journal` or `mandate-accounting`, although layer 3 would allow it: the risk state is a transition function over values and the account sub-ledger fold stays in `mandate-accounting`. `mandate-domain` also owns `WorkingUniverse` and `AssetId`, the schema's UUID form, because three streams read them (item 21). `mandate-accounting` re-exports `AssetClass` from `mandate-domain` (one line, identical variants, no methods), which closes half of the known-issues row; `mandate-accounting::InstrumentId` (a looser broker-id type whose constructor returns `AccountingError`) and `mandate-marketdata::AssetClass` (whose `as_str` is the vendor path spelling `us-equity`, baked into dataset paths) are **not** migrated here, because both change a public API and belong in their own change. `xtask/layers.toml` and CODEOWNERS are founder-owned, so the four entries are the one item this decision cannot take alone. (2) **Pending markers name stories, not streams:** `cargo xtask markers` accepts only `#[ignore = "pending E<n>-<n>"]`, so tests carry E10-1 (families S, V, P), E10-3 (the version vector and family C), E6-4 (families R, T, L), or E17-1 (the envelope and strategy field split, V-034 to V-039 and `platform_proposed` provenance). A reference case needs no marker: a case absent from `status.toml` is pending by construction. (3) **The document keeps its decimals as text, checked against the field's own grammar.** Every decimal field is a `SchemaDec`, a `mandate-spec` newtype holding the raw text and the `$def` grammar it satisfied, checked as text before anything wraps it. The check is against the whole `$def`, not the pattern alone: `decimal` carries `not: {const: "-0"}` beside its pattern, a value the pattern itself matches and `DecStr` would normalise to `0`. It is **not** a bare `mandate_canon::DecStr`: that type normalises rather than rejects and implements the wider journal grammar of journal spec §4.6, so `DecStr::parse("0.020")` returns `Ok("0.02")` and would accept MC-S04, and it also takes `007.50`, `1e3`, and `.5`, allows 29 integer digits where the schema allows 28, and knows nothing of the narrower `fraction`, `open_fraction`, `unit_positive`, and `positive_decimal` grammars. Text is still the storage, because the schema's grammars allow up to 28 integer and 28 fractional digits, which `Price` (9 places), `Usd` (28 places but a 96-bit significand), and `Ratio` (24 places) cannot hold; that is what keeps the Rust parse in agreement with `jsonschema` on every MC-S case and on fuzzed mandates (ES-22) and the canonical bytes, and the version hash, identical to the document the owner confirmed. Checking the whole `$def` makes every grammar canonical, so `DecStr::parse` on a `SchemaDec` is the identity and `canonical_bytes` round-trips, which a property test asserts and a planted bug attacks; `decimal` is the one signed grammar and is referenced only by `behavior.signal_models[].params[].value`, so it is also the only one whose ordering reverses between two negatives. V-012, V-013, and V-014 need only ordering, so `SchemaDec` implements `Ord` by decimal value and `mandate-canon` is not touched. (4) **Out of arithmetic range is a typed error, never a rounded number:** a rule that must compute with a field converts it, and a value the target type cannot hold exactly returns `SpecError::OutOfRange { path }` (code `out_of_range`), not a V-code, because the mandate is schema-valid and no specification defines such a rule. (5) **Comparisons are exact and never go through a rounded product:** the five §5.2 comparisons and their 1.25x hard forms are compared as exact products on 256-bit intermediates in `mandate-num` (equivalent to cross-multiplication for exact decimals), returning a boolean, so a scale limit can never decide whether a limit fires. (6) **Reported ratios round half-even at 12 places; the allocation scaling rounds up at 12.** §5.1's H', E0', C', and L' are `ceil(X x (E + delta) / E, 12)` with one rounding, the conservative direction MI-2 needs; the reference computes the same value through a 60-significant-digit intermediate and a differential property test asserts the agreement rather than assuming it. (7) **The worst-case figures round half-even at 28 places:** §4.2 states no scale and an exact product of two 28-place decimals needs 56, so one rounding at the widest scale `Usd` carries reproduces every reference case; the owner-facing *display* rounding is left to the founder (item 14). (8) **`ValidatedMandate` is the only way in:** streams G, H, and J take `&ValidatedMandate`, which only `ValidatedMandate::new` constructs and only when the schema parse, every V-rule, and the policy hierarchy pass, making AGENTS.md rule 1 structural rather than a convention. (9) **The policy check returns the §4.3 runtime overlay with the violations,** so "stricter" has one definition tested once: `PolicyOverlay::auto_allowed` is what stream H calls before returning `auto` and `PolicyOverlay::effective` is what stream G calls for a limit's ceiling. (10) **Missing is not violating,** except `two_approver_above_usd` and `stop_distance_max`, where §4.3 makes an unset mandate value itself the violation because "no limit" is looser than any limit. (11) **Change classification is the join over changed paths,** with the DEC-121 pinning switch as the one rule that reads the whole change at once; arrays are compared whole when changed paths are computed, so a ladder change reports `/risk/drawdown_ladder` (MC-C01 fixes this). (12) **`ChangeClass::Invalid` is a classification, not a violation:** a changed `environment` or `connection_id` is `Invalid` from `classify` (§9.2) and V-031 from `validate`, and neither surface depends on the other. (13) **Warnings and violations are disjoint;** acknowledging a warning is the confirmation flow's job. (14) **The risk state's inputs carry their own time and session,** a step at or before the previous step is `clock_went_backwards`, durations are integer seconds, and equity staleness and the `scale_lift_after_s` timer count regular-session seconds for equities and all seconds for crypto; nothing reads a clock (ES-21). (15) **One journal event per thing that changed, in the fixture's exact shape:** `AgentModeApplied` only on a change (MI-6), `InstrumentRestrictionChanged` once per restriction that changed, a latch reason only when it is not plain confirmation, `hard_breach_pending` among the trigger reasons and `hard_breach_cleared` among the lift reasons (a hard-breach lift carries that reason *instead of* an `action`, MC-R19 step 3), two rungs both journalling `hard_breach_pending` under a single `hard_breach` restriction (MC-R19 step 2), and `GoalCompleted` carrying either `{on_complete}` for the `goal_complete` input (MC-R16, MC-R17) or `{reason, then}` for a confirmed `profit_stop` (MC-R22), so every member is optional. (16) **A rejected input still produces an outcome** carrying the rejection and a journaled `MandateVersionApplied { result: rejected }`, so a caller cannot lose a refusal by matching on `Ok`. (17) **The tests land in three PRs against `main`** for the ES-13 size rule: PR 1 creates both crates with the whole public API as stubs, adds the layers and CODEOWNERS entries, wires the `mandate` reference-case suite with every family dispatched, and carries the S, V, and P tests; PR 2 adds R, T, and L; PR 3 adds C. Neither of the later two adds a stub, and the coordinator's go comment may re-slice it. (18) **The condition language is in `mandate-spec` and nowhere else; its evaluation order is not here:** §6.3's tree, the V-017 depth limit, V-018, the V-023 type rules, `Condition::matches` over a `Facts` trait, and the `ConditionTooDeep`, `ConditionTypeMismatch`, and `ReservedField` errors are `mandate-spec`'s, while §6.2's order, the built-in AUTO purposes, the admission ceiling, and every `Facts` implementation are stream H's, which is why the A-family cases are H's. Stream H's merged brief defines its own copy of the tree and those errors; per its own item 5 and Dependencies section it imports these and deletes the copy in its implementation PR, so its tests PR does not block on F (item 21). (19) **Family F (agent flatten, MC-F01 to MC-F04) is stream G's,** not this stream's: spec §11 assigns family F to agent flatten and family L to goals (the stream-F work-graph row swaps the two names), stream G's merged brief #127 owns those four cases under E6-3, with `agent_flatten` in its public API and the harness interpretation, and the plan reads no mandate field at all. This stream's harness dispatches `agent_flatten` to "not interpreted until E6-3" and one coordinator comment moves it back. (20) **`mandate-num` gets one set of exact operations:** E4-2's tests PR (#118) landed `Ratio` (signed, 24 places) and `Usd::ratio_to` on `main`, so this stream reuses both rather than adding a second ratio type and adds only the exact comparison predicates, `Usd::times_ratio`, `Usd::scaled_by` (one rounding), and `Ratio::{times, complement}`; stream G's claim also names `Usd x Fraction`, so the coordinator sequences which stream lands each operation. (21) **Shared types have one home, named here, because three streams read them.** The independent review of the brief found the same type in two briefs three times, so each is settled: `WorkingUniverse` lives in `mandate-domain` in stream G's richer shape (`Known { instruments, pinned }` or `Unavailable`, so "universe not yet read" is distinguishable from "universe empty") over `AssetId`, and G's copy, in its `#[doc(hidden)]` `spec_types.rs` over `InstrumentId`, is deleted by G's first implementation PR after F's tests PR merges, which G's merged brief already plans; the id type for anything from a mandate, a thesis, or a reference case is `mandate_domain::AssetId`, the schema's UUID form, while `mandate_accounting::InstrumentId` stays the wider broker-facing id (`AssetId` to `InstrumentId` total, the reverse fallible and needed only at a connector); the §6.3 condition tree, `Condition::matches`, and its three errors are `mandate-spec`'s alone, taken up by stream H in its implementation PR (item 18); the risk snapshot's field is `day_start_equity`, the name every `risk_state` case uses, not `day_open_equity`, and the snapshot also carries `latched`, `active_rungs`, and `inherited_loss`, which stream G's gate and oracle 1 both read; and the V-code enum is `Violation`, leaving `Rule` for `autonomy.rules[]`, which stream H reads by that name. F's tests PR lands all of them first so the other streams have something to import. (22) **Live code in a tests PR, as the coordinator's amendment to DEC-83** (ruling on the tests PR #140): "stubs only" admits an exception when every other test needs the code to construct a value, the code is a total function or a grammar check with no rule logic, and the author runs the mutants gate **by hand** and reports zero missed — because the configured gate skips a crate while it carries pending tests, so such code would otherwise reach `main` ungated. `SchemaDec` and `mandate-domain` qualify, as does `ModelId::parse` (the schema's `^(fast|llm|quant)\.[a-z0-9_]{1,48}$`, added in the tests PR for T and L and accepted in its review round 2): V-007 compares the document's models against a registry the caller supplies, and a registry keyed by a type with no public constructor cannot be supplied at all, which is what the `mandate` harness hit when it came to read the fixture's own `validation_context_defaults`. 13 mutants, 12 caught, 1 unviable, 0 missed, and it also closes the five `as_str` and `model_type` mutants #140 had to disclose as accessors no test could reach. Review round 3 of #140 added a fourth admitted case: **a one-expression spec formula**, pinned by live tests with zero missed mutants — `hard_wait_s` (min(`breach_confirm_s`, 10), §5.6), `class_allowed` (V-039), `platform_defaultable` (the §7 table), `ValidationReport::is_valid`, and `Confirmation::is_pending` are each the whole of the rule they state, so a stub returning the wrong constant would be a rule written wrongly rather than a rule not yet written. The same ruling accepts the `mandate-accounting` change (the local `AssetClass` replaced by `pub use mandate_domain::AssetClass`, identical variants and spelling, no accounting test touched) and the `mandate-refcases` addition (a new `src/mandate.rs` and one `read_fixture` arm, no existing test touched), and sanctions the split of item 17 (four PRs as of item 28), with the harness's `risk_state`, `goal`, `change`, and `risk_day` arms as harness rather than crate API, filled by the PR that carries each family's tests. (23) **Three names differ from the brief and the sibling briefs, and the code's win** (same ruling). `mandate_domain::MarketSession`, not `Session`, and it keeps an `Overnight` variant that §6.3's condition form has no name for, because a bar and a risk input can be overnight even though no order may trade there (DEC-30); stream H's tests PR uses `MarketSession` and states the mapping of its four condition-form values. `goal::status`, not `goal_status`, since the module already says `goal`. `RiskState::open` takes a `&dyn SessionClock` the brief did not show, because §5.2 and §5.5 count an equity's staleness and scale-lift timers in regular-session seconds and this crate holds no calendar — supplying the clock keeps ES-21's "no clock reads" true without hiding a calendar dependency inside a pure crate. (24) **Equality on a `SchemaDec` is by value, not by `(text, grammar)`** (review round 1, a real defect found in live code): the grammar is a witness of which field's `$def` admitted the text, not part of what the number is, so deriving equality over the pair while ordering by value alone broke `Ord`'s contract — `0.5` as a `fraction` and `0.5` as an `open_fraction` compared `Equal` and yet were not equal, enough for a `BTreeSet` to hold both and `max` to pick either, and a policy value compared against a mandate field is exactly that pair. `PartialEq` and `Eq` are hand-written so that equality is the order's own verdict, and `Hash` is **not implemented at all**: ES-21 bans `HashMap` and `HashSet`, so a hash of a document value has no legitimate caller, and a hand-written one only added a mutant no test could catch. `ParseError::OffGrammar` also gained the `Pointer` every other variant carries, with `SchemaDec::parse` returning a path-less `GrammarMismatch` that the document parser wraps, since a value does not know which field it is being read for. (25) **MC-S15 is `wrong_type`, not `off_grammar`** (review round 2 raised the choice and asked that the doc say which). `protection.stop_distance` is `oneOf [open_fraction, null]`, so a JSON null satisfies the property's own schema and is refused only by the `then` clause of `if enabled: true`, whose `$ref: open_fraction` is `type: string`. A null is not text off a grammar; it is the wrong type, which is what `jsonschema` reports for this case, and ES-22 requires the Rust parse to agree with it on every MC-S case. The same null with `enabled: false` parses, so the conditional is what rejects it and the test asserts both halves. (26) **When an accumulate goal's target and its spend run out together, the reason is `target_qty`.** §3.1's "done when" column lists the two quantity conditions before the spend one, `GoalCompleted` carries exactly one reason, and no reference case has both true — so the order has to be settled somewhere rather than left to whichever branch an implementation tests first. (27) **A quantity increment is a decimal, not the whole-or-fractional grid.** `GoalInputs::qty_increment` was typed `mandate_num::ShareIncrement` in the tests PR #140; the reference cases carry the increment as a decimal (`1`, `0.0001`, `0.000001`), and `ShareIncrement` has only `Whole` and `Fractional`, the latter meaning nine places. MC-L02's whole point is a 0.00005 remainder below a 0.0001 increment, which nine places would call tradable, so the case could not have passed. The field is a `Qty`, and a zero increment is `SpecError::InvalidInput` (code `invalid_input`) rather than a guess: nothing is ever below a zero increment, so treating it as "not done" would buy forever, and treating it as done would stop a goal that is not done. This corrects a signature the previous PR merged; no implementation depended on it. (28) **The tests land in four PRs, not three, re-slicing item 17.** PR 2 carries families T (the risk day) and L (goals) with their two harness arms; PR 3 carries family R (the §5 risk-state fold); PR 4 carries family C. T and L came to roughly 660 lines of tests before the harness arms, and R is the largest and most safety-critical piece in the crate — 24 cases over ten input kinds and fifteen per-step expectations, with the breach-confirmation and allocation-scaling oracles — so pairing it with goals and risk days would put the fold's review in the same sitting as two unrelated families, against ES-13. Item 17's constraint holds unchanged: no later PR adds a stub. Reversible, and the coordinator's go comment may re-slice it again. (29) **Two shapes §5.10 states that the merged API could not express, added on the coordinator's ruling in the review of the family-R tests PR** (#156), because DEC-128 item 17 puts the whole public API in the tests PRs and no later PR adds to it. (a) `RiskEvent::RiskLimitTriggered`'s `action` was typed `LimitAction`, which is §5.4's two-valued `daily_loss_action` and has no `scale_sizes`, while §5.10 journals **a rung's own** action: 14 of the 24 `risk_state` cases state `{RiskLimitTriggered, drawdown_ladder[0], scale_sizes}`, and a correct implementation failed all 14 on that member alone and on nothing else. Both members are `LadderAction` now, which already carries the three spellings the fixture uses and is already pinned live; `LimitAction` stays two-valued so `daily_loss_action` cannot hold `scale_sizes`, and the total `From<LimitAction> for LadderAction` is what lets a daily-loss trigger journal its action beside a rung's. The alternative the review tried, a third variant on `LimitAction`, passes the same cases but makes a document value the schema rejects representable, so it loses the trust ladder's first rung for nothing. (b) `ApplyResult::Applied` carried only `allocation_change`, and MC-R21 step 5 states the `max_loss_from_allocation` a floor-loosening version applied (§5.10's payload lists it beside the classification); it is a second optional member now. Neither shape had an implementation depending on it. This is the third time a type from the first tests PR met its own reference cases for the first time in a later one (items 27 and 28 are the others), which is the argument for running a throwaway implementation against the fixture in the tests PR rather than waiting for the implementation PR. (30) **Slice P's readings of §4.3** (implementation [#263](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/263); the coordinator's ruling there, refining items 9 and 10). (a) **Nearest, once:** a level's key is compared with its ancestors from the nearest outward, and only the first that states the key and is broken is reported. (b) **`Absent` at a level states nothing**, as a child and as an ancestor; `Absent` in a mandate violates only for `two_approver_above_usd` and `stop_distance_max`; `stagger_window_s` and `independent_approval_required` are not mandate fields. (c) **Derived mandate values:** `auto_allowed` is the default or any rule being `auto` (admission alone is `admission_auto_allowed`); `research_agent_allowed` is any admitting model; `research_weight` is the admitting model's weight; `exits_only_at_max` is the `at` of the first rung whose action is not `scale_sizes`, by action; `max_output_age_s` is the largest over every model; `signal_model_types` is every model's prefix; `environments` is `[environment]`; the research keys are absent without a research envelope. (d) **A value of the wrong type for its key is `invalid_input`**, refused, never read as a pass. (e) **The overlay is the levels' tightest stated value**, never the mandate's: maximum min, minimum max, permission `false` wins, requirement `true` wins, set intersection; `effective` returns the mandate's value with no stated ceiling and the ceiling for an absent mandate value; `auto_allowed` is true when no level states it. (f) **The three platform levels are `LevelName::Platform`.** (g) **An empty chain cannot skip policy:** `ValidatedMandate::new` always puts `platform_base()` first and takes only the levels below it, so `&[]` still checks the base; `check` keeps the chain as given for the MC-P harness, whose chains carry their own base. (h) **A chain holds only ancestors:** a level named `Mandate` is `invalid_input`, since it would bind at save time and vanish from the overlay at runtime. (i) **`narrow` cannot narrow an exit:** it takes an `AddingPurpose` (`Open` or `Increase`), whose `TryFrom<Purpose>` refuses the four exit purposes, so a `risk_exit`, `protective`, `discretionary_exit`, or `owner_exit` AUTO never reaches it (AGENTS.md rules 2 and 13); `ask` and `deny` are never raised. | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79). Item 3 was corrected before merge after the independent review showed the original text false: `mandate_canon::DecStr` normalises rather than rejects, so it is not the schema grammar. Item 24's sentence on `Hash` was corrected in review round 2, which found it describing a hand-written implementation the code deliberately does not have. Four things stay with the founder, listed under the brief's Decisions needed: the two `xtask/layers.toml` entries and the two CODEOWNERS lines the tests PR adds (item 1), the schema's unbounded decimal scale, the display rounding of the confirmation screen's dollar figures (item 7), and the sequencing of the `mandate-num` additions across three claims | The spec is the contract and this stream writes no spec, so every choice here is about how code realises a rule the spec already states. Three forced the shape of everything else. The schema's decimal grammars are wider than any exact fixed-width type in the workspace, so the document had to keep its decimals as text to stay in agreement with `jsonschema` (ES-22) and to keep the version hash byte-identical; once it does, "comparisons are exact" (§5.2) can be honoured literally by cross-multiplying rather than by rounding a product, and no scale limit can decide whether a risk limit fires. Putting the rules below the gate and handing the gate a `ValidatedMandate` moves AGENTS.md rule 1 from the reviewed rung of the trust ladder to the unrepresentable one. And splitting the risk state's inputs from the account fold keeps one crate the owner of each number, which is what the reference implementation does and what made the fixture figures reproducible by hand before any type was written | Put the rules in `mandate-risk` beside the gate (the gate would then define the limits it enforces); put the shared vocabulary in `mandate-spec` (every later crate would depend on the mandate rules to name an asset class); depend on `mandate-accounting` and fold the sub-ledger inside the risk state (two crates would own the same positions and cost basis); convert every decimal to `Usd`, `Ratio`, or `Price` at parse time (a schema-valid mandate would then fail to parse, breaking ES-22, and the canonical bytes could no longer be reproduced); round products before comparing (a rung could fire or fail to fire because a product rounded); report an out-of-range decimal as a new V-code (a code no specification defines would appear in an owner's report); round the allocation scaling half-even (a withdrawal could lower drawdown, breaking MI-2); migrate `InstrumentId` and marketdata's `AssetClass` in the same change (two public APIs and the dataset path code, in a PR already at the ES-13 limit); one large tests PR (over the ES-13 limit for a safety-critical crate, and unreviewable in one sitting) |
| DEC-132 | Stream J, the E17 thin slice as code, where mandate spec §8 states the research-agent contract and no document says how code realises it ([task brief](tasks/M5-J-research-thin-slice.md)). (1) **One new safety-critical crate, `mandate-research`** (layer 5, `pure = true`, `allowed_external = ["thiserror"]`, with a CODEOWNERS line): not a module of `mandate-spec`, because the document crate must stay below the gate at layer 4, and admission is runtime state over the document, the policy overlay, and the folded universe. (2) **The crate never calls a model:** a thesis arrives as a typed value, with no HTTP, prompt, artifact store, or `ModelInvocationRecorded`; `python/research_spike` shows the shape the LLM produces and E17-2's shell owns that boundary. (3) **Admission does not call the autonomy classifier:** `admit` returns `FirstOrderFacts` (`new_instrument`, `thesis_confidence`, and the effective `autonomy.admission`) and the caller composes stream H's `classify`, because §8.5 says admission does not itself authorize a trade and stream H's brief has stream J supplying those two flags; `new_instrument` is true on a renewal too (MC-N14). (4) **Every §8.5 check is a total predicate,** so `checks` evaluates all seventeen and `admit` takes the first failure; `Check { number, reason, failed }` keeps the spec's ordinal in the value and makes the ordering testable. (5) **Checks 1 to 3 are refusals that also report `ignored`** (§8.2's ignored outputs), through one `RefusalReason::is_ignored_output` predicate. (6) **`asset_class` and the leveraged-ETP flag are instrument reference data, not thesis fields:** `Thesis` carries neither, `InstrumentFacts` carries both, so a thesis cannot talk its way past check 10 or 11 (MI-16), which the fixture's single dictionary would otherwise allow. (7) **Corroboration is platform-derived, not agent-asserted** (DEC-101: it is *recorded* in `ThesisProposed`), so a model cannot satisfy check 15 by writing `independent_source` into its own output; earning the fact is E17-7's data-plane work. (8) **The stagger offset uses `mandate_canon::Digest::of_parts`** for §8.4's `SHA-256(workspace_id ‖ 0x00 ‖ thesis_id)` and reduces it big-endian modulo the window as a `u64` byte fold (`acc × 256 + 255 < 2^40`, every step checked), so no `sha2` and no big-integer dependency is added and ES-04's decimal rule is untouched; a window of 0 gives 0. (9) **The fold is the only place a lineage retires,** exactly on a journaled `lineage_retired` refusal of a lineage not already retired (§8.6 item 4, MC-N27). (10) **A retired lineage keeps its holder entry:** the map records which lineage held what, not what the universe holds now, and a lineage loses the entry when another lineage's thesis for that instrument is admitted (MC-N28), so nothing can remove twice. (11) **A renewal journals no `UniverseChanged`** and differs from a first admission in exactly check 17, which a property asserts. (12) **An ungrouped instrument is its own group, said explicitly** (`InstrumentGroup::{Named, Ungrouped}`), so a group id spelling an asset id cannot claim it. (13) **Invalidation is evaluated by the caller;** the crate reads the verdict and keeps the text for the journal and the approval screen (DEC-126). (14) **A lowered `max_instruments` refuses and never removes:** the ceiling enters check 17 alone and an over-ceiling universe drains by expiry (MI-19). (15) **Shared types have one home:** `UniverseChange`, the universe change reason, `InstrumentRestriction`, `AutonomyDecision`, `AssetId`, `AssetClass`, and `WorkingUniverse` come from `mandate-domain` or `mandate-spec` (DEC-128 item 21's mechanism), never a second copy; the item as first written said F's `RemovalReason` could not carry `thesis_admitted`, which reading F's shipped code in #140 disproved — it carries all seven, so only the name differs. (16) **No score is ever carried forward, by construction:** no type accepts a predecessor's score, so MI-18's second half is unrepresentable and `FoldStep::score_carried_forward` returns false. (17) **The crate returns events and appends nothing:** the streams, canonical payloads, and the `causation_id` tying `UniverseChanged` to its thesis entry are the executor's and `mandate-journal`'s. (18) **An unavailable working universe is an error, never an empty one** (`universe_unavailable`), as stream G's gate also treats it. (19) **Pending markers name stories** (`pending E17-2`, `E17-3`, `E17-7`, `E17-9`), per DEC-110. (20) **Check 5 (`universe_pinned`) is unreachable for a validated mandate** (V-036 with V-037 make check 4 fire first, which is what MC-N08 expects) and stays as the structural statement of MI-20, with a property asserting the invariant whichever check fires. (21) **Landing the lineage fold is not the revision loop DEC-111 item 5 gates:** the crate never proposes a revision, and the cap and its retirement (a removal, so exits-only and risk-reducing) are what make an ungated loop impossible, so they come first. (22) **Checks 4, 6, 7, 16, and 17 read the overlay's effective value,** not the mandate's alone (§4.3, and the keys stream F's brief names for this stream), so each compares against the stricter of the two: a policy with `research_agent_allowed: false` fails check 4 beside V-036's own condition, and a lowered ceiling binds at checks 7, 16, and 17. Both are additions to what the reference models and both only ever refuse more, so no committed case moves. Check 5 is not among them: it reads the envelope field `universe.pinned`, not `WorkingUniverse::Known { pinned }`, and it is MI-20 rather than a policy key. (23) **Three rules stay live rather than stubbed** — `RefusalReason`'s ordinals, codes and `is_ignored_output`, `PolicyOverlay`'s six stricter-of rules and its two profile constructors, and `group_of` — because each states a rule exactly once and a stub cannot hold a rule; `tests/rules.rs` pins every branch unignored, together with every accessor and code string, and `cargo mutants -p mandate-research`, run by hand, reports `79 mutants tested: 4 missed, 55 caught, 20 unviable` — the four being the four stub bodies, so every line of live code is at 0. Three equivalent mutants were removed rather than approved: `effective_cost_cap` uses `min`, neither profile constructor uses `..Self::default()`, and `LineageState::empty` gave way to `default()`. (24) **The swap PR asks one DEC-77 exception,** scoped to constructor and builder lines: stream F's `AssetId::parse`, `GroupId::new -> Self`, `SchemaDec::parse(text, grammar)`, `cost_cap_usd_per_day` as a `SchemaDec`, `ValidatedMandate::new(...)` and a private-field `PolicyOverlay` all differ from the narrow views, so deleting `spec_types.rs` also edits `tests/common/mod.rs` and the construction lines of three test files, changing no assertion. | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79); the brief's Decisions needed holds **seven founder items** — the `xtask/layers.toml` and CODEOWNERS entries with the layer-6 alternative, §8.4's `corroboration` wording, check 5's unreachability under V-036 with V-037, the two §8.5 gaps the independent review found (a zero-source thesis whose corroboration kind is unchecked against its sources, and a revision whose predecessor is never checked for having failed or for being in the lineage), and items 6 and 21 raised as judgements — and **three coordinator items**: one shared universe change reason with stream F, the family-N harness that needs two crates, and which stream closes E17-3 | Mandate spec §8 fixes the rules and the 28 family-N reference cases fix the results, but not the crate, the call graph, or which facts the agent may assert about itself. The two interpretations that change behaviour rather than shape, items 6 and 7, both close the same hole: a model output that describes its own instrument or its own corroboration would decide checks 10, 11, and 15 for itself, which MI-16 forbids and DEC-101 exists to prevent. Everything else follows the reference's own structure, which the 28 cases pin exactly | A module of `mandate-spec` (rejected: the document crate must stay below the gate, and admission is runtime state); layer 6 with a dependency on `mandate-builder` so `admit` returns the first order's decision (rejected: it inverts the data flow stream H's brief states and makes admission depend on the order builder); trusting the thesis's own `asset_class`, `leveraged_etp`, and `corroboration` as the reference's single dictionary implies (rejected by items 6 and 7); adding `sha2` for the stagger digest (rejected: `mandate-canon` already hashes); computing the eligibility floor here (rejected: it needs market data and stream G owns it, so a second copy could only be laxer); landing E17-9's revision loop with the fold (rejected: DEC-111 item 5 gates the loop on E17-8) |
| DEC-133 | E7-2, E7-3 and E7-4, the idempotent executor and the broker connector abstraction, where the specs state the rules and no document says how code realises them ([task brief](tasks/M6-K-executor-and-connector.md)). (1) Two new safety-critical crates at layer 6, as ADR-0001 ES-02 already plans them: `mandate-executor` (`pure = true`, `allowed_external = ["thiserror"]`) and `mandate-alpaca` (`pure = false`, `allowed_external = ["reqwest", "rustls", "secrecy", "serde", "serde_json", "thiserror", "tokio"]`), each with a CODEOWNERS line, so the executor's decisions are reviewable without reading an HTTP client and the client cannot see folded state; `mandate-executor` declares no `IntentSink` of its own and names no `mandate-runtime` type, because the two are the same layer and neither can implement the other's trait, so an intent enters only as `Input::Intent` and the adapter implementing stream I's trait lives in the shell, at layer 8 in `mandate-shell` since DEC-138 amended this item (review round 1, finding 4), while `mandate-domain`, `mandate-spec` and `mandate-risk` are ordinary dependencies the implementation PR adds once those crates exist; `xtask/layers.toml` and `CODEOWNERS` are founder-owned, so this item is the one the decision cannot take alone. (2) Two entry points and recovery as an input, the same shape DEC-131 fixed for the runtime: `fold` replays an account-stream event and produces no effects, `handle` is the only producer of effects (ES-06), and `Input::Started` opens the startup reconciliation and resolves an unacknowledged submission by query, never by resubmission. (3) One writer, one stream, and the executor appends everything on the account stream, in three groups: the copied facts (`AgentModeApplied`, `TradingDayStarted`, `ClockAdvanced`, `OwnerAcknowledged`, each with a `causation_id`, plus `RiskDayStarted` derived from the copied `ClockAdvanced` crossing midnight America/New_York); the risk-state records (`MandateVersionApplied`, `RiskLimitTriggered`, `RiskLimitLifted`, `HighWaterMarkReset`, `PositionReleased`, `InstrumentRestrictionChanged`, `GoalCompleted`), whose arithmetic is stream F's fold called as a library while the append is the executor's; and `UniverseChanged`, whose admission decision is stream J's and whose append is the executor's — every risk input carrying a non-decreasing `risk_clock`, and anything not yet interpreted returning `NotInterpreted` with its owning story named (review round 1, finding 7). (4) The binding gate is a direct dependency on `mandate-risk`, not an injected port, because a gate a caller can substitute is not independent of agent logic (AGENTS.md rule 1; journal spec section 2); stream I's injected `GateDryRun` can only narrow (DEC-131 item 4) while this one decides, and until stream G's crate exists the call site is a crate-private function returning `Unimplemented`. (5) The client order id is derived from the intent id alone, never from a counter, a fresh ULID, or the attempt number, because trading domain spec section 5.7 requires a resubmission after a confirmed absence to carry the same id; the attempt number is journaled for the audit trail and changes nothing the broker sees. (6) There are only three derivations and one validating parser (`for_intent`, `for_replacement` from the `OrderStateChanged` that recorded a broker-initiated replacement, and `for_protection` from the `ProtectionChanged` that records a protective order submitted on its own) and no constructor taking a free string, so an id reused across restarts is unrepresentable rather than forbidden. (7) Write-before-acting is structural: every submitting `Effect::Broker` follows the `OrderSubmitted` draft that names it in the same effect list, the shell submits only on `Committed` or `AlreadyCommitted`, and the effect list is the only way a request can be made (journal spec section 5.2, AGENTS.md rule 5, DEC-07). (8) Deduplication is a fold lookup: an intent whose id the fold already carries produces an empty effect list and an acknowledged handoff, so `IntentSink`'s at-least-once re-hands cost nothing and no in-memory set can be lost to a restart. (9) An unacknowledged submission is resolved by a query on the client order id, and absence is confirmed only after `unknown_absent_lookups` consecutive absences spanning `unknown_absent_window_s`, then the gate re-runs; one absent answer never resubmits, because a lagging read replica would otherwise be enough to double an order. (10) A timeout is never a rejection: an unknown outcome is `Unknown`, its reservation is held, its quantity counts as filled for exposure and concentration, and no new order is submitted in that instrument until it resolves (section 5.3 rule 9). (11) `Abandoned` is exactly the spec's two cases, a gate denial or an intent older than `max_intent_age`, and the age check guards every `Intent` to `Submitting` transition rather than only a resubmission, since trading domain spec section 5.7's diagram states it unqualified and an intent re-handed after a long outage has no submission behind it (review round 1, finding 2); nothing else abandons an intent and an abandoned intent is never re-sent. (12) Reconciliation's step order is part of the algorithm, orders then fills then positions then cash then fees then `ReconciliationRun`, because a position difference a missing fill explains is not a mismatch and pausing on it would pause a healthy agent at every restart. (13) Adoption is scoped to the order set, the only row section 11's on-mismatch column adopts: an order-state difference resolves to the broker through `OrderStateChanged` plus a `CompensatingEvent` naming what changed and which event ids were corrected, and a missing fill is ingested, while a position, cash or fee difference is never adopted (positions pause the agents holding the instrument, cash alerts above the band and pauses when persistent, fees are never silently adjusted), because a compensating event for a position difference would make the ledger agree with the broker while destroying the evidence that they disagreed (review round 1, finding 1). (14) A mismatch pauses the agent and alerts, and this crate never lifts it: only a copied `OwnerAcknowledged` carrying step-up evidence does, and no tick, later agreeing reconciliation, or restart clears it. (15) A `ReconciliationRun` is never positioned after a submission it did not cover, so stream I's hold stays positional (DEC-131 item 13) and needs no change and no payload read: the run is appended with `expected_head` equal to the head its snapshot was taken at, so an `OrderSubmitted` that landed in between answers `HeadMismatch` and the reconciliation is recomputed against a fresh snapshot, the payload's high-water mark remaining an audit record rather than a field another stream must read (review round 1, finding 6). (16) External activity is defined by the broker's side: an order or fill with no client order id of ours is external, switches every agent on the account to `exits_only` until the owner acknowledges, and blocks claiming that instrument, while a reject naming an unknown client order id counts only toward the 403 threshold unless it carries a fill (section 7.1, section 7.3). (17) Account restrictions originate here: `AccountStateObserved`, `RejectObserved` and `AccountRestrictionChanged` are account-stream events this crate writes from statuses and rejects, the agent effect is journaled as `AgentModeApplied` and the runtime copies it, and no mode is inferred from a single broker push without the restriction it implies. (18) The account-wide endpoints are unreachable from agent-scoped code: `CancelAll` and `ClosePosition` take an `AccountWideScope` only the account and workspace kill-switch paths can construct, so AGENTS.md rule 13 is a type rule; RC-14's `cancel_all_orders: agent` and `close_all_positions: agent` are scoped actions (every order of that agent by client order id, each confirmed, and exactly the agent's sub-ledger quantity), not those endpoints. (19) The executor's half of mandate spec section 5.9 is the orders: entering `exits_only` or stricter cancels the agent's working opening orders and leaves protective orders resting, while the pending approvals are the runtime's half (DEC-131 item 23), so neither side does both. (20) A sequence is one effect list: cancel, confirmation, gate re-run, submit and re-placement are emitted from one `handle` call, so a crash inside a sequence leaves the journal saying where it stopped and `Input::Started` resumes from that point. (21) Every unprotected interval is an interval and not a flag: `ProtectionChanged` records its start and its end, the `max_unprotected_s` bound is enforced at the tick that reaches it, and the alert is in the same effect list as the cancel and the re-placement. (22) The five parameters trading domain spec sections 5.4 and 5.7 name without a value are read from the effective-dated configuration rather than hard-coded, and the brief proposes them (`max_intent_age` 120 s, `unknown_absent_lookups` 3, `unknown_absent_window_s` 15 s, `protective_replace_buffer_trading_days` 5, `restriction_403_threshold` 3 consecutive), every one failing safe in either direction; adding them to the spec table is a spec-only PR citing this decision (ES-22), so the values stay Proposed (founder). (23) Broker numbers never pass through a float (ES-23): every quantity, price and amount is read as raw text and parsed by `mandate-num`, and exponent form, excess places, or a value already rounded by a JSON float is a typed error with a stable code. (24) `BrokerExchangeRecorded` is redacted before it is recorded, for credentials and for personal data: the authorisation headers are removed and every personal-data field in the body is replaced by an opaque `pii_refs` entry before the bytes are hashed or stored, because the raw `/v2/account` body carries the broker's `account_number` and account id, which journal spec section 6.4 holds by reference (review round 1, finding 3); the pass is a total function over the wire types rather than a denylist, so a field the broker adds later cannot slip through, and the record is inline while small and by `sha256:` artifact reference above the limit. (25) Paper's simulated fees and dividends are marked rather than hidden: `simulated = true`, excluded from the cash and fee comparisons, included in reported P&L and in buying power (section 10, R-22). (26) Reservations are folded state released by trading domain spec section 5.7's whole terminal set, `Filled`, `Canceled`, `Rejected`, `Expired`, `Replaced` and `Abandoned`, not the four broker statuses: an `Unknown` order confirmed absent and then abandoned would otherwise hold its reservation for ever and drain buying power (review round 1, finding 5), a `Replaced` order's reservation passes to the linked new order, and nothing else releases one — not a timeout, not a restart, not a reconciliation that merely disagrees; buying power is the lower of the model and the broker (DEC-34, DEC-104). (27) An uninterpreted input fails loudly (DEC-85): an event type, payload field, broker status or command this crate does not interpret returns `NotInterpreted` naming the owning story, and a broker status outside section 5.7's table pauses the agent and alerts, which is the spec's own last row. (28) Alert payloads carry opaque ids and a message key only (AGENTS.md rule 6, DEC-11), and a test walks every payload the crate can build. (29) Determinism is asserted, not assumed (ES-21): `BTreeMap` and `BTreeSet` only, no floats, no clock, no randomness, exact `mandate-num` arithmetic, `fold_version` 1 with a committed golden journal, and a property that two runs of the same inputs give equal effect lists. (30) The priority channel is the shell's and the journaled order is the handling order (ES-06), so a replay reproduces the priority a kill switch had. (31) ES-24's budget is measured over the gate-and-draft step alone, excluding the append and the broker round trip, and the implementation PR reports it. Credentials come from the account owner's own paper keys through an injected lookup, are held as `SecretString`, and are never stored, journaled, logged, or written; only the paper host is compiled in (ES-23); live accounts, OAuth and fund movement are out of scope and no `BrokerRequest` variant can express them. **Amendment (PR #152, review round 1).** (1a) Item 1 is amended for `mandate-alpaca`: it sits at layer 7, not 6, because it is an adapter over the core that implements `mandate-executor`'s `BrokerConnector` and names its types, and two crates of one layer cannot depend on each other; `mandate-executor` stays at layer 6. The `xtask/layers.toml` and CODEOWNERS entries remain the founder's. (18a) Item 18 is tightened: `AccountWideScope` holds an `AccountWide` enum with only `Account` and `Workspace`, so no code can build an account-wide scope for an agent; the Alpaca transport allows a request only as a (method, path) pair from a fixed endpoint table, and the two account-wide pairs (`DELETE /v2/orders`, `DELETE /v2/positions/{symbol}`) are reachable only through constructors that take an `AccountWideScope`. (32) The connector error is honest: `BrokerConnector::call` returns `ConnectorError`, whose `Unknown` (a timeout, a dropped connection, a 5xx after the request left) is the only variant the shell hands the executor as `Input::Broker(Err(..))`; an answer that could not be read (`Unreadable`) or a request that never left the process (`NotSent`, including a refused path) is neither unknown nor a rejection, and the shell stops and alerts on it. (33) Redaction scope for item 24: personal data is the `id` of the account object (the object that carries an `account_number`) and any `account_id` or `account_number` key anywhere; order ids, client order ids, asset ids and fill ids are not personal data and stay in the record. (34) Cancel on Alpaca is a lookup then a delete: `GET /v2/orders:by_client_order_id`, then `DELETE /v2/orders/{broker id}`; a 404 on the lookup is `Absent`, and a 422 on the delete is followed by a second lookup whose answer (for example `Order(filled)`) settles the outcome. The following rulings came from the coordinator during review and are recorded here as **Proposed until the coordinator confirms them** (PR #152, Decisions needed): (35) a confirmed absence is journaled as `OrderStateChanged` with `state: unknown` and `lookup: absent`; (36) the intent carries its protective prices, `IntentBody::Order { protection: Option<ProtectionPrices { stop, take_profit: Option } > }`, computed by the order builder, and an entry without them is sent as a plain order; (37) `Ports::fees` is the effective-dated `mandate_accounting::Config`, from which paper's simulated fees are computed (item 25); (38) the reference-case harness asserts only the keys this stream owns (`actions`, `orders`, `reconciliation`, `protective_sell_qty`, and seeds `initial.open_orders` and `initial.positions`), never a gate decision, and drops `RC-23` and every case with no such key; (39) an account-wide restriction (external activity, a closing-only account) is held as one row for every agent (`*`), so an agent deployed inside the unacknowledged window is covered too; (40) a copied `ClockAdvanced` carries the causation `clock:<workspace>-<n>`; (41) the property generators start every script with an opening intent and its acknowledgment, and the reconciliation properties give the broker one share more than the shadow ledger, so no property can pass on an empty run. | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79); item 1 (as amended, layer 7 for `mandate-alpaca`) needs the founder's `xtask/layers.toml` and CODEOWNERS entries, item 22's five parameter values are Proposed (founder) because they add rows to a spec table, and items 35 to 41 are Proposed until the coordinator confirms them | R-03 (duplicate or orphaned orders after crashes) is the highest-impact risk in the log, and its mitigation is named as write-ahead intents with idempotency keys, reconciliation on restart, and a fault-injection gate. That mitigation is only real if the key is a pure function of journaled facts rather than anything a process holds, if the journal entry structurally precedes the request, and if the broker rather than our own memory settles an order-state difference — while a position, cash or fee difference is never adopted at all, because section 11 pauses, alerts or refuses to adjust it rather than writing it away; the twelve enumerated crash points are what turns E7-3's acceptance clause into something a test can fail | One crate for the runtime and the executor (an HTTP client and the agent loop under one lint header, and two stream writers in one process); the client order id derived from the intent and the attempt (a resubmission after a confirmed absence would carry a new id and double the order); a submission-time ULID (a restart could not re-derive it); treating a timeout as a rejection (the duplicate R-03 names); a gate behind an injectable trait like the runtime's dry run (configuration could replace the binding gate); comparing positions before ingesting missing fills (every restart would pause a healthy agent); a second ledger inside the executor for paper's simulated records (two ledgers that disagree, and the owner reads the wrong one); recording real fixtures first (no paper credentials or egress in this environment, and a hand-built fixture computed from the spec is the shape a recording replaces) |
