Slice 4 runs as **4a** (the exit price ladder, its rungs and the floor, and [#264](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/264)'s follow-ups) then **4b** (the triggered-stop watchdog, replacing `watched`'s stub). Sessions and auction windows move to slice 5, beside the trading day they read; item (4)'s order is extended accordingly. (10) **The rung ID** (same comment; [trading-domain spec](../specs/trading-domain.md) §2.3, v0.12). Each ladder rung after the first is a new order named for the exit's `client_order_id` plus `-l{n}`, n derived from the journaled rung count, so a restart derives the same ID; the first rung keeps the exit's ID. An agent decision: it neither weakens a safety rule nor touches money. (11) **The watchdog exit's identity** (same comment; §2.3, §5.5, v0.12). The watchdog's exit is the executor's own journaled intent, `md-w-<event>` from the watchdog record (like `for_replacement`), purpose `risk_exit`, its agent the position's single holder (item (3)(b)) or none (`*`). A `*` exit is cancellable by every scope covering its instrument, by its own ID, and no agent-scoped kill switch treats it as its own; each has a test. It is never refused (rule 13). (12) **An exit with nothing to price from** (same comment; §5.6 step 4, v0.12; rules 3 and 13). With no fresh sane bid, no sane bid within 5 minutes and no last trade: a risk exit, protective order, kill-switch flatten or owner exit falls back to its intent's own limit, never below an owner exit's floor, journaled with an owner alert; a discretionary exit is held and re-evaluated every tick, the hold journaled once, never dropped, with the same alert. The ladder resumes stepping at the next priceable quote or trade; a fallback price is never where it ends. `exit_limit` returns no error for any purpose. Tests: a risk exit with no bid and no trade goes at its own limit; an owner exit's fallback respects the floor; a discretionary exit is held, then goes when a quote arrives; `exit_limit` never errs, as a property over every purpose. (13) **Rule 5's wait, bounded in every state** ([#286](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/286) round 1, B1; the coordinator's ruling). The wait starts at the `GateDecided` that allows the exit, its latest one (a held exit has not started waiting, item (8)), folded as the intent's `allowed_at`. The bound covers every opening of the agent in the instrument that the exit waits on, whatever its state: `Submitting`, `Unknown`, `Accepted`, `PartiallyFilled` or `PendingCancel`. A `Submitting` opening is cancelled as soon as the broker accepts it. At the bound it becomes `Unknown`, which a submission unanswered that long is (§5.7), and is queried; an answer that the broker never had it holds the exit on that `Unknown` (rule 13's hold) until §5.7's absence rule resolves it. After every step, every tick and every order-state change, each waiting exit asks the cancel of any opening accepted since, and once nothing holds it back is re-gated and submitted, whatever ended the wait: a confirmed cancel, the broker reporting the opening canceled or filled with no confirmation, an expiry, a reject, a fill, or a query answered past the bound. A cancel the broker refuses while the opening's cancel is outstanding, in any state, is overdue at once and queried, never read as the opening's own reject; a refusal after the opening filled changes nothing. §5.7's table has no edge from `PendingCancel` to `Expired` or `Rejected`, so such a report is journaled and ignored and the bound ends that wait; the table is not changed here. Tests: the reviewer's three paths (a `Submitting` opening cancelled once accepted, or queried at the bound; an opening reported gone with no confirmation; an opening filled while its cancel is outstanding), a refusal after the opening moved on, a wait that starts at the allow ending a hold, and a property over random scripts: no exit stays neither submitted nor denied past the bound plus one tick except under rule 13's holds, and none goes while its agent's opening is live and unanswered. (14) **A passive exit over a stop-only placement** (#286 round 1, M1). Where the resting protection has no take-profit (crypto's one stop-limit, DEC-36), a passive exit answers slice 5's `Unimplemented { story: "E7-4" }` in `begin_exit` before anything is cancelled or journaled, so the stop keeps resting: never an OCO in a crypto instrument (§5.4 allows it simple orders only) and never a remainder left unprotected. `passive_exit` answers the same stub for such a sequence found on the journal. Like `replace` and `crypto_add`, it is unreachable before slice 2 (item (4)'s pin). Tests: a stop-only placement with the exit passive and marketable, and a journaled passive sequence with no take-profit. (15) **The release path's journal order** (#286 round 1, minor 1). A held exit that a tick releases journals its sequence's `ProtectionChanged` and protective cancels before its `GateDecided`, as on arrival (item (8)); `a_held_exit_leaves_protection_resting_until_released` pins the order. (16) **Modes other than `normal`** (#286 round 1, minor 4). A copied `AgentModeApplied` whose `to` is anything but `normal`, one of mandate spec §7.4's three stricter modes or a string that names no mode, cancels the agent's openings: cancelling only reduces risk (rule 3), so an unrecognised mode fails in that direction. No behaviour change; `any_mode_but_normal_is_read_as_stricter` pins it. (17) **A passive exit that ends without its OCO** (rule 3; found by #286 round 1's mutation run, outside the ruling). A passive exit denied or abandoned once its OCO's cancel is confirmed, for instance too old at a slow confirmation, ends its sequence as a marketable sequence's finished exit does: the recorded OCO is re-placed for the position held and the sequence ends, where before the position was left with no protection, no interval and a stale sequence. Test: an abandoned passive exit re-places the protection. A passive exit held, or waiting on its opening, after that confirmation opens an interval the bound ends (item (19)). (18) **Rule 5's wait ends when the opening goes overdue** (#286 round 2, B1′, the coordinator's ruling; amends (13)). An exit's wait on its agent's opening ends when the opening is marked overdue, at rule 5's bound or when the broker refuses its cancel, not when the query is answered. The query still goes. A broker that never answers the query, or 404s an opening it acknowledged earlier, therefore holds no exit by its silence. An opening the broker may still hold is `Unknown`, and the gate holds the exit on it (`unknown_order_in_flight`, rule 13's hold); one still resting is a self-cross for the broker to refuse. `OrderDetail::answered` is removed. The overdue mark applies to one submission attempt (nit 2): a resubmission after a confirmed absence is a new attempt with a new detail, and an exit waits on it afresh. The property's venue can now lose an opening it acknowledged, so every later query 404s, and drop query answers; restoring the old condition fails it (minimal script: an exit waiting on two openings, the acknowledged one lost, the exit still waiting at 15 s). Tests: the rule-5 tests updated to the new timing, and the widened property. (19) **A passive exit's wait is bounded** (#286 round 2, M2′; closes (17)'s gap). Once nothing rests and a passive exit is neither placed nor finished, because it waits on its opening or the gate holds it, `settle` journals `unprotected_start` once for its sequence; `passive_exit`'s placement or `replace` ends it. At `max_unprotected_s` the bound ends every exit in the instrument, in any sequence: a working one is cancelled, and one with no order yet is abandoned with `unprotected_interval_limit`, so `settle` re-places the recorded OCO in the same step (item (17)). It ends them all, not the sequence's exit alone, because `settle` re-places protection only once no exit is working, and one left working would keep the position unprotected past the bound. The old OCO is never re-placed while the exit waits. Tests: the bound ending two working exits; a passive exit waiting on an opening the broker never had (the query 404s and the gate holds the exit on the `Unknown`) and one held on another agent's `Unknown`, each bounded at 30 s with the OCO re-placed for the whole position, and a placement ending the interval its wait opened. An opening acknowledged and then lost releases the exit at rule 5's bound (item (18)), so the interval's bound does not fire on that path. (20) **A passive exit beside other exits** (#286 round 2, M1′). The remainder OCO covers the position less the exit and less what every other exit in the instrument may still sell: each live non-protective sell's unfilled quantity (the set `gate::available` walks, plus an order back in `Intent` for resubmission) and each exit received with no order yet. The passive exit is never refused for them (rule 13). Where anything was netted, the placement hands the sequence on: `unprotected_start` records it again, its interval is bounded like any other, and once the other exits end `replace` covers the held quantity less what protective orders still cover, so the stop never covers more than the position before or after they fill. An exit the placement was sized around goes without cancelling it (`fits`: what protection covers plus what exits may still sell is at most the long position). Any other exit cancels the whole placement, the passive exit's own OCO included, and waits for the confirmations; `replace` then re-places what is left at the smaller size through the same interval. That is the ruling's conservative interim, taken because slice 2 owns the replace and because cancelling protection before a risk-reducing sell keeps §5.3's reading of `gate::available` unchanged, so no exit is ever denied for the shares a passive OCO holds. At the bound (item (19)) the exits beside the placement are ended and the placement, being protection, keeps resting. Tests: `a_passive_exit_keeps_the_stop_on_exactly_the_position` extended to every split of ten shares between a passive and a marketable exit, in both release orders, filled or cancelled (stop ≤ position before and after the fill, and = position once the other exit ends); an exit waiting on its opening that the placement was sized around; an exit it was not sized around; and the handed-on bound's cancel and abandon. Ten seeded bugs are each caught. (21) **Rule 13's hold codes in the property** (#286 round 2, minor 1). The property exempts an exit only while the gate holds it with `unknown_order_in_flight`, `agent_paused`, `agent_stopped` or `broker`. The ruling named `account_trading_blocked`, which is the executor gate's deny code for an add in a blocked account; it holds an exit there with `broker` (`gate::account_failure`), so the set uses the code the gate emits. (22) **An ES-13 size exception for #286** (#286 round 3, minor 1). Outside `#[cfg(test)]`, excluding blank and doc lines, the slice is +528 −124, net 404 non-generated lines against the merge base, over ES-13's 400 for a safety-critical crate. Round 2's three fixes added about 146 of them, so the earlier +379 −91 figure is stale. The slice is not split, because those three fixes depend on each other. The sizing fit (M1) feeds the protection check that both the settle step and the cancel wait read. The unprotected interval (M2) is what bounds a handed-on passive exit. The overdue release (B1) is what lets that bound be reached when the broker reports a lost opening. Landing any subset would ship an intermediate state a review has already found unsound. | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | Reversible engineering and process: it moves work between slices of one crate and names the conservative owner of a leg. It weakens no safety rule: an unattributed leg holds openings and never an exit, it is cancelled by its own id by every exit and kill switch in its instrument, and the stubs are unreachable while the pin holds | Land slice 7 on its own with about 24 temporary `BEHAVIOUR_ONLY_TESTS` rows (reverses the shrink-only list); keep the fold stubbed and land only the confirmed cancel (no live test reaches it but the kill switch); give an unattributed position to the mandate's only agent (guesses an owner, which rule 3 forbids); have `ProtectionChanged` carry `agent` (a journal-spec change for a fact the entry already records) |
| DEC-161 | **How E10-1's semantic slice reads §4.1 and §4.2 where the spec, the reference, and the merged types leave a choice** (E10-1 implementation, slice V; [task brief](tasks/M5-F-mandate-spec.md), claim [#124](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/124)). (1) **Two slices, for ES-13.** Family V is about 555 non-blank, non-doc src lines in `mandate-spec`. This slice carries every V-rule except E17-1's field split, V-038 included, the four worst-case figures, W-001 to W-005, `Mandate::at`, and the harness's `semantic` arm (about 485 lines in `mandate-spec`, about 160 in `mandate-refcases`, a tool crate); the next carries V-003, V-034 to V-037, V-039, W-006, and `worst_case_stop_distance` (about 70). V-038 cannot wait: its test in `tests/validate.rs` is `pending E17-1`, and once `validate` is not a stub that test would fail on an assertion rather than at a stub, which the pending gate refuses (DEC-137). Both `pending E17-1` markers in the file therefore go here; `the_platform_proposes_five_instruments_and_three_revisions` passes on this slice's V-020. **The coordinator ruled on #238 in round 1 to accept this slice at about 508 src lines as a one-off, setting no precedent for other slices:** it reviews in one sitting, it is tabular rule-per-row, and splitting it further would separate the rules from their shared figures; part 2 stays a separate PR. (2) **A diverged mandate is refused.** V-009 (the order the owner wrote), V-015 (a date with no calendar position), and V-020 (a listed default's value) read the document the mandate was parsed from, so `validate` first calls `canonical` and returns `diverged` if the public fields no longer match it: a report describes only the mandate that is hashed and enforced. (3) **The figures are exact.** Each is `Usd × Fraction` with no rounding; a fraction past `Fraction`'s nine places, or a product `Usd` cannot hold, makes the document unevaluable, `out_of_range` naming the field (DEC-128 item 4), never a V-code and never a rounded figure. V-002 and V-032 compare exactly. Display rounding stays the open founder item. (4) **Provenance entries cover their subtree.** An entry at a path speaks for everything under it. V-020 judges each entry: a `platform_default` is valid only where a §7 path covers it and, where §7 fixes a value, the document holds that value at the entry's own path, so an entry under a fixed-value field (`/autonomy/default/x`) names nothing and is refused; `approvers` only with one workspace user; system fields are skipped. V-022 reads every entry on, under, or **above** an `auto`'s path (`/autonomy/default`, `/autonomy/admission`, `/autonomy/rules/<i>/then`), so `/autonomy` stated by the compiler cannot carry an `auto` past it; `ref.py` reads the exact path only, and no case tells the two apart. (5) **V-023 reads §6.3's table:** `thesis_confidence` is "decimal in [0, 1]" there, so it is bounded like `combined_score` and `drawdown`, although `ConditionField::is_unit_bounded` omits it; and a condition decimal wider than the `Ratio` the order path compares it as is V-023 at load, which is stream F's half of #216's backlog row. (6) **V-006 claims are on a group or on an ungrouped instrument,** two variants rather than one string, so a group id that spells an asset id never claims that instrument (the `_group_claimed` reading #216's review found in `ref.py`). (7) **`ConnectionId::parse`,** the schema's `$defs/id` and nothing wider, joins `ModelId::parse` under DEC-128 item 22: V-031's `PreviousVersion` cannot be built without it. (8) **`ref.py` accepts any `platform_default` on `leveraged_etp_disclosure_version`;** §7 and the merged `platform_defaultable` table say `null` only, and the crate follows them. A 60,000-document differential against `ref.py`'s `semantic` (three seeds) found this and DEC-151 item 1's `too_deep` as its only disagreements. (9) **An end date `Date` cannot hold is V-015.** `mandate_time::Date` spans 1970 to 9999, so a pattern-valid `end_date` before 1970 is read as no date (DEC-151 item 3) and reported as V-015 rather than V-030. The document is refused either way, and any such date is before every validation date. (10) **Part 2's readings** (E17-1's field split). W-006's "a research agent configured" is a signal model with `admits_instruments`, as `ref.py` reads it; a research envelope with no admitting model is V-036 either way. V-003 compares the pinned list to exactly the goal's one instrument, in order and count. V-035 counts the pinned instruments exactly, and a count `u32` cannot hold is V-035 rather than a wrapped number. `worst_case_stop_distance` is the stop plus the offset when the universe admits crypto, summed exactly as a `Ratio` and returned in the `positive_decimal` grammar (the sum of two open fractions can reach past one), while without the offset it is the document's own stop in its `open_fraction` grammar; `None` when there is no stop, and a distance past a `Ratio`'s 24 places is `out_of_range` naming its field. The four figures keep computing `position × stop + position × offset` as part 1 merged them, so a figure's `out_of_range` boundary is unchanged | Accepted (agent, reversible engineering, DEC-79); item 1's size Accepted (coordinator, #238 round 1, one-off) | Each reading refuses rather than admits where the spec is silent, and none widens what a document may do | One PR for the whole family (about 545 lines in the safety-critical crate); rounding the figures; reading exact provenance paths only; bounding `thesis_confidence` only where `is_unit_bounded` says; a string key for group claims |
| DEC-162 | **How the shared harness runs family A (the sixteen `kind: autonomy` cases) through `mandate_builder::classify`** (E6-2's harness-and-status PR, DEC-77 stage 4, DEC-130; claim [#125](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/125)). The arm is `crates/mandate-refcases/src/mandate/autonomy.rs`, its own module as `research.rs` is. (1) **The policy is the strictly parsed document's, not a `ValidatedMandate`'s.** The case header says an `autonomy` case evaluates §6.2 alone, `reference/mandate/generate.py` asserts only the JSON Schema for family A, and no case states a validation context; under the fixture's `validation_context_defaults` (one approver user) MC-A10's two-approver threshold breaks V-024, so validating would fail a case for a rule it does not test. `classify` re-checks the rules it reads itself (unique ids, V-017, V-018, V-023). (2) **No policy overlay is applied**: no case states a policy chain, and `PolicyOverlay::narrow` is not part of `classify` (DEC-128 item 9). (3) **An opening's `action` must state all fifteen §6.3 facts** and nothing else; none is defaulted, because `ActionContext` infers none (DEC-130 item 19). This is stricter than `mandate-builder`'s own harness. (4) **A risk-reducing `action` states only `purpose`**, since §6.2 step 3 decides it before any fact is read; a fact stated beside it fails the case. The harness fills the other fields with zeros, `crypto`, and an instrument no case names; they cannot decide a case, because a decision reached through the rules is labelled `rule:<id>`, `default`, or `admission_ceiling`, never `builtin_risk_reducing`, so a `classify` that read them fails on `by`. (5) **The approval is compared both ways**: an ASK must state and match `approvers_required` and `on_timeout`; an AUTO or a DENY that states either fails, since §6.4 gives it no approval. (6) **Every key is read (DEC-85)**: the case's top level, its `action`, and its `expect` are each swept, `action` joins the shared `CASE_KEYS`, and `autonomy` leaves `PENDING_KINDS`. The oracle is the module's own tests (every expected member edited, dropped, and planted; every action member dropped and planted; every stated fact read back through `Facts` under its own §6.3 name), each shown to fail on a planted harness bug, and five bugs seeded in `mandate-builder` (step 3 skipped, the ceiling at `>=`, the ceiling ignoring `new_instrument`, one approver above the threshold, the last matching rule deciding) are each caught by the cases. (7) **The status move is its own PR.** The spec guard refuses `crates/mandate-refcases/status.toml` beside code (ES-22), as family N's harness (#219 to #223) and its status PR (#224) were split. So this PR lands the arm and its oracle, and a status-only PR citing this entry then lists `MC-A01` to `MC-A16` as passing, at the head of the `[mandate]` table and apart from E10-3's MC-C rows. (8) **Family B stays pending on E6-8.** `propose` and `decide` pass every B case in `mandate-builder`'s own harness, which hands `decide` the case's expected verdict. Running B here means composing propose, the gate, and `decide`, and a `GateInput` needs account, instrument, market, and conduct snapshots, a `GateConfig`, and a `RiskSnapshot` that `gate_state` does not state, so the harness would need documented defaults. Could run today: the ten holds (MC-B03, B06, B10, B12, B15, B16, B18, B19, B20, B29) reach no gate, but each states a `gate_state` that would need a reading of its own; the four exits (B04, B07, B23 allow, B22 defer on `discretionary_exit_regular_session_only`), because an exit passes the owed checks; and B21 (deny `max_orders_per_day`), because check 6's orders-per-day item is implemented and a stop there precedes the owed refusal. Cannot until E6-8: the ten equity openings the gate allows (B01, B02, B05, B08, B09, B11, B13, B14, B24, B25), since checks 5 and 6 are owed and the gate returns `Unimplemented`; the three crypto increases (B26 to B28) also need E6-10 for check 2's "USD pairs only". MC-B17 and MC-B30 to B32 are stream G's (DEC-130 item 3) | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | Harness readings only: no production crate changes, and every reading makes a case fail sooner rather than pass on something unread. Validating the policy would test V-024 in a family that does not state its inputs; defaulting an opening's facts would let a case pass on a value nobody wrote | Validate each case's mandate under the defaults (fails MC-A10 on V-024, a rule family A does not test); default absent opening facts as `mandate-builder`'s harness does (passes a case on unstated input); ignore approval members on an AUTO or a DENY (a stated member would go unchecked); harness family B now with the gate's verdict read from the case (a stage-4 PR must run the real gate, thirteen B cases would stop at `Unimplemented`, and the other fifteen need gate defaults no case states) |
| DEC-163 | **How E6-8's implementation reads trading-domain §8.2 and §9.6: check 5, check 6, the pacing of an allowed exit, the cancel rule and the surveillance report** ([task brief](tasks/E6-3-risk-gate.md), DEC-129, DEC-77 stage 3; stream G, claim [#123](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/123)). Every control compares against `GateInput::now` and an instant or count the executor folded; `mandate-risk` reads no clock. Only an opening or an increase is ever denied by checks 5 and 6 (`AGENTS.md` rule 13): a risk exit, a protective order and a kill switch's order pass them untouched, an owner exit is paced by the participation caps only, and a discretionary exit is paced by all of them and never denied. (1) **Freshness.** A quote's age is judged upstream: §4.2 leaves the staleness threshold to the data profile's configuration and DEC-168 item 5 makes the bound the reader's, so a quote past it reaches the gate as `MarketSnapshot::quote: None`. No quote, a quote stamped after `now`, or a `stale_mark` instrument restriction (DEC-129 item 23) is `stale_mark` for an opening at check 5. A crossed quote (`bid > ask`) refuses to decide an opening (`GateError::QuoteUnsane`); a locked one (`bid = ask`) is sane. A reducing purpose never reaches check 5 (§8.2: "any source"), a crossed or missing quote prices no exit, and no usable quote bars market orders as the stale-quote arm of a presumed halt (DEC-129 item 28), so a market exit is sent as a marketable limit. (2) **The collar is a closed interval.** For a buy, `[ask × (1 − band), ask × (1 + x)]`; for a sell, `[bid × (1 − x), bid × (1 + band)]`. The passive band is measured from the same side of the quote as the aggressive bound, the stricter of the two readings of "a wider passive band". Both ends round inward (`Price::collar_bound`), both ends pass, and a price past either is `price_outside_collar`. `x` is `collar_other_x` when the 20-day median dollar volume is known and below the threshold, and `collar_liquid_x` when it is at least the threshold or unknown: an unknown median takes the narrower collar, so the ambiguous case admits fewer prices (round-1 review m5). An opening with an unknown median is denied by the eligibility floor before check 5, so this tier binds the pricing of a discretionary exit. (3) **Check 6's order** is §9.6's table order after the collar: order size against trailing 5-minute volume, daily participation, order-to-fill, the opposite-fill interval, the close window, self-trade prevention, then the mandate's `max_orders_per_day`. Both participation caps and order-to-fill report `conduct_limit_breached`, as does self-trade (the registry has no self-trade code). The opposite-fill interval includes its last instant: §9.6's "within 60 seconds after" an opposite-side fill still covers an opening exactly 60 s after it, which is denied, and one a nanosecond later is allowed; only a fill on the other side counts. This is the reading that never allows an opening the text could deny, and it differs on purpose from the re-entry cooldown's "until `reentry_cooldown_s` after" (mandate §5.3), which lifts at the boundary. It amends this item's first draft (strict), after CI's mutation gate on #311 found nothing pinning the boundary; the tests PR's `properties::only_an_opposite_side_fill_starts_the_interval` oracle (`elapsed < 60`) is corrected to `elapsed <= 60` in its own tests-correction PR. Order-to-fill is compared without dividing (`orders > max × max(fills, 1)`), counted from `order_to_fill_min_orders` orders, the proposal not among them. (4) **A missing volume.** An opening whose `trailing_5m_volume` or `adv_20d` the executor could not supply is `conduct_limit_breached`, failing closed as the eligibility floor does for an unknown median. An exit with a missing volume is not sliced by that cap, since a cap with no volume cannot say how much to hold back and rule 13 forbids refusing the exit. A cap that works out to zero (an order cap of zero or truncated to zero, or a daily cap used up or overdrawn) is treated exactly like a missing volume: it slices nothing and leaves `applied` unset. A cap above zero but below `min_order_size` slices at `min_order_size`, or sends the whole exit if that is smaller, never below it. No new defer or verdict code. This is the round-1 review's ruling on its blocker B2: before it, a zero cap allowed an owner or discretionary exit with `qty` 0, an order the broker refuses, which would deny the exit rule 13 protects; risk exits and protective orders were never sliced and are untouched. (5) **The collar prices a discretionary exit.** A limit past the aggressive end is raised (a sell) or lowered (a buy) to it; a limit past the passive end is brought to it; a market order becomes a marketable limit at the aggressive end, since "the collar prices them" leaves no exit unpriced. An equity price then goes on the Reg NMS grid inward (`Price::on_tick`); a crypto price stays at the bound, because the snapshot carries no venue increment and the executor's rounding against the order (§2.1) moves it inward. `Pacing::applied` names `Collar`. With no usable quote there is nothing to price by and the exit goes as proposed. In the close window a discretionary exit is sent as a marketable limit (`CloseWindow`); an owner exit is not, because §9.6 paces owner exits "by the participation caps only". (6) **Slices.** A discretionary or owner exit is sliced to the smaller of `order_size_participation × trailing_5m_volume` and what is left of `daily_participation × adv_20d` today, each truncated to the instrument's increment (whole shares unless fractionable). Each cap that binds is named in `applied`, where a cap binds when its slice (item 4) is below the proposed quantity; a cap equal to it slices nothing. Item 4 leaves no zero slice, so no defer code for one is needed: the PR's question whether to add one is answered no, and its backlog row is withdrawn. (7) **Cancels.** `evaluate_cancel` denies `min_resting_time` for a non-marketable opening order canceled strictly inside `min_resting_time_s` of `resting_since`, unless the cancel precedes a risk-reducing order. That flag is how every exit sequence and kill switch cancels are exempt: `CancelInput` has no cause field and the executor sets it. The two booleans stay in #311; a required `CancelCause { RiskReducing, Replace { marketable }, Discretionary }` is a follow-up story with its own tests correction (backlog, round-1 review n2). The decision's purpose is the canceled order's (`Open`, `Protective`, else `DiscretionaryExit`, since a working order records no exit origin), and its only check is check 6. (8) **Surveillance** states figures and flags thresholds, and judges nothing (§9.6: "the platform does not supervise users' trading"). Orders are `submitted − cancels_excluded` (an error if the exclusion is larger), the ratio is `orders ÷ max(fills, 1)` truncated to an integer (round-1 review m4), and the breach uses item 3's comparison. Self-trade flags each instrument with any agent in its set. Close-window activity flags any count above zero, since §4.3 and §9.6 allow no opening there. Concentration is each end-of-day market value as a share of the agent's equity, rounded up, and stated as `1` above equity or when equity is not positive. It is a figure only and never flagged; `SurveillanceBreach::Concentration` is not raised in v1. §3.3 supplies no number, and a threshold the platform chose would be compliance-visible, so a threshold is the founder's (backlog). This answers the PR's second question: no concentration flag. The `Ratio` to `Fraction` step goes through the exact decimal text, because `mandate-num` has no conversion (backlog). (9) **No new `mandate-num` arithmetic.** The brief's `Qty::times_fraction` is the existing `Qty::portion(Fraction, ShareIncrement)`, truncation to the increment, so the PR touches `mandate-risk` only, plus `xtask`'s `BEHAVIOUR_ONLY_TESTS`, whose two `mandate-risk` rows the pacing retires. (10) **What still fails closed.** After E6-8 no §9.1 check is owed for a US equity. An ordinary whole-share limit opening is decided; a crypto opening is refused `Unimplemented("evaluate", "E6-10")` for check 2's USD-pairs rule, and an equity opening breaking §5.3 rule 2 or 7 in a way DEC-129 item 27 leaves unregistered (below `min_order_size`, fractional in a whole-share instrument, fractional GTC or fractional bracket) is refused `Unimplemented(.., "DEC-129 item 27")`. E6-6's second slice is an input gap, not a refusal: the gate reads `AgentSnapshot::day_trades` as supplied. (11) **The folded state's contract** (round-1 review M3). "Today" is the risk day, 00:00 to 00:00 America/New_York (mandate §5.3). `ConductState::filled_today` and `orders_today_per_instrument` count only the deciding agent's orders in the account it trades (§9.6: "per agent per instrument per day") and reset daily; a fill counts its order once however many fills it took, so partial fills cannot dilute the ratio, and orders count every purpose, each `client_order_id` once, rejected ones included, less exit-sequence and kill-switch cancels. `last_opposite_fill_at` is the latest fill per instrument and side by any agent in any account of the related-accounts group and is never reset; an older fill stops blocking when the interval passes. `participation_today` is the day's filled quantity on both sides by every agent across the related-accounts group plus their working orders' open quantity, reset daily. `resting_since` is each working order's broker acceptance, which `evaluate` does not read; the executor passes it to `evaluate_cancel`. `AccountSnapshot::related_account_resting` lists every side on which an order rests, per instrument, in any account of the related-accounts group, this account and the deciding agent's own orders included, protective orders too: the gate filters nothing. In this account a non-protective order already denies an opening at check 4, so the set adds resting protective orders there; a bracket opening beside a resting protective sell is therefore denied, the conservative reading, and whether a stop counts is a backlog item. `CancelInput::marketable` is whether the order's limit trades against the current quote, `false` with no usable quote. Each reading counts more or blocks sooner where the spec is silent. (12) **Size exception.** #311 adds 518 non-test source lines to `mandate-risk` at review, 537 after the round-1 fixes, against ES-13's 400. It stays one PR, as DEC-167 item 5 kept a slice over 400 whole: checks 5 and 6, the pacing of an allowed exit, the cancel rule and the surveillance report land together so that no check is half-wired, since the gate refuses to decide an opening while a check is owed and `-D warnings` refuses code only a test reaches. Not split (round-1 review M1). | Accepted (agent, reversible engineering, DEC-79); the round-1 review's rulings on #311 are recorded in items 2, 4, 6, 7, 8, 11 and 12. A concentration threshold stays with the founder, and until then the report flags none | Each reading refuses an opening where the spec is silent and never refuses an exit; every boundary has an in-module test and a planted bug it catches | The passive band measured from the opposite side of the quote (wider); a missing volume allowing an opening; denying a zero slice; allowing a zero slice with `qty` 0 (the first draft, overruled as B2); a defer code for a zero slice; an unknown median taking the wider collar; a platform-chosen concentration threshold; a crate-level quote-age constant (rejected by DEC-168 item 5); a new `Qty::times_fraction` |
| DEC-164 | **The pending gate gives one verdict per tree: a property is judged by the minimal failure proptest reports, and its run is isolated** (the coordinator's ruling on [#230](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/230) comment 5860360338; #227's round-2 delta review). Amends [DEC-137](#decisions) item (1) for properties only. (1) `cargo xtask ci pending` reads a failing property by proptest's own report of it, `Test failed: <why>.` over the minimal input or `Test aborted: <why>`, and nothing else in its output: not the panics of the cases shrinking moved past, and not the minimal input's `Debug`. Any other test fails on one panic and is still read whole. (2) The run clears every inherited `PROPTEST_*` variable and sets `PROPTEST_RNG_SEED` (the seed #199 pinned) and `PROPTEST_DISABLE_FAILURE_PERSISTENCE`, so no `*.proptest-regressions` file saved by an earlier run on other code is replayed or written; each property keeps the case count its source names. (3) `mandate-executor`'s three E7-4 properties (`protective_sell_quantity_never_exceeds_the_position`, `every_unprotected_interval_has_a_journaled_start_and_end`, `no_interval_exceeds_the_limit_without_an_alert`) get `BEHAVIOUR_ONLY_TESTS` rows, expiring with E7-4: their minimal failure is their own E7-4 assertion, which the backlog said they would need once the gate read the cause. (4) `cargo xtask ci mutants` reads an empty `cargo mutants --list --json --in-diff` answer as zero mutants (the parse #233 also landed; one implementation is kept) and ends the job there, before a run that could test nothing; any other output that is not a JSON list is still an error | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | On main at 874927c the whole-output reading passed those three properties on the pinned seed and failed them on seeds 1, 2 and 3; read by their minimal failure they give the same verdict on all four. On #230 thirty properties whose minimal failure was a prefix assertion passed on a stub report from a case shrinking had moved past, and a checkout's saved failures changed which cases a later run tried. The change only removes evidence the gate accepted: every property that fails away from its stub at its minimal case is now reported, and the three new rows are named one by one and reported for deletion once their tests stop at a stub. It is process tooling, so an agent decision | Pin a case count by environment (overrides only properties that take proptest's defaults, and changes what a property means in `ci test`); run each pending property under several seeds (multiplies the run's time and still reads incidental text); classify by panic source location (a stub's `Err` is unwrapped in the test file, so the location names the test, not the stub); leave the three properties without rows (the gate would fail on main) |
| DEC-166 | **How E7-7's implementation lands in `mandate-shell`** ([task brief](tasks/E7-7-tracer-bullet.md), DEC-138, DEC-157, stream L). (1) **Sliced by upstream readiness** (the brief's Decisions needed 6). Slice 1 makes live the three adapters whose upstream is implemented and whose every input the shell holds: `StoredBars` (`inspect` then `dataset::read`), `MovingAverage` (E4-2's crossover) and `AlpacaConnector` (`TradingClient` over the given transport, driven by a current-thread tokio runtime that the shell builds per call). Every other adapter stays a stub answering `Cause::Unimplemented`, and later slices flip them one at a time. (2) **The shell never invents an input.** A stage stays refusing while its upstream is a stub, or while any of its inputs has no production source. On `main` at 15218ac that covers: `ValidationContext` for `Validate`; the sub-ledger and broker positions, session and `max_exit_offset` that `agent_flatten` needs but the runtime's `FlattenRequest` does not carry; `ExecutorConfig`, the fee `Config` and the instrument snapshot for `mandate_executor::handle`; the account snapshot, quote and `RiskContext` for the builder; the `GateInput` assembly; the agent-stream payload schemas; and the protective prices. In addition, `mandate-executor`'s own crate doc forbids any caller outside its tests from driving `handle` until the gate port lands. A flatten probe over a synthetic empty input would pass while `plan` could not plan, so it is not written. (3) **The binary holds no transport yet.** It runs over `Disconnected`, which refuses every request as `NotSent { code: "no_transport" }`, because every run refuses at the first probe before any request. `AlpacaPaperHttp` is not `Clone`, and the connector drives a fresh `TradingClient` per call over a clone of its transport, so the manual run's transport arrives with the slice that makes the exit probes real, together with a stream K change (derive `Clone`, or implement the transport for a shared reference). (4) **Stored data is trusted only when `inspect` says so.** It must be the pinned symbol's daily bars, with no partition problem, no duplicate start, no trading day listed without a bar, no true or unclassified gap, and no split inside the span (or corporate actions recorded for only part of it). The closes are returned exactly as stored: the shell adjusts, filters and repairs nothing. `Admitted` gains the pinned `symbol`, and `ModelRef` gains the envelope's `params`. (5) **A one-close outlier is not the shell's to judge.** Doing so is price arithmetic, which DEC-138 item 3 keeps out of the shell. `tests/tracer.rs::outlier_close` stays pending until a market-data trust rule or E6-8's mark-and-collar exists, and this is reported to their owners. | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | Each keeps DEC-138's rule that the shell holds no trading logic, and each keeps every unfinished stage refusing; none touches live money, spending, legal text or a safety invariant | Write the adapters against invented configuration (a shell decision the brief's stop conditions forbid); probe the flatten path on an empty synthetic input (a probe that passes while `plan` cannot plan); wire the binary to `AlpacaPaperHttp` now (it would read credentials for a run that always refuses before a request) |
| DEC-167 | **How E6-4's `mandate-spec` part is delivered and read** (stream H, claim [#124](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/124); [task brief](tasks/M5-F-mandate-spec.md), families R, T, and L). (1) **Slices, one PR at a time, under ES-13.** Slice 1 is T (`risk::risk_day`) plus the three §5 primitives that read no mandate: `Confirmation::update`, `size_factor`, and `reset_lift_order`. It takes the breach-time property live now. Then comes L (`goal::status`). Then the §5 fold in two parts, since together it is about 1,100 lines: R2 covers marks, fills, risk days, clock ticks, universe changes, and the profit stop; R3 covers acknowledgments, allocation changes, floor loosening, goal completion, and retirement. L, R2, and R3 wait for E10-1 slice P, because every one of their tests builds a `ValidatedMandate`, and `ValidatedMandate::new` is slice P's. The harness assertion that a stub is never a pass moved ahead of T in a tests-correction PR ([#239](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/239)). (2) **The risk day** is the America/New_York calendar date of the instant. Its bounds are that date's midnight and the next date's midnight, and `length_s` is the distance between them. New York changes clocks at 02:00, so both midnights always exist, and the boundary instant belongs to the day it opens (§5.4, MC-T01 to MC-T05). (3) **V-040: the scale factors' fractional digits sum to at most 12.** This follows the coordinator's rulings on [#245](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/245) (major 1) and [#251](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/251) (round 1). `drawdown_ladder` allows 5 rungs and `factor` allows 28 places (`^0\.[0-9]{0,27}[1-9]$`), so a schema-valid ladder's size factor could need 140. The size factor has two consumers, and neither may round it (DEC-128 item 4; §8.3 names its only three roundings). The risk state reports it as a `Ratio` of 24 places (§5.5). The order builder multiplies its targets by it as a `SizeFraction` of 12 places (§8.3 step 2), whose bound is load-bearing for the builder's 256-bit budget, so it cannot widen. The tighter of the two binds. Without the rule, one such mandate would make every risk step an error, or every builder proposal empty, against rules 3 and 13. The rule bounds the sum of the digits rather than the digits of the whole product, because a subset can need more places than the whole ladder: 2¹³ × 10⁻¹³ needs 13, while 2¹³ × 10⁻¹³ × 5¹³ × 10⁻¹⁰ = 10⁻¹⁰ needs 10. The sum bounds every subset. It is a V-rule rather than a narrower `factor` pattern, because a pattern would take away precision the schema grants each factor on its own; five 2-place rungs still fit. A `Violation` carries a code and no pointer, and a sum has no single offending rung, so the report points at `/risk/drawdown_ladder`. It is agent-accepted under DEC-79 like the rest of this row: a stricter V-rule only refuses more mandates and weakens no safety rule. The fold's size factor stays out of R2 until V-040 is implemented.  (4) **A goal's reasons, as `reference/mandate/ref.py`'s `goal_status` orders them** (the coordinator's ruling on [#279](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/279), round 1, blocker 1). `end_date` is judged first, then an accumulate goal's target, then its spend (DEC-128 item 26 orders those two). A goal past its date is done for its date, whatever else holds. §3.1 lists the three conditions without ranking them, and `ref.py` is the oracle the cases come from. Only the reason label moves: `then` and `stop_reason` are `ref.py`'s for each goal type. "Below the minimum order" for the quantity is the remainder's notional **at the ask**, as `ref.py` reads it. That ask must come from a quote that passed §5.6's sane-and-fresh filter; the order path's caller in `mandate-risk` (stream G) guarantees it. A remainder is measured exactly: `target_qty` as a `Qty`, or `out_of_range` naming `/goal/target_qty`; `max_spend_usd` as a `Usd`, or `out_of_range` naming `/goal/max_spend_usd`. A position past the target leaves nothing. A zero `qty_increment` and a negative `goal_spent_usd` are `invalid_input` for an accumulate goal, the only type that reads them. An end date whose closing midnight the calendar cannot state (9999-12-30 and -31) is an error, unless an accumulate goal is done for its target or its spend anyway. Both bad inputs are refused before the end date is read, so past its date they are refused alike (#279 review round 2). (5) **The §5 fold ships in four slices, not item 1's R2 and R3.** Written whole, it is about 1,050 non-blank non-test lines, so item 1's two halves would each be well over ES-13's 400. Each slice takes live the tests that pass on it, and everything it does not fold yet is `Unimplemented`, never a silent answer. R1 is the limit conditions of §5.2 and §5.6, which the public `risk::conditions` reads, with #261's minors 1 to 3. R2 is the fold's spine over marks, fills, and clock ticks: the agent sub-ledger, the ladder, breach confirmation with the hard trigger, the daily-loss trigger, the floor, and the effective mode. At about 560 lines it is the one slice over 400. It cannot split further, because `-D warnings` refuses code that only a test reaches, and every one of those pieces runs at every step. R3 adds staleness, universe changes, the risk day (rollover, renewal, and lift), and the profit stop. R4 adds acknowledgments, allocation changes, floor loosening, goal completion, and retirement. **`RiskState` borrows the `SessionClock` it opened with** (`RiskState<'c>`). `step` takes no clock, and every later step counts an equity's staleness and lift delays on it. No caller names the type, so every caller compiles unchanged. The clock is not state, so two states that folded the same inputs are equal whichever clock each holds. **`mandate-num` gains `UsdExact::quotient`**, one rounding of one exact quotient, because §5.1's `X × (E + Δ) ÷ E` and trading spec §8.1's `B × q ÷ Q` both need more places than a `Usd` holds before the division. (6) **R2's readings of the fold.** The dispatch for R2 used item 1's split, which puts the risk day, universe changes, and the profit stop in R2. R2 follows item 5, which is later and binding. (a) **A fill is not a sane quote.** §5.6 arms, latches, and clears a hard breach on a sane quote. A fill quotes nothing: E moves by the fill's price against the last mark, and the mark stays where it was. `ref.py` counts fills, which lets one flash print latch a limit on the next fill, against DEC-63. The crate follows the spec, no reference case changes (checked by regenerating with the fix), and the `ref.py` fix is backlogged. (b) **The risk clock is whole seconds and monotone** (§5.2). An instant between two seconds is `invalid_input`, at opening and at every step, because rounding it either way could end a wait up to a second before §5.6 lets it. A step at the previous instant folds with no time passing: the stub's "at or before" wording would refuse the first step of MC-R05 to MC-R08, MC-R14, MC-R15, and MC-R20. A step before it is `clock_went_backwards`. A refused step leaves the state exactly as it was, because the step runs on a copy. (c) **Staleness is guarded until R3.** A held instrument's mark reaching `mark_max_age_s` with no counted sane mark, or a counted mark failing its checks while held, is `unimplemented`, never a step without `stale_mark`. An expiry is judged by what was held over the interval, so it stands when the step's own sale leaves the book flat, as in `ref.py`. A book flat for the whole interval never goes stale. (d) **E = A + cash + qty × mark**, where cash is sale proceeds less purchases, the opening cost included. That is §5.1's A + realized − basis + market value with the basis split cancelled out. `ref.py`'s post-loop lift reset is `lift_s = 0` whenever the rung is not lifting (the #124 handover's items 4 and 5). (e) **Only a `scale_sizes` rung lifts on its lift level** (§5.5). A latched `exits_only` or `flatten_and_pause` rung lifts only on acknowledgment (§5.8), whatever the drawdown does. (f) **A `daily_loss` restriction's mode is the mandate's `daily_loss_action`.** `flatten_and_pause` pauses (§5.4) and fires the agent's kill switch; `Restriction::mode` alone says `exits_only`. (g) **Crypto counts wall seconds** for its lift delay, active time, and staleness, whatever the caller's clock says. Confirmation and the hard wait count wall seconds for both classes (§5.2, §5.6). (h) **Refused at opening:** a negative inherited loss, which would lower the floor (`invalid_input`). H opens at the allocation, which the schema makes positive, and in R2 only rises. **Refused at a step:** a sale of more than the agent holds, which would be a short sale (rule 12). (i) **No MC-R case passes on R2 alone.** Every equity case's base has a `profit_stop` goal, and every crypto case opens with `risk_day_started`, both R3's. With the profit stop stubbed out locally, MC-R01 to MC-R04, MC-R18, and MC-R19 match every expectation. | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | One slice per family keeps each review to one sitting. The risk-day reading is §5.4's sentence and nothing more: no stored length can disagree with the bounds, and the year-tiling test is the independent oracle. | One PR for all three families: the R fold alone is the crate's largest safety-critical piece. Opening L and R now with their tests still pending: it would add code that no live test covers. |
| DEC-168 | **Stream L's upstream sources for the tracer, each tests first: the instrument snapshot and latest quote in `mandate-alpaca` (E7-8), the agent-stream payload schemas (E7-9), and the control-stream schemas and their `JournaledFact` mapping (E7-10)** (the coordinator's rulings on [#171](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/171) of 2026-09-28, items 2 and the scope addition, which assigned this id; claim #171). Items 1 to 8 are E7-8's; E7-9 and E7-10 add theirs in their own PRs. (1) **Two reads, neither a `BrokerRequest`.** `TradingClient::asset` reads `GET /v2/assets/{symbol}` on the paper host (the allowlist's twelfth row, a `GET` and nothing else), the symbol written by `symbol_segment`, the same rule the positions path uses, so a pair is sent as `BTCUSD`. `DataClient::latest_quote` reads the data host. The executor's vocabulary is stream K's and is unchanged. (2) **The data host is kept apart by type.** A `QuoteRequest` has private fields and is built only from an instrument: `/v2/stocks/{symbol}/quotes/latest?feed=iex` for an equity (the paper profile's IEX feed, trading-domain spec §4.2, FR-2.7) and `/v1beta3/crypto/us/latest/quotes?symbols={base}%2F{quote}` for a pair. It goes only through a `DataTransport`, which cannot be handed an `HttpRequest`, and a `TradingTransport` cannot be handed a `QuoteRequest`, so neither host can be sent the other's request. ES-23 compiles in these two hosts and no other. `AlpacaPaperHttp` implements both traits with the same paper credentials. (3) **Numbers.** The data host sends prices and sizes as JSON numbers, so the read takes each number token's own text through `serde_json`'s `raw_value` (as `mandate-marketdata` does, no new dependency), refuses a string, a `null`, an exponent and more than 9 places, and parses with `mandate-num`; never an `f64`. The asset record's order constraints arrive as strings and follow `wire`'s rules, so a JSON number there is `float_number`. (4) **Every failure refuses** (`AGENTS.md` rule 3), as a `ReadError` with a stable code: `absent` (a `404`, a missing or `null` quote, a pair missing from `quotes`), `other_instrument` (the answer names another symbol), `one_sided` (a zero price on either side, which `Price` cannot hold and no mark or collar can use), `stale` (older than the caller's bound), `ahead_of_clock` (stamped after the clock's now), `overloaded` (a `429` or a `5xx`), `unexpected_status` (any other status, such as a `403` for a feed the account may not read), and the wire and transport codes. A crossed or locked quote is kept as sent: whether it is sane is its reader's (spec §4.1, §8.2; the builder's `CrossedQuote`, the gate's `SaneQuote`). A quote is read whole before its age is judged, and nothing is retried inside a read. (5) **Staleness bounds are the caller's.** `latest_quote(instrument, max_age)` and `AssetSnapshot::current(now, max_age)` take them, because the data profile's staleness threshold (§4.2) and §3.1's daily refresh are configuration; the crate holds no threshold of its own. Age is whole nanoseconds against the injected clock, and an age of exactly `max_age` is current. An asset snapshot is stamped with the clock's now when it is read. (6) **The asset fields read** are §3.1's that the gate, the eligibility floor and the executor use: the asset id, the symbol, the class (`us_equity` or `crypto`; any other is `wrong_type`), the exchange (§3.1's five codes, `OTC` and `CRYPTO`; any other is `Other`, which §3.2 item 2 makes ineligible), the status (`active` or `inactive`; any other is `wrong_type`), `tradable`, `fractionable`, the `ipo` and `ptp_no_exception` attributes (an equity must carry the `attributes` array, since its absence cannot say "not an IPO"), and the three order constraints, required for a pair and optional for an equity. The fields §3.1 marks "loaded; unused in v1" are not read, and the exit tier and the liquidity figures are not in the asset record, so they stay with their owners. (7) **Tests first** (DEC-77). The tests PR carries the request layer live (the allowlist row, `symbol_segment`, `QuoteRequest`, `DataTransport`), because it is the host boundary and has no stub to wait on, and the reads as stubs answering `ReadError::Unimplemented` under 21 tests `pending E7-8`. Every refusal case reads a good answer first, so an implementation that always refuses (`absent`, `stale`, `one_sided` or `not_json`) passes none of the 21, and every reading case asserts exact values. The fixtures are hand-built from Alpaca's documented shapes, because no paper credentials were available, and each says so in its README row; the manual paper run records the real ones. (8) **Not journaled here.** These reads are reference data, not broker exchanges on the order path, so this crate records no `BrokerExchangeRecorded` for them; whoever folds a snapshot into the effective-dated `ins` or takes a mark records what it used | Accepted (agent, reversible engineering, DEC-79; the coordinator's rulings on #171) | The coordinator placed both sources in `mandate-alpaca`, and the type split keeps the order path's allowlist exactly as strict as before while adding the one host ES-23 already permits | The quote in `mandate-marketdata` (against the ruling, and a layer-6 crate the shell would read quotes from beside its historical downloads); one transport trait for both hosts (a trading request could be dialled at the data host); a staleness constant in the crate (configuration the spec leaves to the profile); reading the numbers through `f64` (ES-23) |
| DEC-169 | **`ValidationContext` from journaled state: a pure fold with refusing defaults** (E10-1; the coordinator's rulings on [#171](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/171) and on [#124](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/124), which assigned this id; claim #124). (1) **Shape.** `ValidationContext::from_journal(&Mandate, ContextArgs, facts)` in `mandate-spec`'s `context` module folds a closed `JournaledFact` enum in journal order, with no dependency on `mandate-journal` (DEC-128 item 1 holds). The enum has twelve variants: `AccountSnapshot`, `ConnectionEstablished`, `ConnectionRevoked`, `DisclosureAccepted`, `AgentVersionActive` (`AgentDeployed`, or `MandateVersionApplied` for a deployed agent), `UniverseChanged`, `AgentStopped`, `AgentFlat`, `ModelRegistered`, `ModelWithdrawn`, `MandateVersionCreated` (each recorded path's source), and `MandateConfirmed` (the confirmed paths). The draft's provenance is folded from those last two, as the coordinator ruled on #124 and again on #255, and is not an argument. The draft's agent, its connection, the validation date, the workspace membership, the instrument groups, and the eligibility failures come in `ContextArgs` from their owners. Mapping journal records to facts is stream L's (E7-10, DEC-168), not this stream's. (2) **A missing fact refuses.** No equity snapshot means equity 0 (V-002). No registration means an empty registry (V-007), never `None`, because `None` means "not checked". No membership means zero users (V-024). No `MandateVersionCreated` for the draft's version means every envelope path is unconfirmed (V-020, item 5). A connection with no `ConnectionEstablished`, or one revoked since, is `live` (V-001 on a paper mandate), and a revocation also drops that connection's equity snapshot, so V-002 sees 0 until a new snapshot (#262 round 1). A live mandate on an unknown connection is refused by the platform policy's `environments: [paper]` (§4.3) rather than by a V-rule. The property the coordinator asked for is `with_every_fact_the_base_is_valid_and_without_any_one_it_is_refused_by_its_rule`. It covers the facts that have a refusing default and asserts the rule each absence must fire (equity V-002, environment V-001, registration V-007, disclosure V-005, membership V-024, provenance V-020). A fact that only adds (another agent's allocation, a claim, a retirement) cannot be known to be missing, so its absence is indistinguishable from its not having happened; the payload owner's completeness guarantees are what cover it. (3) **Readings.** Only facts about the draft's connection count toward equity, other allocations, claims, the environment, and the loss carry. The latest snapshot, environment, and version win. The draft's own agent is never "other". An `AgentStopped` retires an agent only when it names the connection of the agent's latest version; a stop naming another connection leaves the allocation and the claims counted, because rule 3 never lets an ambiguous record add V-002 headroom (#255 round 1). A retired agent holds no allocation until a later version redeploys it, and an `AgentFlat` after a stop that did not retire it releases nothing (#255 round 2, n1). Its claims (its latest version's pinned instruments and its working universe, which only `UniverseChanged` changes) stay until an `AgentFlat` after the retirement (trading spec §7.1). An `AgentFlat` for a live agent changes nothing. An agent with `UniverseChanged` facts and no `AgentVersionActive` has no known connection, so its admitted instruments count as claimed on every connection (V-006 fails closed, #262 round 1). The loss carry sums the connection's retirements at most `LOSS_CARRY_DAYS` = 90 days before the validation date, both ends included; a retirement dated after the validation date does not count (mandate spec §5.7, #255 round 1). A negative loss adds nothing rather than lowering the carry. The previous version is the draft agent's latest active version. A sum `Usd` cannot hold is `Err(Num(Overflow))`, the only error. (4) **Tests first** (DEC-77): the stub returns `SpecError::Unimplemented`, and 17 tests in `tests/context.rs` are `pending E10-1`. They include a property against an oracle that decides each quantity by its last deciding step rather than by replaying forward. A no-op fold, a constant context, and a fold that skips the checks each pass none of the 17. A throwaway implementation passes all 17, and each of 27 bugs planted in it fails at least one test. (5) **An envelope path the draft's provenance does not mention is unconfirmed** (the coordinator's ruling on #252, minor 4). `ProvenanceMap::at` reads an unmentioned path as `user_entered` and confirmed, and V-020 and V-022 read only the entries present, so a document with no entries passed both, even with `admission: auto`. `from_journal` therefore takes the draft. It reads the draft's own version's latest `MandateVersionCreated` for each recorded path's source, confirms a path only when the latest `MandateConfirmed` for that version names it or an ancestor of it, and adds a `user_entered`, unconfirmed entry for every part of the document no entry covers. The walk stops at a covered path, descends only where an entry lies below and the node has children, and never adds the system fields; a recorded path below a scalar or an empty array or object names nothing, so that node itself is added unconfirmed, and a path past the end of an array leaves each existing item uncovered, so each is added (#262 round 1, the blocker; an in-module property checks every document leaf ends up covered). V-020 then fires on each added entry, and an `auto` under one fires V-022 (`auto_with_no_provenance_is_v022`). `validate` itself and the MC-V cases, which state their provenance, are unchanged. No production caller may use `validate` or `ValidatedMandate::new` except through `from_journal` until this lands (backlog) | Accepted (agent, reversible engineering, DEC-79; option 1 accepted by the coordinator on #124) | A pure fold keeps the rules below the gate and the crate graph unchanged, and refusing defaults make a missing source a refusal rather than a skipped check (AGENTS.md rule 3) | The constructor in `mandate-shell` (rule-bearing defaults in the shell, against the #171 ruling's item 2); `mandate-spec` parsing journal payloads (reverses DEC-128 item 1 and waits on unowned schemas); an error for every missing fact (callers would learn to supply placeholders); `None` for an unknown registry or environment (skips V-007 or V-001) |
| DEC-171 | **The product's public name is Owlhead, at owlhead.ai; "Mandate" stays the internal name.** Anything public-facing says Owlhead and links owlhead.ai: the web app, the marketing site, outreach, and disclosures. The repository, the crates (`mandate-*`), internal identifiers, and the working documents keep "Mandate", and nothing is renamed. Legal and compliance text naming the product stays a founder decision (DEC-79) | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-28) | The founder bought owlhead.ai as the product's public name and home, and said "no need to rename anything". The codename is load-bearing across every crate, PR, and document in flight, and it does not need to match the brand | Renaming the repository and crates to Owlhead: churn across every open stream for no user-visible gain |
| DEC-172 | **E10-3's readings of §9: change classification as code** (stream F, claim [#124](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/124); DEC-128 items 11 and 12, DEC-121, DEC-43). (1) **Pinned instruments compare by whole entry.** An entry the old list does not hold is an added instrument and increasing, including one whose `asset_id` stayed while its symbol or asset class changed; only removals are reducing; the same entries in another order are neutral, a reading that never decides step-up because a pure reorder is unreachable between valid versions (V-009 rejects an unsorted list, MC-V14). The reference implementation compares ids only and would call a changed symbol neutral; no case covers it, and §9.2's row names adding and removing only, so its last row (every other change is increasing) decides. (2) **`pinning_switch` reads the change's own paths.** Its `paths` must be `changed_paths` of the same two documents; any other list, shorter or longer, is `false`, the answer that needs step-up, so no caller can make a change reducing by leaving a path out. (3) **`classify_autonomy` of two identical rule sets is `Neutral`.** `classify` never asks, because nothing under `/autonomy` changed. (4) **The `change` harness arm compares all five members, and `step_up_required` may be absent only from a case that says `invalid`,** where step-up must be `false` (an invalid change is refused, not stepped up); a missing member anywhere else fails its case. `every_change_case_passes_and_fails_on_each_edited_expectation` edits and drops each member of each case. (5) **`null` and absent.** A `null` or absent maximum is unbounded, so a maximum removed is increasing and one set from `null` is reducing; a `null` or absent minimum is no minimum; a ladder `factor` that becomes `null` has risen; `crypto_stop_limit_offset` `null` means unset, so newly set is increasing and cleared is reducing; `end_date` `null` means no end. Decimals and integers compare by value through `SchemaDec`'s order, dates by text, which the schema's `YYYY-MM-DD` pattern makes date order. A value of the wrong type at a numeric path is a `ParseError`, never a guess. (6) **A new rule set that repeats a rule id is increasing.** §9.2's shapes identify a rule by its id, so none can be read off a repeated one; an id repeated only in the old set changes the count of kept ids, which the reorder check already calls increasing. (7) **MI-2 is the risk state's, not classification's.** "An applied allocation change never triggers or lifts a limit" is §5.1's, pinned under E6-4 in `tests/risk.rs`; `classify` reads two documents and no state, so it can trigger or lift nothing. Its counterpart here is a property: an allocation-only change is classified by its direction alone, whatever else the document says. (8) **`Invalid` wins, and needs no step-up.** A changed `environment` or `connection_id` is `Invalid` whatever else changed, including the pinning switch (which cannot contain those paths anyway), and `step_up_required` is `class == RiskIncreasing`, so `false`. (9) **The single-comparison shapes.** "One value changed so it matches less (more) often" is read for `lt`, `lte`, `gt`, and `gte` by the decimal moving, and for `in` and `not_in` by strict set containment; `eq`, `ne`, a changed field or operator, and a set that neither contains nor is contained by the old one are increasing. A rule whose condition is unchanged may only make its `then` stricter; a rule whose condition changed must keep its `then`. (10) **Paths come from the canonical values the version hashes,** walked object by object with arrays compared whole (DEC-128 item 11), so a reported path is exactly one that changes the hash; each path's verdict reads the typed field where the row has one and the canonical text otherwise. (11) **Scope.** The harness interprets all 48 family-C cases and the status PR marks every one that passes: the claim assigns MC-C01 to MC-C48 to E10-3, and the backlog's acceptance clause (MC-C01 to MC-C35) is its floor, not its ceiling. The diff view and the step-up flow itself are later stories. (12) **Readings the implementation adds** (E10-3's implementation PR). An absent member and a `null` one are different canonical values and different hashes, so a change between them is a changed path; every schema member is required, so this arises only between goal types (`accumulate`'s nullable `max_avg_price` against a goal without it), where `/goal/type` already makes the change increasing, so it can add a path to the list but never move the verdict. The reference's `dict.get` reads the two as equal. A same-set reordering of `asset_classes` is neutral, as pinned instruments are under item 1; the reference calls it reducing, and neither asks for step-up. Approvers compare as an ordered list, so reordering them is the "approvers" change §9.2 calls increasing. A mandate whose public fields no longer match the document it was parsed from has no canonical form, so `classify`, `changed_paths`, and `pinning_switch` refuse it (`diverged`) rather than classify fields the version would not hash. (13) **The `not_in` shapes are pinned in-module.** No `tests/change.rs` test and no MC-C case reaches a `not_in` rule (the MI-11 property generates `in` lists only), so reading `not_in` like `in`, or giving it no direction, passed every one of them and survived the mutation gate. `src/change/tests.rs` pins both directions for `auto` and for `ask` and `deny`, in the implementation PR, as #263 closed its survivors with module tests: `tests/` still changes only by deleting pending markers (DEC-77). | Accepted (agent, reversible engineering, DEC-79) | The spec states each row's direction but not how an implementation reads an absent value, a repeated id, or a caller's path list; each reading here takes the side that asks for step-up when the spec is silent (AGENTS.md rule 3) and agrees with the reference implementation on all 48 cases | Compare pinned instruments by id (a symbol or class change would pass as neutral); trust the caller's path list in `pinning_switch` (a shortened list could make a change reducing); make `Invalid` require step-up (it is refused, so step-up would imply it could be approved); read MI-2 as a classification property (classification holds no state to trigger or lift) |
| DEC-174 | **E7-9 cannot be tested first yet: journal spec v0.4 closes no agent-stream payload schema and its test vectors hold no agent-stream event, so no agent-stream schema is registered until a journal spec change supplies both** (stream L, claim [#171](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/171); DEC-168; the acting backend coordinator's dispatch in the coordinator log on [#165](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/165), which assigned this id). (1) **What is written.** `mandate-runtime`'s `step.rs` journals eleven agent-stream event types: `AgentModeChanged`, `KillSwitchActivated`, `OwnerExitRequested`, `ObservationRecorded`, `ModelOutputRecorded`, `DecisionMade`, `IntentProposed`, `ApprovalRequested`, `ApprovalResponded`, `ApprovalTimedOut` and `ApprovalCanceled` (journal spec §9, agent stream). `mandate-executor` writes only the account stream, so it adds none. Nothing writes the agent stream's `StreamOpened`, which §2 requires at seq 1. Driven through every path that journals them and wrapped in a valid envelope, each of the eleven is refused by `Draft::parse` as `unknown_schema`. (2) **No source closes their schemas.** §9 lists each event's *key* payload fields, which DEC-131 item 25(h) already reads as not closing the schema, and `journal.yaml` (version 3) holds five chain vectors, all on the account stream. With no vector and no closed field list, any registered schema would be invented, so E7-9 registers none and writes no test for any of the eleven. An unregistered event stays `unknown_schema`, which refuses the append, so nothing the runtime would act on is journaled (`AGENTS.md` rule 3), and DEC-157 item 7 stands. (3) **Where the runtime's payloads contradict the spec**, each a ruling for the spec change rather than for `mandate-journal`: empty strings where §4.2 requires `null` (`DecisionMade.reason_code` on an allow, `ApprovalResponded.step_up` on a denial, and `OwnerExitRequested`'s `bid`, `bid_size`, `floor`, `user` and `step_up` when nothing was confirmed); instants written as risk-clock seconds, an integer in `ApprovalRequested.deadline` and decimal text in `ObservationRecorded.at`, `ModelOutputRecorded.as_of` and `expires_at` and `ApprovalResponded.at`, where §4.4 admits integers only for counts and durations and §4.7 and the `FillApplied` vector write a timestamp string; `ObservationRecorded.data` and `ModelOutputRecorded.content` written inline, where §9 names an artifact and a content hash; `IntentProposed` as `instrument`, `side`, `qty`, `limit` and `purpose`, where the `IntentReceived` vector's intent fields are `intent_id`, `agent_id`, `instrument_id`, `side`, `type`, `tif`, `qty`, `limit_price` and `purpose`; key fields §9 lists and the runtime does not write (`DecisionMade`'s outputs used, model weights, clips applied and combined conviction; `ModelOutputRecorded`'s content hash, direction, conviction, confidence, horizon and `ignored` reason; `ApprovalRequested`'s content shown; `ApprovalResponded`'s responder role, step-up evidence parts and separation-of-duties result); and members §9 does not list (`AgentModeChanged.lifecycle`, from DEC-131 item 25(h); `KillSwitchActivated.subject` and `mode_event`; `OwnerExitRequested.bid_size` and `floor`). The agent `StreamOpened`'s subject fields are unfixed too: the registered schema admits only `stream_type: account`. (4) **The path (Proposed; the founder approves the vectors, as their file requires).** A journal spec change closes the eleven schemas and the agent `StreamOpened`, rules on every item in (3), and adds one agent-stream vector per event in test vectors version 4, sequencing the approval rows with M7's spec PR (claim [#213](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/213)), which extends them. E7-9's tests PR then follows DEC-77 against those vectors, and any runtime change the rulings need is stream I's. (5) **Found, outside E7-9:** the executor's account-stream drafts fail the registered schemas and vectors too. Every draft carries an integer `risk_clock` (`batch.rs`), where the `FillApplied` vector has a timestamp string and the `IntentReceived`, `GateDecided` and `OrderSubmitted` vectors have none; `IntentReceived` writes `agent`, `kind`, `instrument` and `limit` where its vector has `agent_id`, `instrument_id`, `limit_price`, `type` and `tif`; and `OrderSubmitted` omits its optional members rather than writing `null` (§4.2). The shell's envelope writes empty `config_refs`, so every event that requires `man` or `mod` would also be `missing_config_ref` | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) for items 1 to 3 and 5; item 4 Proposed (founder: the vectors) | A schema the spec does not fix would make the journal accept whatever the runtime writes today, contradictions included, and a later ruling would then have to upcast stored events (§8) rather than correct a draft. Refusing the append is the state the tracer is already in, and it adds no risk | Derive each schema from §9's key fields and the runtime's payloads (invents the field list and takes the code's side of every contradiction in (3), which `AGENTS.md` forbids); register an open payload for the agent stream (admits any member, so it checks nothing); stub a registry API with no test behind it (a surface nothing pins) |
| DEC-175 | **An approved pull request merges when the coordinator names its reviewed head and the `merge` workflow finds CI green on that head** (carries out DEC-79; amended 2026-09-30 after the independent review of #313, which found that a label alone was not bound to a commit). On the step-3 PASS of the [ship playbook](../../.cursor/skills/mandate-mode/playbooks/ship.md), from a reviewer on a different model (DEC-79), the coordinating agent writes `Coordinator-approved-head: <full sha>` into the pull request's description and adds the `coordinator-approved` label. The label is the act of approval: GitHub lets only people and apps with triage access or above apply it. The line binds that approval to one commit by convention, not by a GitHub permission, since a pull request's author can edit its description; builders here have no pull request tool and the coordinator writes the descriptions, so a builder's tooling can at worst drop the line or leave a stale sha, and both refuse the merge. The line stands alone inside the description's body, outside any code fence, exactly once; the key is case-sensitive, the sha is all 40 hex digits in either case, and a leading `- ` or `* ` is allowed. `.github/workflows/merge.yml` runs on the label, on a description edit, when a pull request leaves draft, on every completed `ci` or `web` run, and on a half-hourly sweep of every labelled pull request, so that an event GitHub drops never strands an approval. It calls `.github/scripts/merge-approved.sh`, which re-reads everything from GitHub, never from the event, reading again up to three times while GitHub is still computing mergeability, and merges only when all of these hold: the pull request is open, not a draft, targets `main`, and carries the label; its description names exactly one approved sha, and that sha is its current head; GitHub reports it `MERGEABLE`; and the latest `ci` run from a `pull_request` event on that head succeeded, plus the latest `web` run when it touches `web/`, since DEC-112 lets `fast` and `full` skip the web checks. The merge names that head, so a push after the reads makes GitHub refuse it, and a push before them moves the head off the approved sha. The commit message is the description's marked body with any `Co-authored-by` line removed; an empty one refuses the merge rather than fall back to GitHub's default (ship step 5). The title and the description's wording are read at merge time and are not bound by the approval; they change only the commit's wording, never which commit merges. The workflow runs from the default branch and never checks out the pull request's code. Two approved pull requests may merge close together; one left conflicting is skipped as not mergeable, and needs `main` merged in and a new approval. `xtask`'s tests drive the script against a stub `gh` for every refusal. The founder turned off "Require review from Code Owners" on the `main` ruleset, 2026-09-30, so that the workflow's `GITHUB_TOKEN` can merge; code owners are still requested for review. Merges by that token start no other workflow, so `nightly` stays the check on `main` itself | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79, on the founder's go-ahead of 2026-09-30 to the coordinator's proposal) | The founder asked how to stop being the merge bottleneck and answered "Yes do everything you need to do" to this plan; the wording and mechanism are the coordinator's, and the founder can amend or revert them. DEC-79 already lets agents merge their own work, but the coordinating cloud session has a read-only `gh` and no merge action, so every approved pull request waited for the founder. It can edit a description and add a label, and the description line binds the approval to one commit without a job that has to revoke it | A label alone, with a job that removes it on push (the first version: GitHub may cancel a queued job, so an approval could outlive the push it should end); a commit status per approved sha (sha-bound, but this session cannot post one); GitHub auto-merge (needs `allow_auto_merge`, uses GitHub's default squash message with the trailers, and is not bound to a reviewed head); a bot account or app token with bypass (a new credential to hold and rotate); the founder keeps merging by hand (the bottleneck this removes) |
| DEC-176 | **Standing delegation: agents accept spec and reference-case changes that only tighten a rule, or that resolve a gap by the reading that adds no risk** (extends DEC-79). An agent may accept, as "Accepted (agent, DEC-176)", a change to `docs/specs/`, a schema, or a reference-case YAML when it (1) makes a rule stricter, or (2) closes a gap or contradiction between the spec and the code, or between two specs, by choosing the reading that adds no risk (AGENTS.md rule 3); and (3) it weakens no safety invariant and no `AGENTS.md` non-negotiable. Such a change goes through the full DEC-79 path: tests first, an independent review on a different model, and green CI; for a reference-case change, the YAML, `cargo xtask refcases --write`, and the reference implementation's checks in the same pull request. Where two readings are open and neither is clearly the safer, the agent takes the more conservative one, records it here as Proposed, and continues (rule 9).
