Still reserved for the founder (DEC-79): live trading, live credentials, or real money; spending; legal and compliance text; and weakening any safety rule. Making the spec agree with more permissive code is weakening, and stays the founder's. The founder reviews after the fact and can revert | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79, on the founder's go-ahead of 2026-09-30 to the coordinator's proposal) | The founder answered "Yes do everything you need to do" to the coordinator's proposal of this delegation. Most founder-waiting items were spec gaps whose safe reading was not in doubt (DEC-174's journal schemas, E6-8's open questions), and each held a stream for a day. Tightening and reconciling cannot add risk, and the DEC-79 review still checks each one | Every spec change waits for the founder: the bottleneck this removes; agents may change any spec rule: would let an agent weaken a safety rule, which DEC-79 reserves |
| DEC-178 | **How the `mandate-refcases` harness reads families G, F, and L** (DEC-77 stage 4, item 3; stream G, claim [#123](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/123), and stream F, claim [#124](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/124)). The YAML header says only "gate: the mandate limits of spec 5.3, in trading spec 9.1 order; every other check passes", "agent_flatten: the agent-scoped kill-switch plan", and "goal: goal completion for the given state"; these are the readings that turn each into a call on merged code. (1) **Where.** `crates/mandate-refcases/src/mandate/risk_gate.rs` interprets `kind: gate` through `mandate_risk::evaluate` and `kind: agent_flatten` through `mandate_risk::agent_flatten`; family L already runs through the suite's `goal` arm and `mandate_spec::goal::status` (E6-4), so it needs only its status rows. `crates/mandate-refcases/tests/mandate_gate_harness.rs` proves the two new arms read every member and compare every expectation. `crates/mandate-risk/tests/refcases.rs`, the crate-local copy whose own doc says it moves here, stays until a follow-up deletes it (backlog). (2) **The full gate, with [DEC-150](#decisions) item 1's `FULL_GATE_ONLY`.** The one entry, MC-G02, also pins the verdict, reason, and unreached `gross` and `gross_limit` values the case states, so an edit to the case fails at the entry instead of passing through it. (3) **"Every other check passes" is an environment in which they do**, so the case's own limits decide. The risk state is healthy at the case's `agent_equity`: high-water mark, day-start equity, and capital base all equal it, the size factor is 1, the mode is normal, and nothing is latched. The account is a margin account, active, in the intraday-margin regime, with equity, maintenance excess, and every buying power at the fixture's `validation_context_defaults.account_equity_usd` (25000). It holds exactly what the agent holds (positions, market values, working orders), so check 7's account 1× bound is evaluated against a real account rather than an empty one. The instrument is a NASDAQ US equity: active, tradable, unhalted, whole-share, plain (not an ETP), status feed current, median dollar volume $90M. Its prior close and its quote (bid = ask) are the proposal's limit price at `now`, with 5-minute volume 1,000,000 and 20-day ADV 10,000,000. The conduct state is empty, and the proposal is a plain day limit order in the regular session with no fee reservation. The gate configuration is the trading-domain `test_default`, plus the five settings it does not carry at `mandate-risk`'s own `test_default_config` values. A case proposing an instrument the base does not pin, or anything but a US equity, fails in the harness, because check 2 still owes crypto's USD-pairs rule to E6-10. (4) **Positions and orders.** The cases state dollars only. Each `positions_mv` entry becomes a one-share position with that market value, since the limits read market values, and a sell's own instrument holds exactly the sell's quantity. Each working opening order is the agent's own: a buy, one share open, opening, not protective, submitted on the New York date of `now`. They take client order ids 1 to n in listed order, and the proposal takes n + 1. Group names become group ids by their rank in text order. (5) **The purpose is assigned, never injected** (DEC-129 item 19). The harness picks the `Origin` and side the purpose implies: open and increase are an order-builder buy, a discretionary exit an order-builder sell, a risk exit a risk-engine sell, an owner exit an owner close, and a protective order a protective leg. The gate's own assignment must then equal the case's `purpose`. (6) **The verdict and its reason are compared together with the checks.** A denial must have exactly one failing check, carrying the stated reason, and an allow must report all eight checks passed, in §9.1's order (item 11). Every `computed` figure the case states must be reported with that value, and a figure the gate did not report fails. A case states only the figures its title is about, so an unstated figure is not compared. (7) **`agent_flatten` names.** The fixture names client order ids and agents with strings, and the crate uses integers. Each order's id is the rank of its name among the case's names, so the plan's ascending ids are the names in text order, and the names must be unique. Agents are numbered by first appearance. (8) **The owner's bid.** `owner_confirmed_bid: true` requires a `confirmed_bid`, and `false` forbids one. `max_exit_offset` is required with a confirmed bid; otherwise it is zero, which no plan reads because the floor exists only for an owner's confirmed bid. No case sets an explicit floor price. The initiator is `risk_limit` or `owner`; any other value fails. (9) **The plan is compared whole.** The mode, the purpose, the cancels as an ordered list, and both endpoint flags are compared, and so is each sell with its pricing, floor, and remainder. An absent `floor_price` means no floor and an absent `remainder` means none, so a plan that adds either fails. A deferred sell's `until` must be `regular_session_open`, the only deferral `DeferredSell` means. (10) **Status.** MC-G01 to MC-G16 and MC-F01 to MC-F04 pass under E6-3, and MC-L01 to MC-L05 under E6-4. MC-G13, an allowed reopen, reaches checks 5 and 6; the harness held it pending while they failed closed (DEC-129 item 29), and it passes since E6-8 ([#311](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/311)) made them whole, so it is swept with the other fifteen, and `the_allowed_reopen_passes_the_conduct_checks_e6_8_made_whole` also denies it `close_window` at check 6 five minutes before the close. The rows land in their own pull request, because the spec guard keeps `status.toml` apart from code (ES-22), as #309 and #310 did for family C. (11) **An allow passes all eight checks.** With E6-8 merged, an allowed US-equity order is `Passed` at every check in §9.1's order, so the arm compares the whole list with that, and an allow cannot rest on a check the gate listed as not reached. MC-G13 is decided that way now, and so would MC-G02 be once restated: as a $1,300 position with no working order it is an allowed opening, an increase, and against that state the full gate allows it and the arm reports its `FULL_GATE_ONLY` entry expired (the backlog's MC-G02 row). (12) **The copied gate configuration is checked against the fixture.** `configs.test_default.gate` is in `fixtures/refcases/trading-domain.json`; the mandate fixture has no `configs`. The fixture's `price_floor`, `liquidity_floor_usd`, the five `collar` members, `opposite_fill_interval_s`, `order_to_fill.max` and `.min_orders`, `close_window_minutes`, and `legacy_pdt_equity_threshold` are compared by value with the twelve `GateConfig` fields they fill, after `DecStr` normalises the fixture's text (`"5.00"`, `"0.20"`), which the strict parsers refuse. Its other eight members (`data_profile`, `unexplained_403_threshold`, `crypto_stop_limit_offset`, `stop_watchdog_s`, `protective_replace_buffer_trading_days`, `bracket_partial_fill_timeout_s`, `max_unprotected_s`, `exit_ladder`) belong to other crates and are named so that the fixture's member list must match exactly. The remaining five fields are compared with `mandate-risk`'s `test_default_config` values. `GateConfig` is destructured whole, so a new field does not compile until it is compared. (13) **A failure starts with what it is about.** Every arm message starts with the member it compared (`verdict: `, `computed.gross: `, `sells[1].pricing: `), and the harness tests match a failure by that prefix rather than by substring, so a failure for another reason that mentions the member later (`the full gate's verdict and reason: …`) does not count. Sells and deferred sells are compared count first, then member by member in order, each struct destructured whole. The copied config and the prefixes are the #317 review's minor 1 and nit 2. (14) **Not compared: the gate's `pacing`.** E6-8 gives an allowed order a `pacing`, and no `gate` case states one. For an opening it is `None` by the gate's own contract, and exits' pacing is pinned by `mandate-risk`'s own tests (`hand.rs`'s `a_participation_cap_slices_and_never_denies` and `gate.rs`'s in-module collar and slice tests), so the arm reads no expectation for it. A group name without a rank is refused rather than read as group 0, which cannot happen for names the case itself lists | Accepted (agent, reversible engineering, DEC-79) | The header leaves the environment, the names, and the optional members open. Each reading drives the merged production code rather than a copy of `ref.py`, and none can let a case pass while part of it goes unread (DEC-85). Eight bugs planted in `mandate-risk` (concentration at `>=`, orders per day at `>`, a cooldown that ignores groups, a gross limit on account equity, a flatten selling the broker quantity, swapped flatten modes, cancelling every agent's orders, and the cancel-all endpoint) each fail at least one case. After E6-8, six more planted in checks 4 to 6 (the collar's buy ceiling measured down from the ask, the one-working-order rule counting the agent's orders in any instrument, each participation cap compared the wrong way, the close window inverted, and a close window that never denies) each fail MC-G13 or its close-window variant | An empty account, as the crate-local harness has, which leaves check 7's account bound untested; a position the size of the case's dollars at the limit price, which would make a position's quantity depend on an unrelated price; reading `max_exit_offset` as required everywhere, which would fail cases that never price a floor; comparing sells as a set, which would miss a reordered plan |
| DEC-179 | **How the family-N harness decides the three first orders (MC-N01, MC-N14, MC-N26) through `mandate_builder::classify`** (stream H at the coordinator's dispatch, stream J being idle; claim [#125](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/125); DEC-132 item 3, DEC-154). The arm is `crates/mandate-refcases/src/mandate/research.rs`; nothing is re-implemented. (1) **The policy is the patched base through the strict parse, unvalidated**, as family A's is (DEC-162 item 1): the admission cases state no validation context, and `classify` re-checks the rules it reads. (2) **Admission's reported ceiling must equal the parsed policy's `autonomy.admission`.** `FirstOrderFacts.admission_ceiling` is the overlay's effective admission, no family-N case states a policy, and the overlay is `PolicyOverlay::permissive()`, so the two must agree; a mismatch fails the case naming both, rather than one of them being ignored. `classify` reads the ceiling from the policy, so this check is what reads the reported one. (3) **The action is `admission_action` as stated, with `purpose` `open`, and `new_instrument` and `thesis_confidence` from admission's `FirstOrderFacts`**, as `ref.py`'s `admit` composes it (`dict(action, purpose="open", new_instrument=True, thesis_confidence=...)`). The action's `instrument` is taken as stated: in MC-N01 and MC-N26 it names `…0002` while the admitted thesis names `…0005`, which `ref.py` also keeps. No rule in `research_equity` or `research_equity_etp` reads `instrument`, so the outcome cannot turn on it (a fixture observation for the founder, not changed here). (4) **`unusual_input` must be `false`.** V-018 reserves the field until the input-drift detector ships, `ActionContext` has no place for it, and `ref.py` marks it unavailable; a `true` fails the case naming it, rather than being classified as if it were false. (5) **The classification is compared whole, as JSON**, the way this module compares journal rows: `decision` and `by`, plus `approvers_required` and `on_timeout` exactly when §6.4 gives the decision an approval, so an approval member stated or omitted wrongly fails. The decision's spelling is the harness's own. (6) **The oracle.** `every_first_order_fact_reaches_its_own_field` reads the built facts back through `Facts` with admission's facts set to `false` and a confidence no stated member holds, since the fixture's thesis confidence equals its `combined_score`; `an_unusual_first_order_input_fails_naming_it` covers item 4; and the existing doctoring tests now require every doctored member of the three cases to fail, with the pending exemption removed. Four harness plants were each caught: the confidence taken from `combined_score` and `new_instrument` fixed at `true` (by the facts test), the `unusual_input` check dropped (by its test), and the approval members never compared (by the three cases). Five production bugs, each seeded and reverted, were each caught: in `mandate-builder`, step 5 skipped, the ceiling labelled with the rule it overrode, and an ASK with no approval (all three cases each); in `mandate-research`, a renewal reported with `new_instrument` false (MC-N14 alone) and an `auto` ceiling reported (all three, through item 2). (7) **The status rows move in a status-only PR** stacked on the harness, as DEC-162 item 7 does for family A (ES-22) | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | Harness readings only: no production crate changes, and each reading makes a case fail sooner rather than pass on something unread. Following `ref.py`'s composition keeps the harness an independent reading of the reference rather than of the crate | Validate the policy (tests rules family N does not state inputs for); classify against a policy with admission's ceiling substituted in (composes an overlay step `classify` does not own, DEC-128 item 9); use the thesis's instrument for the action's (diverges from `ref.py` and leaves the stated `instrument` unread); ignore `unusual_input` (an unread member, DEC-85) |
| DEC-16 | **No external durable-execution engine.** Durable timers and long waits (an approval pending for hours, "run until December") are journal-backed: a timer is a journaled event in the Postgres journal, read back behind the `TimerSource` trait, and when it fires it enters the runtime as a journaled input like any other, so the gate and the executor decide from journal state. The journal stays the single source of truth (rule 5; HLD "Durability") | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-27) | State is event-sourced: every change is journaled first and state is the fold of the journal. A durable-execution engine keeps its own log of where each workflow is, which would make a second source of truth that can disagree with the journal after a crash (a timeout the engine fired against an approval the journal recorded), the class of bug the journal exists to prevent. No new infrastructure, no licence question | Restate (a second durable log; the server's licence would need checking); Temporal (a second durable log, a server cluster to run, no mature Rust SDK) |
| DEC-17 | Messaging between the processes of a workspace deployment. **Recommendation (2026-09-26, coordinator, for the start of M5 per ADR-0001 ES-20):** Phase 1 uses Postgres `LISTEN`/`NOTIFY` plus journal tailing on the workspace's Postgres, with intents and timers behind the in-process `IntentSink` and `TimerSource` traits; one process per agent deployment (DEC-08) and one Postgres per workspace deployment mean no new service and no message-bus-shaped abstraction in the core. NATS JetStream is reconsidered at M8, when the control plane and many deployments need fan-out. The E6-1 brief (stream I) is built on this recommendation | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-26): Postgres LISTEN/NOTIFY plus journal tailing for Phase 1; NATS JetStream reconsidered at M8 | The journal is already the durable log (ES-20); one dependency set on the edge; a single binary; NATS is Apache-2.0 and stays available | NATS JetStream now; Kafka / Redpanda; Redis Streams |

Evaluate first a declarative signal-expression layer (arithmetic and comparisons over the platform's indicators and data), which covers most custom-signal needs while every expression stays data the platform validates, bounds and replays (backlog, "Later") | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-27) | Safety and scope | Plug-ins in v1 |
| DEC-155 | **The M7 escalation v0 architecture** ([task brief](tasks/M7-escalation-v0.md), claim [#213](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/213)), for E8-1 to E8-3 and M7's CLI owner control, where no document says how a grant becomes an order, how the owner's answer reaches the runtime, or what step-up means before E9-4. (1) **A new pure crate, `mandate-approval`, at layer 1** (`safety_critical = true`, `pure = true`, `allowed_external = ["thiserror"]`, depending on layer 0 only, with a CODEOWNERS line): the request content and its canonical hash, response admission, step-up checks, re-validation as a comparison of values, the drift arithmetic, the ask budget, the closed `Notification` type, and quiet-hours delivery. Layer 1 keeps it from reaching `mandate-spec` or `mandate-risk`, so it can compare but never decide a limit. (2) **Owner input is journaled on the workspace control stream first**, as the new `ApprovalResponseSubmitted` and `OwnerCommandIssued`, and the runtime copies what is addressed to its agent with `causation_id` (journal spec §2); the control-stream `event_id` is the idempotency key, and `Input::ApprovalResponse` is retired. (3) **The request carries its content inline** in `ApprovalRequested` as a canonical object with its SHA-256 content hash, large parts (theses, model outputs) by artifact reference; a response must repeat the hash. (4) **Step-up v0 is `CliConfirm`**: the owner re-types the confirmation code `approvals show` printed for the content hash (for a kill switch, a code computed locally from the scope); it proves the owner read the content, does not authenticate against an identity provider, and is accepted only on a `paper` stream, by a match with no arm that admits `live`. Approve, resume, the owner Stop, acknowledge, owner exit, and the kill switch need it (mandate spec §6.1, PX-4 as accepted in DEC-135); skip and pause never do (PX-4, PX-7). The kill switch follows DEC-158 option (c), accepted by the founder: without valid step-up it still stops the agent and flattens as an automated flatten does (waiting for the regular session for equities, MC-F04); only privileges beyond that need valid step-up. (5) **The CLI is an untrusted surface and a one-shot writer**: it commits events, and the runtime re-runs every check that decides an outcome; on `Fenced` it retries with a new epoch for a new command, which DEC-131 item 18 forbids only for the runtime's long-lived writer of its own stream. (6) **The only v0 notification channel is `cli_inbox`**; email and chat are E8-4 | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | The runtime already asks, binds, times out, and cancels, but records a grant and acts on nothing (DEC-131 item 25(a)). Journaling the owner's answer before the runtime reads it keeps the journal the only channel (DEC-16, DEC-17, E6-1's "the journal is the channel"), survives a crash of either process, and gives the replay and duplicate cases a key. Phase 1 has no identity provider (E9-1 and E9-4 are M8, DEC-148) and mandate spec §6.4 requires step-up for live approvals only; making the paper-only method unusable for live by type means no live path can inherit it | A separate approval-service process (a second writer and a second state to reconcile, before M8 needs one); an in-process command channel that is not journaled (a crash loses the owner's answer and nothing records who answered); holding approvals and resume until E9-4 (M7 could not exit); a TOTP secret on the founder's machine (a new credential outside the vault, rule 7) |
| DEC-156 | **The M7 safe-default and re-validation readings** ([task brief](tasks/M7-escalation-v0.md)). (1) **Lateness is judged at the runtime's clock**: a response's effective time is the later of its `submitted_at` and the runtime's folded risk clock, and one at or after the deadline is refused, including one committed in time and processed after a restart. (2) **Admission, then re-validation, in one batch, in the brief's twelve-check order**: pending, timely, a human approver, delivered, the content hash, step-up; then the approver count and independence, the version, the mode and the instrument's restrictions, re-classification, the gate dry run, and drift, with every compared value journaled in the new `ApprovalRevalidated`; on `act`, `IntentProposed` carries exactly the bound fields and the executor's binding gate still decides. (3) **Drift band: 100 bp for `us_equity` and 200 bp for `crypto`**, the smallest collar aggressiveness `x` of each class in trading spec §9.6, applied symmetrically to the last folded `MarkUpdated` against the one recorded at the request, with no mark failing closed. (4) **Re-classification may not tighten**: `deny`, or `ask` by a different `DecidedBy` label, skips. (5) **Anti-fatigue bounds**: one pending risk-adding approval per agent (kept from DEC-131); at most 10 requests per agent per America/New_York risk day; no re-ask of an instrument the owner skipped until the next risk day or applied version; none after a timeout for one `timeout_s`; each suppressed ASK recorded on `DecisionMade`. (6) **A request is grantable only once delivered on some channel, and quiet hours govern push channels only** (the coordinator's round-1 ruling on #215): `cli_inbox` is a pull channel, delivered in the request's own batch and never suppressed, and `approvals list/show` list every pending request whatever the push state. (7) **Two approvers and independent approval are counted, never assumed**: the fold keeps a grant set per approval; a grant short of `approvers_required` is `counted`; until E8-6 and E9-5, an approval needing a second approver, in a one-person workspace, times out. (8) **Step-up freshness**: a grant, resume, Stop, or acknowledgment processed more than 300 s after its authentication is refused; an owner exit and a kill switch are judged at the moment the owner committed them, so a late read still applies them; pause needs none; an owner exit refused this way leaves every automated exit, the kill switch, and pause untouched. (9) **DEC-131 item 25(j) is closed**: a response is its own input, and admission reads the pending set minus the batch's own cancellations and the mode the step applies, so a grant in the same step as a cancelling tightening or kill switch is never acted on | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | Every reading only turns an act into a skip, so none adds risk (rule 3). E8-3 asks for drift re-validation and no document defines drift: reusing the collar's own `x` adds no new parameter and is never looser than the gate. Judging lateness at the owner's claimed time would let a delayed grant act on a moved market. The budget and suppressions only remove asks, which answers the approval-fatigue adversary without touching exits (rule 13) | Re-pricing a stale grant (mandate spec §6.4: no re-pricing in v1); drift as a new envelope field (a spec and schema change the owner would have to confirm, better proposed after the soak); no drift check beyond the binding gate's collar (the 20% passive band would let a stale buy rest far from the market the owner saw); unbounded re-asking (the fatigue the brief's adversary list names) |
| DEC-158 | **Should the owner's kill switch need step-up?** ([task brief](tasks/M7-escalation-v0.md), the coordinator's round-1 ruling on [#215](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/215)). Mandate spec §6.1 puts the owner's kill switch inside `owner_exit` ("The owner closes a position or triggers a kill switch", "The owner's instruction (step-up)"), and PX-4 (DEC-135) reads it that way. `AGENTS.md` rule 13 says the kill switch "is always available ... and does not depend on model state", and any step-up that can fail (a passkey that is not to hand, an identity provider that is down) would block it. **The options:** (a) **keep step-up for the kill switch**, as the spec says (the current design); (b) **no step-up for the kill switch**, as for pause; (c) **step-up that fails open for the stop and closed for the privilege**: a kill switch without valid evidence still stops the agent and flattens as an automated flatten does (waiting for the regular session for equities, MC-F04), while the owner-exit privileges (selling equities outside the regular session at a confirmed bid, DEC-58 and DEC-66) need valid step-up. That reading is consistent with the rest of the design, because a missing or failed authentication then never blocks a reduction and never unlocks anything a stranger could misuse beyond an ordinary flatten. **Mitigations kept under every option:** in v0 the step-up is the local `CliConfirm`, computed on the owner's host from the scope with no network, identity provider, runtime, or model state, so it fails only on a mistyped code; its freshness is judged when the owner committed the command, so a runtime that was down still applies it when it reads it; pause needs no step-up and stays available from the same CLI; and the broker's own controls remain outside the platform. **Recommendation: (c)** once E9-4 brings a step-up that can fail for reasons other than the owner's typing | **Accepted (founder, 2026-09-27): option (c).** The founder answered "c". A kill switch without valid step-up still stops the agent and flattens as an automated flatten does (waiting for the regular session for equities, MC-F04); only the owner-exit privileges beyond that (selling equities outside the regular session at a confirmed bid, DEC-58 and DEC-66) need valid step-up. The M7 spec PR amends mandate spec §6.1 to match | Weakening an authentication requirement is reserved for the founder (DEC-79), and the brief's first draft had exempted the kill switch on rule 13 alone (review finding 1 on #215). In v0 the question is almost moot, because `CliConfirm` cannot fail except by typing; it becomes real with E9-4's passkeys and the web and mobile Stop control (PX-3), which is why it is asked now | Exempting the kill switch without the founder (weakens §6.1 silently); step-up for pause too (PX-4 (c), rejected by DEC-135) |
| DEC-165 | **How the M7 tests and implementation PRs build `mandate-approval`** ([task brief](tasks/M7-escalation-v0.md), DEC-77 stage 3, claim [#213](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/213)). (1) **The tests stage is split by story and crate, and the MC-E harness waits for the cases.** Near 2,700 lines in one PR is past review size, so the pure crate comes in two: this PR is the crate's whole stub API with E8-1's tests (content, confirmation code, notification, quiet hours) and E8-2's ask budget, and E8-3's tests PR follows with admission, step-up (the kill switch's included), re-validation, drift and E8-2's lateness, with the check-table, clock-accumulator, principal, assertion-ledger, scaled-integer, field-comparer and kill-switch oracles. The runtime's grant path (retiring `Input::ApprovalResponse`, `OrderPlan::classify` returning the `DecidedBy` label, `Proposal.asset_class`, and the transition-table and causation-walker oracles for EI-1, EI-3, EI-6, EI-7, EI-8's same-step handoff and EI-12, with PB-1, PB-4, PB-13, PB-14's late read and PB-21) is the third tests PR, and the CLI's is the fourth, which also owns the kill switch's scope with the control-stream events: that an agent-scoped switch never uses cancel-all or close-position (`AGENTS.md` rule 13) and the `--connection` and `--workspace` rows (the coordinator's ruling on #240). The kill switch's confirmation code is the pure core's (`kill_switch_code`, a function of the scope and the control stream's head, DEC-155 item 4), with its stub and pending test in E8-3's tests PR. MC-E01 to MC-E31 are not in `mandate.yaml` until the M7 spec PR, and a harness cannot name a case that does not exist, so each hand test names the MC-E case and planted bug it stands for, and the harness comes with a tests correction after the spec PR. (2) **The crate has its own two-variant `AssetClass`**, because `mandate-domain` is at layer 1 too and a crate may depend only on strictly lower layers; the runtime maps one onto the other. (3) **The content object has exactly nine keys**: `action` (`instrument`, `asset_class`, `side`, `qty`, `limit`, `order_usd`, `purpose`), `trigger` (`mandate_version`, `decided_by`), `evidence` (`combined_score` with its §6.4 label, and `outputs`, each an event id, an artifact hash or null, and its author label), `risk_impact` (one `{field, value, cap}` per §6.3 figure: `order_usd`, `position_usd_after`, `gross_usd_after`, `bought_today_usd`, `drawdown`, `daily_pnl_fraction`, each beside the mandate's own cap), `reference_mark` (`price`, `seq`), `deadline` (a UTC timestamp), `default` (§6.4's sentence), `choices` (`approve`, `skip`), and `approvers` (`required`, `independent`); decimals are canonical strings, and a test walks every key at every nesting level. **The Trigger row's owner-written rule text waits for the spec PR (§6.4)**, which fixes where it sits; it then needs its own test and must be scanned apart from the platform's text by the advice-wording test, or excluded from it by construction (backlog, E8-1's follow-up). If the spec PR names them otherwise, the tests follow by a tests correction. (4) **Step-up is judged in the order of the brief's check-6 codes**: missing, stale, reused, method. The window is inclusive (evidence exactly 300 s old counts), and evidence authenticated after the moment it is judged at counts as stale, so a clock skew fails closed. (5) **The kill switch has its own entry point, `kill_switch`, whose answer has no refusing variant** (`AutomatedFlatten(reason)` or `OwnerExitPrivileges`), so DEC-158 option (c) holds at rung 1; the implementation returns no error from it, and the runtime must read any error there as `AutomatedFlatten`, never as a refusal. Pause, resume, the owner Stop, acknowledge and owner exit go through `owner_command`, where pause always applies. (6) **The ask budget's precedence is `budget`, then `skipped_today`, then `recent_timeout`**; the timeout window is `[t, t + timeout_s)`; any `MandateVersionApplied` after an owner skip lifts it; a suppressed ask is not an `ApprovalRequested` and so does not count. (7) **Quiet hours are `[start, end)` in America/New_York wall time**, a window whose end precedes its start spans midnight, and no quiet hours means send; `deliver_now` takes the same `RiskClock` as every other entry point, so the crate has one time base. (8) **A notification payload is exactly `{"subject": <approval id>, "text": "approval_needed"}`.** (9) **Test files are named by topic** rather than the brief's `tests/{hand,properties,refcases}.rs`: `content.rs` and `budget.rs` (hand tests), `properties.rs`, and `common/mod.rs` here; `admission.rs`, `revalidation.rs` and `grant_properties.rs` in E8-3's PR; `refcases.rs` with the MC-E harness after the spec PR. **The E8-1 and E8-2 implementation adds:** (10) **The confirmation code is the first eight hex digits of the content hash**: short enough to re-type, and bound to one content, while the runtime still compares the full hash (EI-14), so the code only proves the owner read this request. (11) **A value with no canonical form is `ApprovalError::Unrepresentable`** (a deadline outside `UtcNanos`'s range, an overflowing order value, a `seq` beyond 2^53 − 1, a clock outside the calendar's), and a caller reads it as "do not ask", never as a reason to act. (12) **Quiet hours are measured as minutes since the window opened, on a 24-hour face,** so a window that spans midnight is computed like any other and one whose end equals its start is empty (never quiet). **The approval-reference correction adds:** (13) **`ApprovalRef::of_requested_event` accepts only the journal's ULID shape** (26 characters of uppercase Crockford base32, the first at most `7`) and returns `ApprovalError::NotAnEventId` for anything else, so rule 6 holds at rung 1: no free text can reach a notification's `subject` (#250 review, major). The check is the crate's own, because `mandate-journal`'s is at layer 2; the first runtime or CLI caller proves the id is the `ApprovalRequested` event's own. **The E8-3 implementation adds:** (14) **Step-up age is a checked subtraction**: evidence whose age `i64` cannot hold (an `authenticated_at` or a judged-at time at either end of the clock) is `stale`, so it is never a panic, and never a wrapped age that looks fresh; the kill switch then answers `AutomatedFlatten(Stale)` and still returns no error (item 5). In-module tests in `src/stepup.rs` pin those extremes against an `i128` oracle, the gap the #240 review named. (15) **A drift the exact arithmetic cannot compute is outside the band**: an overflowing `× 10 000` skips as `drift`, as a missing mark does, and never errs or acts. (16) **The kill switch's code is the first eight hex digits of the SHA-256 of a canonical object** with the keys `confirms` (`kill_switch`), `control_head` (decimal text), `scope` (`agent`, `connection` or `workspace`) and `target` (the id, or null). The object's own `confirms` key keeps a kill-switch code from being any approval's code. Writing the head as text needs no canonical integer bound, so computing the code cannot fail. (17) **ES-13's 400-line split threshold counts non-test `src` lines**: files under `tests/` and `#[cfg(test)]` modules are excluded. That is the rule #236, #240 and #250 were held to (the coordinator's note on #240). | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | Each reading keeps the brief's invariants and only fixes a name, an order, or a boundary the brief left open; every boundary resolves toward a skip or a refusal except the kill switch, which option (c) resolves toward the stop and flatten. The split keeps each PR reviewable, as the brief's own implementation stage is split | One tests PR for all three crates (about three times the size); a harness that fails on missing cases (fails away from its stub, which `ci pending` rejects); `mandate-approval` at layer 2 to reuse `mandate-domain`'s `AssetClass` (the brief and DEC-155 fix layer 1); a kill switch through `owner_command` with a refusing variant (a refusal would be representable) |
| DEC-180 | **Earned autonomy is the product direction: the owner states a goal, the platform drafts the envelope, and autonomy grows only by owner-picked, bounded, expiring delegations** ([ADR-0003](../adr/0003-earned-autonomy.md); drafted by a Cursor agent at the founder's request of 2026-09-30 to research Meta Muse, SpaceXAI Grok Bot, and OpenAI Dots and recommend a direction). (1) Three moves: goal-first drafting (DEC-182), delegations and the autonomy dial (DEC-181), and Mandate as the money layer for general agents (DEC-183); the desk surfaces and the name follow (DEC-184). (2) Held back: `auto` proposed by the platform anywhere (Autopilot by default), delegations over admissions, perpetual delegations, the platform promoting trust on its own, live money for anyone (`AGENTS.md` rule 8, DEC-102), and user-supplied research agents (counsel and the DEC-99 forward-paper gate). (3) Unchanged: DEC-03 to DEC-07, DEC-47, DEC-97's envelope and strategy split, the gate, the ladder, the lifetime floor, journal before acting, kill switches, the eligibility floor, conduct controls, and `on_timeout: skip`. (4) Sequencing per [ADR-0003](../adr/0003-earned-autonomy.md) part 9: documents first, then the delegations spec change, then the code stories under DEC-77 once the open M7 and E6-2 pull requests merge; a web prototype on fixture data may start at once (DEC-200) | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-30; the recommended answer to the founder's question of that day). Autonomy grows only by owner-picked, bounded, expiring delegations; the platform never promotes trust and never proposes `auto` | Muse (Sentinel as sole permission authority, scoped one-time to perpetual approvals), Grok Bot (a chief-of-staff bot, Auto Review), and Dots (Custom Rules, 4,000+ app plugins) made scoped, growing trust the expected shape of an agent in September 2026, while brokers ring-fence money without supervising agents. Mandate's gate, journal, and AUTO/ASK/DENY rules already carry the weight; what is missing is a goal-first front door and a bounded way for trust to grow. Alpha Arena season 1 (four of six models lost 42 to 59 percent with no gate) is the evidence against letting the model own sizing and limits. Sources are linked in [ADR-0003](../adr/0003-earned-autonomy.md) | Autopilot by default; model-decided orders; automatic trust promotion; perpetual grants; general agents holding broker keys; doing nothing ([ADR-0003](../adr/0003-earned-autonomy.md), Alternatives rejected) |
| DEC-181 | **`autonomy.delegations`: bounded, expiring, owner-picked permissions that turn an `ask` into `auto` inside the envelope; V-022 restated so an approval card may offer delegation scopes** ([ADR-0003](../adr/0003-earned-autonomy.md) parts 2, 3, and 6; mandate spec v0.6 amendment, in its own spec-change pull request). Invariants, which bind the spec text (MI-26 to MI-29 there): (1) a delegation lifts only an `ask` from §6.2 step 4, and only from the rule it names or the default; it never lifts `deny`, never changes a limit or the gate, and the step-5 admission ceiling applies after it, so no delegation covers a newly admitted instrument in v1; (2) each is bounded by a condition, a maximum order value, a maximum order count, a maximum total order value, and a start and expiry at most 30 days apart, counted from journaled decisions; a spent or expired delegation stops matching and the decision is `ask`; (3) it lifts nothing while the agent's mode is stricter than `normal`, while a ladder rung is in effect, while a latched limit has not lifted, or while a kill switch in its scope is engaged, and a new version with any other risk-increasing change carries none over; (4) every delegation is `user_entered` and created by the owner with step-up as a confirmed version (§9.2: add or widen is risk-increasing, remove or narrow is risk-reducing, expiry is runtime); the platform may offer scopes on an approval card, none pre-selected, "Approve just this" first, each with Skip's visual weight; the compiler and templates still never propose `auto`; (5) an owner-connected agent can neither create, widen, nor pick one (DEC-141 items 1 and 2); (6) `on_timeout` stays `skip`; (7) each automatic decision under a delegation journals its ID. The autonomy dial (Supervised, Co-pilot, Autopilot) names what the owner's choices add up to; moving it opens the field with nothing pre-filled. The approval card shows counts only (asks approved, skipped, timed out, orders run under each delegation), no profit or outcome until counsel answers question 35 and questions 36 to 38 | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-30; the recommended answer to the founder's question of that day). V-022 is restated as the mandate spec change states it; the delegation code stories (E8-8) follow the DEC-77 sequence | Muse's scoped grants are the closest proven pattern, and its help centre notes the agent "learns over time which decisions need your sign off"; the difference here is that the owner, not the platform, widens trust, every step expires, and the gate's limits bind regardless. Bounding by value, count, and time turns a yes into a capped, auditable capability rather than a standing permission | A delegation that lifts any matching `ask`, including the owner's own exposure guards (lets a broad delegation quietly defeat a rule the owner wrote); lifting `deny` or the admission ceiling; no expiry; platform-suggested scopes pre-selected; counting only filled orders (under-counts: counting at decision is the conservative accumulator) |
