| DEC-200 | **`web/` code starts now; the Cursor coordinator owns `web/` and merges UI PRs** (the founder's decision of 2026-09-28, recorded by the Cursor coordinating session on the founder's instruction; stream W3, claims [#247](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/247) and [#246](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/246)). (1) **Timing.** Web code starts now rather than at M9. This amends DEC-134's "`web/` code stays at M9" and [ADR-0001](../adr/0001-engineering-setup.md) ES-01's "`web/` from M9", and ratifies the web stack now rather than at the start of M8 as PX-14 (c) planned ([DEC-135](#decisions)). [HLD §11](../HLD.md#11-technology)'s Next.js for the web app is ratified; React Native for mobile approvals is not decided here. (2) **Stack.** Next.js 16 with the App Router, React 19, TypeScript in strict mode, Tailwind CSS v4, shadcn/ui primitives (copied into `web/` as source), Motion ([motion.dev](https://motion.dev)) for animation, Vitest with Testing Library for component tests, and Playwright for end-to-end tests later. npm with a committed `web/package-lock.json`; the Node.js LTS release pinned in `web/.nvmrc`. Each framework has a row in [dependencies.md](../dependencies.md). (3) **Ownership and merging.** `web/` belongs to the Cursor coordinator ([coordination playbook](../../.cursor/skills/mandate-mode/playbooks/coordination.md) §2), and backend streams do not edit it. UI PRs are merged in the Cursor coordinating session after green CI and an independent review by an agent on a different model from the author's; DEC-79 applies unchanged. The Claude Code merge coordinator does not review or merge UI PRs (coordination §5). (4) **What binds the UI.** The [product-experience brief](../product/09-product-experience.md) and the specs: principles P1 to P10, PX-1 to PX-14 as DEC-135 and DEC-136 decided them, the §5 rules, and compliance text only as named placeholders (§5, rule 9). Where a spec and the UI disagree, the spec wins. (5) **No third-party analytics or session replay, and framework telemetry disabled** (`NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1`). (6) **Mock data until the APIs exist.** Until the backend APIs exist, the UI runs on typed mock data whose shapes match the specs, and every mock is labeled as a fixture. The UI never connects to a broker (rule 8). (7) **CI.** A change that touches only documentation and `web/` takes DEC-112's short path in `fast` and `full` (`.github/scripts/docs-only.sh`), so both required checks still report; the web checks (lint, typecheck, the Impeccable detector, a no-gradients check, tests, build) run in `.github/workflows/web.yml`, a separate workflow filtered to `web/` and not required. A change to `web/` and to anything else runs the full path. (8) **Visual direction: no gradients, flat colour, no generic AI aesthetics (founder, 2026-09-28); design work follows the vendored skills listed in `.cursor/skills/` and the Impeccable detector gates `web/` in CI.** The detector flags gradient text and AI-typical palettes but not a plain gradient, so `web.yml` also fails on any gradient in `web/src`. **Supersedes:** DEC-134's "`web/` code stays at M9"; ES-01's timing for `web/`; PX-14 (c)'s timing in DEC-135; and, for UI PRs only, coordination §5's rule that the Claude Code session merges for both sides. DEC-134's design work and the other PX decisions stand | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-28) | The founder decided on 2026-09-28 that Cursor builds the web UI now, in `web/`, and merges UI PRs itself; the Claude Code coordinator agreed to stay out of UI PRs and asked for a reserved DEC block (DEC-200 to DEC-249) and a directory the backend streams do not touch. The W1 brief and the PX decisions give the UI its rules, so the stack need not wait for M8. One owner, one directory, and one workflow keep UI PRs out of the backend merge queue and away from the safety-critical crates; the short path keeps a web-only PR from paying for the Rust suite, which DEC-112 and DEC-113 exist to save | Keep `web/` code at M9 (DEC-134, ES-01); ratify the stack at the start of M8 (PX-14 (c)); send UI PRs through the Claude Code merge queue (it asked not to, and they would wait behind safety-critical merges); run the web checks inside `cargo xtask ci fast` (every Rust PR would install Node.js, and every web PR the Rust toolchain); make `web` a required check (a path-filtered required check never reports on other PRs, the problem DEC-112 avoided); pnpm or Bun (npm ships with Node.js, so there is one tool fewer to pin) |
| DEC-201 | **How `web/` carries the Owlhead name** (the founder's instruction of 2026-09-28, recorded by the Cursor coordinating session; stream W3, claim [#247](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/247)). [DEC-171](#decisions) decides the public name (Owlhead, at owlhead.ai), that every public-facing surface says it, and that the repository, crates, code and working documents keep "Mandate"; this decision only applies it inside `web/`. (1) **The UI says Owlhead**: the shell wordmark, page titles on routes that are not sensitive, and the metadata (`applicationName` "Owlhead", `metadataBase` https://owlhead.ai). (2) **"Mandate" stays the envelope term in UI copy** ("Your mandate"), as the specs use it; only the product's name changes. (3) **Sensitive routes keep generic titles** under P5 and §5 rule 6 (an agent page is titled "Agent · Owlhead", an approval "Approval request · Owlhead"), with no agent name, ticker or amount. (4) **The wordmark is typographic**: "OWLHEAD" set in the UI's display face, with no symbol and no favicon, until a founder-approved mark exists; agents do not draw one. **Amended by [DEC-203](#decisions):** the founder supplied the mark, so the UI shows it with a lowercase "owlhead" wordmark in outlines, and has a favicon. (5) **Code identifiers in `web/` stay "mandate"** (the `mandate-web` package name, paths, and identifiers), per DEC-171 | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-28) | The web slice needs the UI's share of DEC-171 settled now: where the name appears, what the envelope is called, how the privacy rule on titles meets a branded title, and what stands in for a mark. "Mandate" is already the precise word the specs use for the envelope. A mark drawn by an agent would be a guess at the brand, so the UI waits for a founder-approved design | Keep the M mark until the new one exists (it spells the codename, not the product); invent an interim symbol (an agent-drawn brand); rename "Your mandate" in copy (loses the specs' term for the envelope); put the product name in sensitive titles (P5 keeps them generic) |
| DEC-202 | **The brand and UI palette is "navy and brass"** (the founder's choice of 2026-09-28, recorded by the Cursor coordinating session; stream W3, claim [#292](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/292)). (1) **Values.** Navy #183D73, the primary colour (the brand, the sidebar header, primary actions); brass #AC7D1B, the one accent; dark brass #634606, brass as text and on the tint; brass tint #FDF1DC, a brass notice field; slate ink #181C21, text; off-white #F7FAFE, the page. `web/COLOR.md`, which lands with the palette port in `web/`, maps these six values to the UI's tokens and meanings and lists every pair with its contrast. (2) **Proportion.** 60/30/10: off-white and the neutrals carry about 60 percent of a screen, navy about 30, brass at most 10. (3) **Contrast.** Every text pair meets WCAG 2.2 AA (4.5:1 for body text, 3:1 for large text and for non-text indicators) and is also checked with APCA, which judges thin text and dark backgrounds more reliably than the WCAG ratio. Slate ink on off-white is 16.4:1, navy on off-white and off-white on navy 10.3:1, dark brass on the brass tint 7.8:1. Brass itself is 3.5:1 on off-white and 2.9:1 on navy, so it marks rules, borders and large type only; text in brass uses dark brass. (4) **Gains and losses** never rely on hue alone: they carry a sign and a word, and `web/COLOR.md` gives a colour-blind alternate pair for them. **Supersedes** Placard's marigold field (`web/DESIGN.md`) as the brand's accent; the palette port moves the meaning colours onto these values. **Superseded by [DEC-204](#decisions):** the palette is Ink and Gold, in a light and a dark theme (`web/COLOR.md`) | **Superseded by DEC-204** (accepted by the founder, 2026-09-28) | Blue reads as trust and steadiness, which a product that holds an owner's money needs first; a single warm, restrained accent against it gives one place for the eye to go and keeps flat fields (DEC-200 item 8) from looking generic. The 60/30/10 split keeps the accent rare enough to mean something. WCAG 2.2 AA is the legal and practical floor, APCA catches the cases its ratio misjudges, and about one man in twelve has trouble telling red from green, so gains and losses need a second channel | Keep Placard's lapis and marigold (the founder chose navy and brass); green and red alone for gains and losses (fails red-green colour blindness); several accents (dilutes the 10 percent and the Meaning Rule); brass as body text (3.5:1 fails AA) |
| DEC-203 | **The Owlhead mark and wordmark** (the founder's artwork and choice of 2026-09-28, recorded by the Cursor coordinating session; stream W3, claim [#292](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/292)). (1) **The mark is the founder-supplied artwork.** The Cursor coordinating session traced it into one flat vector path, `web/src/components/brand/owlhead-mark.svg`, and the UI uses that flat single-colour silhouette. The founder's shaded original is not used in product UI, because DEC-200 item 8 requires flat colour. (2) **The wordmark is lowercase "owlhead" set in P052 Roman (URW++)**, used as outlines only (`web/src/components/brand/owlhead-wordmark.svg`). The font file is not committed or distributed, and the UI loads no font for the wordmark. (3) **Colour.** One colour at a time from DEC-202: navy on light surfaces, off-white on navy. **Amended 2026-09-28 (founder):** the mark is always navy on a light surface, never off-white on navy; the app icons are the navy mark on an off-white tile; and the brand has no tagline ("Agents that trade under your mandate." was removed). **Amended by [DEC-204](#decisions):** the mark and wordmark are ink on light and off-white on dark, following the theme; the favicon, the app icons and the Open Graph image are the ink mark on an off-white tile; there is still no tagline. (4) **Assets.** The favicon, the app icons, and the Open Graph image are generated from these two sources by `web/scripts/brand-assets.mjs` and committed with it; the minimum sizes are 16 px for the mark and 96 px wide for the lockup. **Amends** [DEC-201](#decisions) item 4, which kept the wordmark typographic ("OWLHEAD", with no symbol and no favicon) until a founder-approved mark existed | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-28) | DEC-201 waited for a founder-approved mark rather than one an agent drew, and the founder has now supplied it. A flat silhouette keeps DEC-200's flat colour, takes `currentColor`, and still reads at favicon size, where shading turns to mud. Outlines render the same on every device, cost no font request, and keep the font file out of the repository | Use the shaded original in the UI (not flat colour, DEC-200 item 8); keep the typographic "OWLHEAD" (the founder supplied a mark); load P052 as a web font (a font request and a distributed font file); an agent-drawn or redrawn mark (DEC-201 forbids it) |
| DEC-204 | **The web UI is redesigned to a consumer-grade standard, in Ink and Gold, light and dark** (the founder's decision of 2026-09-28, recorded by the Cursor coordinating session; stream W4, claim [#297](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/297)). (1) **Standard.** The owner-facing screens reach the polish of Robinhood, Public or Wealthfront without their gamification: no confetti, streaks, badges, celebratory motion, or anything that rewards trading. The [product-experience brief](../product/09-product-experience.md) still binds and wins over any design choice; Home's primary action stays "open an agent or an approval". (2) **Structure.** One hero number per screen and one chart as its centre (a scrubbable equity chart on Home and on each agent, with the mandate's levels as labelled price lines), generous space, few boxes (lists are rows on the page; a panel appears only when it carries meaning), and purposeful motion that respects reduced motion. The header keeps Stop as its dominant control; a quiet sidebar on desktop and a tab bar on phones replace the signage bands. **Amended by [DEC-206](#decisions), [DEC-207](#decisions) and [DEC-208](#decisions):** Stop is quiet until something needs the owner, rather than the header's dominant control (DEC-206); the phone's tab bar is Home, Approvals, Agents and More (DEC-207); on desktop a labelled floating dock replaces the quiet sidebar, and the header gains a wide command bar and folding breadcrumbs (DEC-208). (3) **Type.** Mona Sans (OFL-1.1, one variable family, weights up to 600, sentence case, tabular figures) replaces the signage type; there are no capitals-only labels or heavy bands. **Superseded in part by [DEC-209](#decisions):** the one face is Public Sans; the rest of this item stands. (4) **Two densities.** Calm for the retail-facing screens an owner lives in (Home, agents, approvals) and dense for audit, settings and connections; both share every token and keep 44 px touch targets on phones. (5) **Palette: Ink and Gold, in a light and a dark theme.** Light: ink #14161A for type, primary actions and the Stop control, on off-white #FDFCFA (the body and cards) and paper #F8F7F4 (wells); gold #AB7D13 for lines, rails and marks, dark gold #6A4D08 for gold as text, and the gold tint #FFF6E6 for the mandate's field. Dark: the same ramps from the other end, with off-white #F8F7F4 type on ink #14161A and night #0B0D11, gold #D9A948 for lines, #F9D28A for gold text and #3C2E14 for the field. Crimson #9C0C12 is the kill switch alone in both themes (its edge is #FD8C81 in dark). Gold is used only through the gold tokens, never as text lighter than dark gold in light mode, and never as a large block. Every semantic pair and every Kumo role pair meets WCAG 2.2 AA in both themes, and APCA is checked in the development tests only. Gains and losses carry a sign and a word; with colour-blind friendly on, a gain is blue and a loss raspberry in light or orange in dark, each kept apart from gold and crimson under simulated deuteranopia and protanopia. `web/COLOR.md` holds the ramps, the tokens, the gold rules and the contrast and colour-vision tables. **Supersedes [DEC-202](#decisions)** (navy and brass). **Amended by [DEC-205](#decisions):** the accent is ultramarine on a cool white page (Ink and Ultramarine), and the colour-blind gain is teal; the rest of this item stands. (6) **Logo.** The Owlhead mark and wordmark ([DEC-203](#decisions)) are ink on light and off-white on dark, following the theme; the favicon, the app icons and the Open Graph image are the ink mark on an off-white tile; there is still no tagline. **Amends DEC-203** item 3 and its 2026-09-28 amendment (navy on a light surface, always). (7) **What stays.** [DEC-200](#decisions) item 8 (flat colour, no gradients, the Impeccable detector); the routes, fixtures and drill-downs; and every safety behaviour already in `web/` (Stop always visible and never disabled, the Stop sheet's order with step-up and cancel, frozen kill and release records, role gates, equal Approve and Skip with a static deadline, nothing optimistic, crimson only on the kill switch, paper labelling, generic titles and opaque identifiers, and the performance disclosure beside every P&L). (8) **Supersedes** the structure and signage type of the Placard direction in `web/DESIGN.md` (the founder's pick of 2026-09-28); `web/DESIGN.md` records the system that replaces it | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-28) | An owner checks on agents that trade for them, and the screens they live in should answer "how am I doing, and does anything need me" at a glance: one number and one chart do that better than signage bands and boxed panels. Polish earns trust in a product that handles money, while gamification would nudge trading, which the brief's No-Nudge rule forbids. The founder previewed Ink and Gold in both themes and approved it: ink and paper keep most of the screen quiet, a single gold accent gives the mandate one place for the eye to go, and a dark theme is expected of a consumer app. Holding gold to lines and dark-gold text keeps it legible and rare, and the contrast and colour-vision tests hold both themes to the same bar DEC-202 set. | Keep Placard (its signage structure reads as an operations console, not a consumer product); a consumer look with gamification (celebrations and streaks nudge trading, against the brief); keep navy and brass (DEC-202; the founder chose Ink and Gold); light only (a consumer app is expected to follow the system theme); gold as body text (gold-500 fails 4.5:1 on paper); one density everywhere (too sparse for audit logs or too tight for Home) |
| DEC-205 | **The web UI's accent is ultramarine, on a cool white page: Ink and Ultramarine** (the founder's decision of 2026-09-29, chosen from a live preview of seven accents (gold, iris, ultramarine, petrol, jade, plum, graphite) and page colours on the real screens, recorded by the Cursor session; stream W4, following [#299](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/299)). (1) **Values.** Light: ink #14161A type on cool paper #FBFDFE (the body and cards) and #F5F7F9 (wells), secondary type #3F4348; ultramarine #547EEE for lines, rails and marks, deep ultramarine #2F4C9D for ultramarine as text and for the focus ring, the ultramarine tint #F3F7FF for the mandate's field, and #D5E1FE for selected text. Dark: #F5F7F9 type (secondary #D5D8DB) on ink #14161A and night #0B0D11; ultramarine #8EAFFC for lines and marks, #C6D7FE for ultramarine text and the focus ring, #242F4A for the field and #2E406D for selected text. Crimson #9C0C12 is still the kill switch alone. (2) **The ramps.** The accent ramp is ultramarine at OKLCH hue 266 on the shared lightness curve: vivid but not neon at steps 400 to 700 (chroma at most 0.175, clamped to 97% of the sRGB gamut) and a quiet tint at 100, 850 and 900 (chroma 0.05 or less). Paper and ink move to hue 255 at their existing low chroma. Every token keeps its name and meaning, and ultramarine takes gold's place in every rule and test. (3) **Contrast.** Every semantic pair and every Kumo role pair still meets WCAG 2.2 AA in both themes, and APCA in the development tests. The focus ring holds at least 7.4:1 against every surface in light and 9.1:1 in dark, and the marks hold 3.49:1 against the page. (4) **Colour-blind friendly.** The blue gain sat 21 degrees from the new accent and merged with ultramarine text under red-green deficiency, so the gain is now teal (hue 205; #0B5E65 light, #72EDFA dark). The light loss is one step deeper raspberry (#521537) so a teal gain and a raspberry loss stay apart for deuteranopes, and the dark loss stays orange. Tritanopia is now simulated alongside deuteranopia and protanopia, and the accent's marks, gain, loss and crimson stay apart (OKLab ΔE 0.1 or more) under all three. Gain against ultramarine text is required to stay apart under deuteranopia and protanopia in light mode, and is information only in dark mode and under tritanopia, where no gain hue at text lightness separates from it; gains still carry a sign and a word. The warning amber stays off every screen, now because at text lightness it sits beside the colour-blind orange loss. (5) **Brand.** The logo stays ink on light and off-white on dark; the favicon, app icons and Open Graph image are regenerated on the cool off-white tile #FBFDFE. **Supersedes DEC-204's gold accent and warm paper only** (the colour values of its item 5); the rest of [DEC-204](#decisions) stands. `web/COLOR.md` holds the ramps, the tokens, the ultramarine rules and the contrast and colour-vision tables | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-29); the accent superseded by DEC-214 (volt) | The founder compared the accents side by side on the real screens and chose ultramarine on a cool white page. A cool accent keeps COLOR.md's first principle (no warm, saturated hue pushes the owner), leaving crimson the only warm, saturated colour on a screen, and a page at a neighbouring cool hue keeps the neutrals from reading as tinted against it. Tuning each step's chroma, rather than scaling the preview's gold chroma by 1.55, keeps the marks vivid without turning neon and the fields quiet. The colour-blind change follows from the accent: the tests require the accent's marks, gain, loss and crimson to stay apart for colour-blind owners, and a blue gain beside a blue accent did not | Keep gold (DEC-204; the founder chose ultramarine); iris, petrol, jade, plum or graphite (shown in the same preview, not chosen); keep the warm paper under ultramarine (the founder chose a cool page); take the preview's values unchanged (the scaled chroma ran the mid steps and the dark fields hotter than a quiet tint); keep the blue colour-blind gain (it merges with ultramarine text under deuteranopia, ΔE 0.04); loosen the colour-vision threshold instead of moving the gain (the tests hold the bar DEC-202 set) |
| DEC-206 | **The Stop control is quiet until something needs you** (the founder's decision of 2026-09-29, recorded by a Cursor agent; stream W4). (1) **Quiet by default.** On a calm screen Stop is an outlined pill: a 2 px ink border (paper in dark mode), an ink octagon and "Stop" in ink type, on the header's own colour. From 64rem it is 40 px tall, the height of the command bar ([DEC-208](#decisions)); below that the pill is 44 px. The hit area is 44 px tall at every width. It stays at the right of the header at every width from 320 px. (2) **Loud when there is a risk reason.** It becomes the filled ink pill (paper in dark mode), in the same box, so nothing moves, while `stopAttention` in `web/src/lib/attention.ts` gives a reason taken from workspace state and existing helpers only: an open alert as the Alerts screen lists it, except the owner's own pause or stop (a stale or failing feed, or an agent restricted by its mandate, the account or market data, which covers exits only and an agent halted by a gate); an agent in exits only; an agent near a loss limit; an unreachable deployment; an order in flight. *Near a loss limit* means the agent has used 80% or more of the loss the limit allows (the daily loss budget, a drawdown rung's depth below the high-water mark, or the capital the lifetime floor lets go), measured with the mandate panel's own headroom (`agentLimits`). No threshold existed in the code, `web/DESIGN.md` or the brief, so this decision sets it (`NEAR_LIMIT_USED` in `web/src/lib/limits.ts`). *In flight* means the broker has not confirmed the order: `Intent`, `Submitting`, `PendingCancel`, `PendingReplace` or `Unknown` (trading-domain spec §5.7). An order resting at the broker, partly filled or not, is confirmed; protective stops rest there all day. A pending approval alone never makes Stop loud. While the workspace loads, the last known tone holds, starting quiet. (3) **Unchanged.** Stop is never disabled, hidden or moved, and its accessible name is always "Stop". When loud, `aria-describedby` adds a short reason, such as "Needs attention: 1 alert, Agent 1 is near its daily loss limit". There is no badge, dot or count on the button. The swap is a colour change at `--duration-hover`, instant under reduced motion, with no pulse, glow or other attention animation (the No-Nudge rule), and both tones keep a visible border under forced colours. Nothing else in the header is filled in ink. Crimson stays on the kill-switch choices in the Stop sheet alone, and the Stop sheet is unchanged. **Amends [DEC-204](#decisions) item 2** ("the header keeps Stop as its dominant control") and nothing else. P1 in `web/PRODUCT.md` is unchanged and still met | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-29) | A brake that is always the heaviest thing on the screen signals danger on a calm screen, and it competes with the one hero number DEC-204 gives each screen. P1 asks that Stop be visible on every screen, high contrast and never disabled, with a 44 px target on phones. It does not ask that Stop be the loudest element. A 2 px ink outline is far above 3:1 against the header in both themes, and its label keeps 7:1 (`STOP_CONTRAST`). Turning loud only for a real risk makes Stop louder exactly when it matters, so the change itself carries information. Counting approvals would make it loud on most screens and blur the difference between approving and stopping. | Always quiet (it would never draw the eye when something is wrong); icon only (a bare octagon is easy to misread); moving Stop into the phone tab bar or the sidebar (the header is the only bar on every screen at every width); keeping it dominant (the founder found it too big and prominent) |
| DEC-207 | **The phone is a remote control** (the founder's decision of 2026-09-29, recorded by the Cursor coordinating session; stream W4). Below the `lg` breakpoint (64rem) the web UI does four jobs: say whether anything needs the owner, approve or skip, stop, and give a glance at how they are doing. From `lg` up, tablet and desktop are unchanged; between 40rem and 64rem the phone's structure applies with the room used sensibly. (1) **Header.** Three things: the mark, which opens Home; the PAPER badge; and Stop. No menu and no search button, and the glass stays. No status strip: a banner under the header, at the strip's height and in its words, appears only when a feed is stale or failing or the workspace is unreachable. Record screens keep the full strip at every width, as a safety need. (2) **Tab bar.** Home, Approvals (with its count), Agents and More. More is a bottom sheet with the account and workspace switcher at the top, then Search (the command palette), Positions, Audit, Connections, Settings and every other screen the role may open. There is no sidebar sheet. The sheet stops below the header and is not modal, so Stop stays in view, pressable and in the accessibility tree while it is open. Targets are 44 px, and the bar keeps the safe-area inset. (3) **Home.** Needs you comes first: the requests, each a row to its record with the static time it is skipped at, then the alerts, or "All clear. Nothing needs you." with a check; a request appears once on Home. Then the account hero with a 180 px chart and its range picker wholly above the tab bar; one row per agent with its name, its state in a word and its headroom ("$274.45 above its daily loss limit"), with no P&L and no disclosure per row; the last three activity entries and "See all activity". No positions, and the footnotes are condensed. (4) **Agent.** The name, its state and Stop this agent at the top; any waiting request as a row; the equity hero with its disclosure and chart, the level legend behind a Levels button, collapsed; then Headroom, one row per limit with the room left, the limit and a thin ink meter ending at the mandate's deep ultramarine post, in no gain or loss colour. The route tabs give way to a plain list of the sections at the foot of every agent screen, with the counts of positions and orders. Key figures, positions, orders, decisions, activity and the mandate card are a section link away; desktop keeps its tabs and full overview. (5) **Approvals.** The inbox is one hairline row per request. A request is one screen, with Approve and Skip equal in size and weight and pinned on the tab bar to the end of the page. The request states the deadline as an absolute time and the whole minutes left, as the [brief](../product/09-product-experience.md) requires; the rows on Home and in the inbox give the time alone. The frozen content, the step-up and nothing optimistic are unchanged. (6) **What stays.** Every safety behaviour in [DEC-204](#decisions) item 7: Stop on every screen and never disabled, crimson only on the kill switch, the disclosure beside every remaining P&L, paper labelling, generic titles and opaque identifiers, role gates, frozen records and the Stop sheet's order. **Amends DEC-204** item 2's "a tab bar on phones", only to say which tabs | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-29) | Squeezing the desktop onto a phone made it crowded: two navigation systems (the tab bar and a sidebar sheet), a status strip on every screen, and one request shown three times on Home. An owner reaches for a phone to glance, to approve or skip, and to stop, so those stay on the screen and everything else is one press away under More or in a section link. Headroom is a distance to a limit, not a result, so the agent rows and the Headroom list need no disclosure beside them; P&L on every row would repeat the disclosure on each. A banner that appears only when something is wrong keeps the header quiet on a good day and loud on a bad one. The layout switches by breakpoint classes, not script, so the desktop DOM only gains hidden elements | Keep the desktop structure squeezed (crowded, two navigation systems, the request three times); show P&L on the agent rows with the disclosure repeated on each (noise on the screen an owner glances at most); a five-tab bar (smaller targets at 320 px, and More is still needed); keep the sidebar sheet (a second navigation beside the tab bar) |
| DEC-208 | **The frame, the dock, the agent wire and the hero number** (the founder's decisions of 2026-09-28 and 2026-09-29, made from mockups and the founder's live preview of the built app, recorded by the Cursor coordinating session; stream W4). (1) **Glass on the frame only.** The sticky header and the phone tab bar are frosted glass: the page scrolls under them and shows through, blurred. The founder turned glass down anywhere else, so cards, panels, the More sheet, menus, popovers, sheets and dialogs stay flat. The two bars that take the frame's place on desktop, the agent wire under the header and the dock at the bottom, wear the same glass. The glass is the card colour at 72% (the least that keeps muted header text at AA over anything scrolling under it; the mockup's 58% did not), with a hairline in the type colour at 8%; the dock's is denser, the card at 85% with a 15% edge. It is a flat colour and a blur, so [DEC-200](#decisions) item 8 (flat colour, no gradients) still holds, and it falls back to the solid card where the browser cannot blur, under reduced transparency and in forced colours. (2) **A labelled floating dock replaces the desktop sidebar.** From 64rem the primary navigation is a glass dock floating above the bottom edge, centred: each item an icon over its name (Home, Approvals with its count, Alerts, Agents, Positions, Connections), then the Audit and More menus, so every screen a role may open is on the dock or one menu away. The current section sits on a pill in the type colour at 14%, with a filled icon and a semibold label, and stays current anywhere inside the section; the pill is never an ink fill, which is Stop's, nor ultramarine, which is the mandate's. There are no tooltips, since every item is named. The lockup moves into the header and the content takes the full width. (3) **The agent wire replaces the price ticker.** From 64rem, while every feed answers, the strip under the header is a wire of what the agents are doing: requests waiting for you first, then today's gate decisions and the owner's own pauses and stops, each item a link to where it is read in full. It is ink and muted text only and shows no amount won or lost, so it carries no performance disclosure. Its left end shows the oldest feed's age, and that opens the four feeds' freshness in a popover. It drifts slowly, holds still under the pointer or focus and stands still under reduced motion. A stale or failing feed, a loading, unreachable or empty workspace, a role that cannot see agents, or a frozen record screen falls back to the full status strip at the same height. Below 64rem there is no wire (see [DEC-207](#decisions)). (4) **A wide command bar with an honest prompt.** From 64rem the ⌘K trigger is a 40 px bar in the middle of the header, 380 to 460 px wide. It reads "Jump to an agent or screen…", because the palette jumps to screens and agents and searches nothing else. Stop's desktop pill takes the same 40 px height ([DEC-206](#decisions)). (5) **Folding breadcrumbs.** The current page's crumb never truncates. Where the trail is too narrow, the earlier crumbs fold into an "Earlier pages" menu before it, and in the narrowest header the trail hides. (6) **The hero number.** The equity figure over a hero chart (the account's on Home, an agent's on its page) has its own display size, scaled with the hero's width so a long value fits at 320 px, up to 4.75rem, in Public Sans ([DEC-209](#decisions)) at weight 600 and -0.03em. Its cents are half size, muted and raised to the digits' tops, and screen readers hear it once, whole. It is set in proportional lining figures, the one exception to tabular figures, because tabular digits leave visible gaps at that size. The change sits on its own line on a soft pill toned by its sign, with the word and the percentage, and the disclosure symbol ([DEC-210](#decisions)) beside it, so the figure's width changing as it scrubs moves nothing. The approval's action keeps the hero size. (7) **Section tabs mark one tab.** An agent's section tabs mark exactly one current tab: the exact match, else the deepest section the page sits under, with the underline under its label. **Amends [DEC-204](#decisions) item 2** (the quiet desktop sidebar becomes the dock, and the header gains the command bar and folding breadcrumbs); DEC-200 item 8 and every safety behaviour in DEC-204 item 7 stand | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-28 and 2026-09-29); item 3 superseded by DEC-215 (no agent wire) | The founder chose the frame as the one place for depth: glass on the bars the page passes under says what floats, and flat everywhere else keeps the content calm. A sidebar took width from every screen for navigation an owner uses between screens, not on them; a dock gives the content the full width. Its first, icon-only version left people lost, so it gained labels and a pill on the current section. A price ticker made the product read like a brokerage and put prices, which the owner does not trade by hand, above everything. A wire of what the agents are doing answers the question an owner opens the app with, in the space the ticker took, and names no result, so it needs no disclosure. A search prompt must promise only what the palette does. A current crumb that truncates hides where you are. A larger, quieter figure makes the one hero number DEC-204 asks for read first; proportional figures keep it even at display size, and the pill's own line keeps scrubbing from moving the page. Two current tabs misstate where the owner is | Directions shown with the calm structure and not chosen: Graphite and Signal (black-and-white, no accent), Midnight (dark with an acid-green accent) and Soft (a periwinkle accent). Looks shown and not chosen: Terminal (amber monospace on black), Colour block (bold fills), Industrial (orange on grey with a monospace), Neon noir (violet on midnight, against the palette's no-purple rule) and Press (a serif figure face on pink paper). Surfaces: glass on the dots as well as the frame (the founder rejected glass beyond the frame), neo-brutalism (hard offset shadows), neumorphism (extruded shadows), tactile (textured controls). Frames: floating islands (the header and sidebar as inset glass islands; the dock took the sidebar's place instead) and a status island (the four feed ages collapsed into one pill in the header; the ages stay in the strip and the wire's popover). The price ticker (a brokerage's strip of prices, replaced by the wire); the wire itself was removed under [DEC-215](#decisions) |
| DEC-209 | **The type is Public Sans** (the founder's instruction of 2026-09-29, recorded by the Cursor coordinating session; stream W4). (1) **The face.** Public Sans (the U.S. Web Design System's grotesque, drawn from Libre Franklin), one variable family (weights 100 to 900) for every role in `web/`: headings, body, labels, figures and the charts' canvas text. It is self-hosted from `@fontsource-variable/public-sans` under the SIL OFL 1.1, which allows bundling and serving it with the app; the Latin file is about 26 KB and the Latin Extended file about 18 KB, and the app loads no font from a third-party host (DEC-200 item 5). (2) **Figures.** Every figure is set with tabular, lining digits (`tnum`; lining is the default): a flagged 1, a straight 7, and a plain oval zero, narrower than the capital O, with no slash or dot; the true minus sign (U+2212) is in the font. Its tabular feature sets the digits only, so commas, points and signs keep their own widths. The one exception is the display hero figure, which is proportional ([DEC-208](#decisions) item 6). (3) **The hero** stays at weight 600, tracked -0.03em (it was -0.035em), where the figures of `$28,478.36` sit close without touching; no type role is tracked tighter than -0.04em, which `web/src/lib/tokens.test.ts` holds. (4) **No figure face.** The hero number is not set in a serif to echo the P052 wordmark; the wordmark stays the one serif in the product. (5) **What stays.** The type scale, the weights (400, 500 and 600, never bolder), sentence case, the 58ch reading measure, and the wordmark as P052 outlines with no font file ([DEC-203](#decisions)). **Supersedes** Mona Sans in [DEC-204](#decisions) item 3, and nothing else in DEC-204 | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-29) | The founder called Inter "an AI smell tell" and asked for the face to be chosen with Impeccable, whose reflex list rejects the defaults (Inter, Geist, Mona Sans, faces that imitate San Francisco, and the fashionable geometrics) and whose product guidance asks for one family unless a second has a role only it can perform. Public Sans comes from the Franklin Gothic line, the grotesque of newspapers and financial pages, so it reads serious and plain rather than like a neutral product default. Rendered in the built app (Home, an agent, the audit timeline, the positions table and a phone), it stayed open and even at 13 to 14 px in dense price tables and steady at the hero size, where the other candidates were either too narrow small or too loud large. Its figures meet every requirement (tabular and lining, distinct 1, 7 and 0, a minus sign), it covers Latin Extended-A (except Ĳ, ŉ and ſ) and Vietnamese, and the whole Playwright suite passed in both themes with no layout change. A serif hero was tried (Literata, Brygada 1918, Alegreya, with raised cents beside the wordmark) and turned down: it splits money across two faces, because the key figures, rows and mandate rails stay sans; it moves toward the warm-paper-and-serif look the product brief lists as an AI cliché; and it competes with the wordmark. | Archivo (narrow and small at 13 to 14 px, loud at the hero size); Golos Text (at UI sizes hard to tell from Inter); Chivo (heavy at 400); Red Hat Text (carries Red Hat's brand); Schibsted Grotesk and Zalando Sans (tabular punctuation and spaces loosen money); Libre Franklin, Albert Sans, Wix Madefor and Commissioner (no tabular figures in the files the packages ship); Hanken Grotesk, Host Grotesk and Rethink Sans (tabular figures only, and generic); Figtree, Onest, Funnel Sans and Reddit Sans (friendly or tied to a brand); Sofia Sans (thin and narrow at 13 px); Instrument Sans, Mona Sans, Inter and Geist (on Impeccable's reflex list); Epilogue and Geologica (display quirks); Atkinson Hyperlegible Next (a slashed zero, turned down under DEC-204); a serif figure face in Literata, Brygada 1918 or Alegreya (see the rationale); P052 as a web font (DEC-203 keeps the font file out of the product) |
| DEC-210 | **The performance disclosure sits behind an info symbol beside each P&L** (the founder's decision of 2026-09-29, recorded by the Cursor coordinating session; stream W4). (1) **What changes.** Beside every P&L in `web/`, where the dashed `[[DISCLOSURE-PERFORMANCE]]` tag stood, the UI shows an info symbol (muted, 16 px, in a 24 px target that is 44 px on touch) named "Performance disclosure". It opens the disclosure text in a popover on hover, on a click or tap, and on keyboard focus with Enter or Space; Escape closes it. The text is also the symbol's accessible description, so a screen reader hears it without opening anything. (2) **What stays.** The placement still puts the disclosure beside every P&L, in the same row and on the same line as the figure, and the text is one hover or tap away; `web/src/app/disclosure.test.tsx` still fails any route where a P&L has no disclosure in its region. The text is still only the named placeholder (brief §5, rule 9), and `[[LEGEND-HYPOTHETICAL]]` and `[[RETAIL-AUTO-LIVE]]` stay visible tags. (3) **Where it stays inline.** Where nothing can be hovered or opened, the full text shows inline: in print, and on every record screen, because a record screen counts collapsed content as not shown (brief §4.1, PX-1) and its artifact is built from what it renders. No record screen, export or share image shows a P&L today; one that does gets the inline text. (4) **Counsel.** How a disclosure is presented is compliance text, reserved to the founder under [DEC-79](#decisions): **counsel must confirm that a disclosure one hover or tap away meets the "clear and prominent" standard before launch.** If counsel requires it visible, reverting is one line: `PERFORMANCE_INLINE` in `web/src/components/domain/placeholders.tsx`. | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-29); counsel must confirm before launch | The tag beside every P&L cluttered every screen (five times on Home alone), and the founder asked: "can you put it behind a symbol, opens on click and hover?" A symbol in the tag's place keeps the disclosure attached to each figure, where the reader is looking, instead of moving it away from the numbers. Keeping the text in the page as the symbol's description keeps it available to screen readers and to the checks that find it, and print and record screens keep it inline because nothing opens there. Because counsel has not yet judged prominence, the change is built to be undone in one line | Keep the inline tag beside every P&L (the clutter the founder asked to remove); one footnote per screen (fewer marks, but farther from each figure, and a reader of one row may never reach it); drop the per-row P&L (done on phones separately under DEC-207, and the P&L that remain still need their disclosure) |
| DEC-211 | **Sign-in is Supabase Auth: Google first, passkeys after** (the founder's decision of 2026-09-29, recorded by the Cursor web session; stream W4). (1) **What it decides.** `web/` signs people in with Supabase Auth (`@supabase/ssr` and `@supabase/supabase-js`, pinned exactly, MIT, `docs/dependencies.md`). Sign-up and the first sign-in are Google over OIDC. After that a passkey signs in: it is offered once a sign-in finds none on the account (`/auth/passkey`, which can be skipped) and is listed, added, renamed and deleted under Settings > Profile. An email magic link is built and hidden behind `NEXT_PUBLIC_OWLHEAD_EMAIL_SIGNIN=1` until there is SMTP; it never creates an account. No message says whether an account or an address exists: a sign-in that did not finish has one sentence whatever the cause, and every email request is answered "If that address can sign in, we've sent a link" (brief O1). (2) **Where it applies.** Sign-in is on only when `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` and `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` are both set and the e2e build flag is off (`authEnabled`, `web/src/lib/auth-config.ts`); otherwise the app is exactly as before, with no gate and no call to Supabase. When it is on, `web/src/proxy.ts` refreshes the session on every request and a pure function decides (`authRoute`, `web/src/lib/auth-routes.ts`): a signed-out visitor at `/` sees the welcome page at the same address, a signed-out visitor anywhere else goes to `/login?next=` and the path, a signed-in visitor at `/login` goes on to `next`, and `next` is only ever a same-origin path, so it cannot redirect off the site. Who is signed in comes from `getClaims()`, which verifies the token, never from `getSession()`, and the session lives in cookies, not in browser storage. The public pages (`/welcome`, `/login`, `/auth/*`) have their own frame, the `(site)` route group, with the app's header but no Stop, dock, wire or paper badge; every screen of the app moved into the `(app)` group with no change of address. (3) **Risks.** Supabase passkeys are a beta API, so both packages are pinned and the client opts in. A passkey is bound to its relying-party ID, `localhost` now and `owlhead.ai` at launch; changing it invalidates every passkey made under the old one, so people add theirs again after launch, with Google as the way back in. The fixture roles (PX-11) are not yet tied to signed-in users: the role switch still decides what a screen shows. The step-up before Stop and approvals is still the mock; Supabase reauthentication or a fresh passkey assertion is future work. (4) **Backend handoff.** The Rust services verify the Supabase access token, a JWT, against the project's JWKS at `https://wyyxngmblqqokcvraflx.supabase.co/auth/v1/.well-known/jwks.json`, with issuer `https://wyyxngmblqqokcvraflx.supabase.co/auth/v1` and audience `authenticated`; `sub` is the user's id. **Applies [DEC-18](#decisions)** (OIDC SSO in v1) to the web app's own sign-in | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-29) | There is no SMTP yet, so an emailed code or link cannot be the main way in, and Google over OIDC needs no email of ours and is the OIDC DEC-18 already chose. O1 asks for a passkey or OIDC; a passkey after the first visit makes every later sign-in one touch, with no password to leak or reset. Supabase gives both, keeps the session in cookies the server can verify, and publishes keys the Rust services can check without calling it. Building email now and hiding it lets it switch on with SMTP and no new screens | Email and password (a password to store, leak and reset, against O1); an emailed code only (needs SMTP, which does not exist yet); an OIDC provider without Supabase (the app would own sessions, refresh and passkeys itself); Auth0 or Clerk (capable, but another vendor and price for what Supabase already gives the web app and the backend) |
| DEC-212 | **The landing page at owlhead.ai** (the founder's brief of 2026-09-29, recorded by the Cursor landing-page session; stream W4). (1) **Where.** Signed-out visitors to `/` get `/welcome`, which renders `Landing` from `web/src/components/site/landing.tsx`, a server component with no props. The sign-in work owns the route, the site header (wordmark, "Sign in") and `/login`; the page starts below that header and brings its own `<main id="main">` and footer, so the layout around it adds no second `<main>`. (2) **Structure.** A hero: the headline "Let an AI agent trade inside limits you set.", one subhead, "Get started" to `/login`, "How it works" to its section, and the paper badge with "Paper trading only. Live trading isn't available." Then How it works in three steps, each beside a real screen (set the mandate; the agent trades inside it, through the gate; you stay in charge, approving or skipping and stopping); Why it's safe, a ledger of what the agent can't do and what you can always do, then five facts; Who it's for (people with their own brokerage account; businesses, marked "Coming later"); five questions; and a footer. (3) **The product is the hero.** The pictures are the app itself: `npm run site-shots` (`web/scripts/site-shots.mjs`) screenshots the production build at 2x in light and dark into `web/public/site/`, hiding every P&L figure, the hero's change pill, the key figures and the loss-today rail first. `next/image` serves them through a `<picture>` whose dark source follows `prefers-color-scheme`, with width and height set; the hero's screens load eagerly at high fetch priority (Next 16 deprecates `priority`). Frames are hairlines, with one hairline phone frame, and a caption says the screens show example data on a paper account. (4) **Copy rules.** Plain English, sentence case, short sentences and the product's nouns (mandate, gate, Stop, kill switch, journal). No performance, returns, testimonials or figures that read as results, and no "profit", "earn", "guaranteed" or "beat the market". Every claim comes from the docs, and what is not built says so: journal export and the chain check (FR-7.4, FR-7.5) and everything for businesses are marked "Coming later". Paper today, no live trading and no date for it (DEC-98, brief B5); no promise against loss (DEC-145). Compliance text appears only as named placeholders (DEC-79): `[[SITE-DISCLAIMER]]` in the footer, `[[NOT-INVESTMENT-ADVICE]]` as the whole answer to "Is this investment advice?", and `[[PRIVACY-POLICY]]` and `[[TERMS-OF-SERVICE]]` in place of legal pages, which nobody invents. (5) **The same design system.** The app's tokens, Public Sans, the serif wordmark, light and dark with the system, flat surfaces and no gradients (DEC-200 item 8). No cards: hairlines, whitespace and type. Ultramarine marks only the step numbers. One authored motion: from 64rem, with motion allowed, the hero's phone rises 12 px and fades in once. (6) **Indexing.** The landing page is the one indexed page: `landingMetadata` (`web/src/components/site/metadata.ts`) sets the title to "Owlhead" alone, a description, `robots: { index: true, follow: true }`, `/` as the canonical address (`/welcome` serves it there), and the existing share image for Open Graph and Twitter. The description is for search results only; the share card keeps the no-tagline rule (web/PRODUCT.md, amended for this). Every app page stays `noindex`. | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-29); items 2 to 5 superseded by DEC-213 (the retro text page and the private beta) | What the product does is the best case for it, so the page shows the screens an owner will use rather than describing them, and a trading product must not sell on returns. One design system means the page promises exactly what the owner sees after signing in. Keeping the page in `web/` shares the tokens, the fonts and the components, and ships with the app. | A separate marketing site or framework (a second design system and deploy that drift from the app, and its pictures would still be the app's); a waitlist-only page (says nothing about what the product does, and brief B5 rules out waitlist framing that implies a date); a template (gradients, bento cards, testimonials and metrics, all ruled out by the copy and design rules) |
| DEC-213 | **The landing page is one retro page of text, and it gathers interest in a private beta** (the founder's decisions of 2026-09-29, made on the live page, recorded by the Cursor landing-page session; stream W4). (1) **What changes.** DEC-212's screenshot page gives way to one page of text in the manner of a 1990s home page: a period browser window (title bar, menus and toolbar as `aria-hidden` scenery, the address, guide links, a status bar) on a dithered desktop, the name in ASCII art, and nine numbered sections beside a contents list: what we're building, why, how it works, how it stays in check, the record (a working tamper demo on example data), who it's for, where we are, questions, and ask for a place. The site header is hidden on the landing page (`OffLanding`), which draws its own window. `npm run site-shots`, `web/public/site/` and the `/site/` public path are removed. (2) **A private beta.** The page calls Owlhead a private beta, opening a few people at a time, with no date. Its guestbook form posts an email and an optional use (own account, money for others, a desk, an agent of one's own) to `POST /api/beta`, which validates it, drops a filled honeypot silently, and stores it in the Supabase table `beta_requests` (`web/supabase/migrations/20260929180000_beta_requests.sql`; row level security lets the publishable key insert and never read), or in `web/.data/beta-requests.jsonl`, gitignored, until that table exists. A repeat address counts as stored, so the form never reveals who is on the list. (3) **What it may say.** At the founder's direction the page may describe what is not built yet, as long as it is listed as coming: "Working now, on paper" (writing rules and checking every order against them, paper trading on Alpaca, approvals and Stop, the record) and "Coming during the beta" (live trading once it has legal sign-off; Robinhood and Kraken Derivatives US; firm and team limits and self-hosting; the MCP server). No performance, returns, percentages, signed amounts, testimonials, "profit", "guaranteed" or "beat the market"; no promise against loss, and it says a loss can end up larger than a limit (DEC-145). (4) **Compliance wording.** At the founder's direction the landing page's named placeholders (`[[SITE-DISCLAIMER]]`, `[[NOT-INVESTMENT-ADVICE]]`, `[[PRIVACY-POLICY]]`, `[[TERMS-OF-SERVICE]]`) give way to plain words: the footer's "Owlhead is in private beta. It is software, not investment advice. Trading involves risk, and you can lose money." and a plain answer to "Is this investment advice?". This departs from DEC-79 for the landing page only; the product's placeholders stay. Counsel reviews both before launch and supplies a privacy notice for the form before the list is used. (5) **Design.** The app's tokens only, light and dark with no `dark:` classes, flat fills and no gradients (the dither and the construction tape are SVG patterns). Period faces for this page alone, self-hosted from `@fontsource` (DotGothic16, VT323, Pixelify Sans, all SIL OFL) and imported only by `landing.tsx`; the product stays in Public Sans (DEC-209). Two product rules are scoped out for this page, with the reason in the tests: the reserved warning tint marks the construction strip and the edited line of the record, since the page shows no gain or loss for amber to be mistaken for; and its blinking "New" beside the beta notice is not a recommendation badge. The blink is the only motion, and only with motion allowed. (6) **Indexing.** DEC-212 item 6 stands, with the description "A trading agent for your own brokerage account, working inside rules you write. Now in private beta, starting on paper." | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-29); supersedes DEC-212 items 2 to 5; amends DEC-79 for the landing page's disclaimer; counsel reviews the copy and the wording, and supplies a privacy notice for the form, before launch | The founder wanted one page that says what Owlhead is, why and how, and gathers interest, with a character of its own rather than another financial-product template. Text stays true as the app changes, where screenshots of a moving app go stale, and a list of requests lets places open a few at a time. | DEC-212's screenshot page (said little about why, and gathered no interest); a visual private-beta page with a request form (built first; the founder chose the text page instead); a third-party waitlist service (another vendor holding the addresses) |
| DEC-214 | **The accent is volt: Ink and Volt** (the founder's decision of 2026-09-29, recorded by the Cursor web session; stream W4). (1) **Values.** Light: volt-500 #7C9217 for lines, rails and marks; olive volt-700 #4C5A09 for volt as text and for the focus ring; the volt tint volt-100 #F2FCD7 for the mandate's field; volt-200 #D2F34A for selected text. Paper, ink, crimson, gain and loss are unchanged. (2) **The ramp.** Volt is OKLCH hue 120 on the shared lightness curve, with its own chroma table: neon at 200 and 300 (C 0.19 and 0.2), olive by 700 (C 0.1), and calm tints from 800. Every token keeps its name and meaning, and volt takes ultramarine's place in every rule and test. (3) **Contrast.** Every semantic pair and every Kumo role pair still meets WCAG 2.2 AA in both themes, and APCA in the development tests. (4) **Colour vision.** Every required check passes under simulated deuteranopia, protanopia and tritanopia; the colour-blind teal gain sits 85 degrees from volt. The colour-blind gain against volt text under tritanopia in light mode, and the colour-blind loss against volt text under tritanopia in dark mode, are information only. | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-29); supersedes DEC-205's accent; the rest of DEC-205 stands | The founder's choice, applied across the app and the landing page. | DEC-215 | **No agent wire: the desktop frame follows the phone's** (the founder's decision of 2026-09-29, made on the live dashboard, recorded by the Cursor web session; stream W4). (2) **What shows instead.** From 64rem the frame follows the phone's rule ([DEC-207](#decisions)): nothing under the header while every feed answers; the full status strip, at the same 34 px, while a feed is stale or failing or the workspace is loading or unreachable; and the full strip on every record screen at every width. | DEC-177 | **Journal spec v0.5 §9.1 closes eight agent-stream payload schemas, and the test vectors gain a generated `agent_stream` section** (DEC-174 item 4, first half; stream L, claim [#171](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/171); id assigned by the backend coordinator's dispatch). (1) **Scope.** The agent stream's `StreamOpened`, `ObservationRecorded`, `ModelOutputRecorded`, `DecisionMade`, `IntentProposed`, `AgentModeChanged`, the agent stream's `KillSwitchActivated`, and `OwnerExitRequested`. The approval events (`ApprovalRequested`, `ApprovalDelivered`, `ApprovalResponded`, `ApprovalTimedOut`, `ApprovalCanceled`) close together with M7's spec change (claim [#213](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/213)), which extends their rows, so this change edits neither those rows nor §2. `ModelInvocationRecorded`, `ThesisProposed`, and `ThesisRevised` close with their own stories. (2) **DEC-174 item 3, the spec's side each time.** Empty values are `null` (§4.2): `DecisionMade.reason_code` on an allow, and `OwnerExitRequested`'s `bid`, `bid_size`, `floor`, `user`, and `step_up` when nothing was confirmed. Every instant is a §4.7 timestamp, never risk-clock seconds: `ObservationRecorded.as_of`, `ModelOutputRecorded.as_of` and `expires_at`, and `step_up.authenticated_at`. Observed data and model content are artifact references (`data_ref`, `content_hash`), never inline. `IntentProposed` carries exactly the `IntentReceived` vector's intent fields less `intent_id` (its own `event_id`, §2) and `agent_id` (the stream's): `instrument_id`, `side`, `type`, `tif`, `qty`, `limit_price`, and `purpose`. The key fields §9 lists and the runtime omits become members: `DecisionMade`'s outputs used, model weights, clips applied, and exit and buy convictions; `ModelOutputRecorded`'s content hash, direction, conviction, confidence, horizon, and `ignored` reason. `step_up` is the structured evidence §9 already names for approvals: assertion ID, authentication time, and method. (3) **The runtime's extra members, each kept because another rule needs it.** `AgentModeChanged.lifecycle`: restrictions lift independently (mandate spec §5.9), so an owner's pause taken under a stricter restriction must replay from the agent stream's own events (§1 principle 4). `KillSwitchActivated.subject`: "touches only its scope" (`AGENTS.md` rule 13, trading spec §5.5) is checkable only if the event names the subject, which rule 15 ties to the stream. `KillSwitchActivated.mode_event`: the executor's `AgentModeApplied` copy names it as `causation_id` (§2), which is how the agent stream's fold confirms the switch from its own events. `OwnerExitRequested.bid_size` and `floor`: trading spec §5.5 and mandate spec §6.1 require the confirmed bid size and a floor below which the exit price ladder never prices. (4) **Rules.** Consistency rules 1 to 13 and subject rules 14 and 15, each derived from a rule already stated: long-only purposes; limit orders for opening and increasing (`AGENTS.md` rule 12, trading spec §5.1); §6.2's dry run before classification and built-in AUTO; `defer` for discretionary exits only (trading spec §9.1); §4.1's ordering; mandate spec §5.9's mode order; trading spec §5.5's owner confirmation; and the research agent's thesis and lineage. Each has an invalid draft in the vectors. They only refuse drafts, so none adds risk. (5) **Vectors.** `reference/journal/generate.py` generates the section and checks it. It first reproduces every version-3 vector, then validates the chain events and drafts against its own mirror of the schemas and rules, recomputes `DecisionMade` from the model output, weights, and observed quote (mandate spec §8.3), checks `IntentProposed` against the `IntentReceived` vector, and requires every seeded validator and vector bug to be caught. After round 1 of the review (items 8 to 15) that is 13 chain events, 47 invalid and 5 valid drafts, 4 batches, 2 range-verification cases, 31 validator and verifier mutants, and 21 vector mutants, each vector mutant re-chained so that only the check it names can catch it. A pytest runs it with `--check` in CI, and `mandate journal verify` verifies the exported chain with its artifacts. (6) **The vectors stay version 3**, against the dispatch's "version 4". `journal::version` is a passing harness case that pins 3, the spec guard (ES-22) keeps the harness change out of a spec PR, and the section is additive, so every existing vector keeps its bytes. The bump follows E7-9's tests PR, which makes the harness read `agent_stream`: that code PR, then a one-line spec change. (7) **Not ruled:** what happens to a model output outside §8.2's ranges (conviction in [−1, 1], confidence in [0, 1]); the schema records it as given. The runtime changes these rulings imply are other streams' work, recorded in the backlog. **Round 1 of the independent review** (the coordinator's rulings; each a tightening, accepted under DEC-176): (8) **M2, the Status line names v0.5 first**, as the mandate and trading specs name their current version; M7's spec change (claim [#213](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/213)) also calls itself v0.5, and whichever merges second renumbers to v0.6. (9) **M3, `AgentModeChanged.lifecycle` has its own three-value type** (`normal`, `paused`, `stopped`), as §9.1's table already said; the generator's mirror had typed it as the four-value mode and accepted `exits_only`. The invalid draft `mode_lifecycle_exits_only` expects `non_canonical` at `payload.lifecycle`, and the mutant `regress.lifecycle_as_mode`, which restores the wider type, is caught only by that draft. (10) **M4, the absent-member rule has vectors and a mutant.** A change in an invalid draft may now be `{path, delete: true}`, which removes the member. `owner_exit_floor_absent` and `decision_reason_code_absent_on_allow` each expect `schema` at the deleted member, and the mutant `record.missing` models a validator that reads an absent member as `null`. It escaped before these drafts and is caught by the second: the first alone cannot catch it, because rule 12 refuses a `null` floor on a confirmed exit with the same reason and path. (11) **B1, `OwnerExitRequested` records who asked and what step-up they gave on every owner exit.** Rule 12 had tied `user` and `step_up` to `confirmed`, but a bid is confirmed only where trading spec §5.5 needs one (an equity sold outside the regular session), so an in-session or crypto close and every owner kill switch would have journaled no owner and no step-up, against mandate spec §5.10 and §6.1 and §9. Now `confirmed` governs only `bid`, `bid_size`, and `floor`; `user` is `text`, never `null`; the new required member `step_up_status` (`valid`, `absent`, `stale`) says which case of DEC-158 option (c) applied; and `step_up` is non-null exactly when `step_up_status` is `valid` (rule 12's second clause). No rule requires a member only valid step-up or a confirmed bid supplies, so no step-up state can hold a reduction (`AGENTS.md` rule 13). The chain now holds an in-session close of a held instrument with valid step-up and no bid, an after-hours close with a confirmed bid whose own limit is the floor, and an owner kill switch with `step_up_status: absent`, with their `IntentProposed` where one is proposed; it gains six invalid drafts and a `valid_drafts` list of three cases that must be accepted, and the mutant `regress.rule_12_ties_evidence_to_the_bid`, which restores the reviewed rule, is caught. The wording of the `OwnerExitRequested` paragraph is flagged for the founder's after-the-fact look, since it describes what DEC-158 option (c) unlocks. (12) **M5, `DecisionMade` gains `ask_suppressed`** (`budget`, `skipped_today`, `recent_timeout`, or `null`), with the name, values, and condition of M7's spec change (claim [#213](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/213), mandate spec §6.4 there): why an `ask` was classified but not asked, so closing `DecisionMade` does not block DEC-156 item 5. Rule 7's second clause makes it non-null only when `autonomy` is `ask`, which rule 7 already confines to openings and increases, so a suppression never skips an exit; that no `ApprovalRequested` names a suppressed decision is checked with the approval events. Two invalid drafts (a non-`ask` decision carrying it, and an unknown reason) and a valid draft (a suppressed `ask` on an opening). (13) **M6, only a §8.3 evaluation's decisions carry its numbers.** Rule 8 had required `exit_conviction`, `buy_conviction`, and `combined_score` on every purpose but `risk_exit`, so a discretionary exit from a goal's completion or a removed instrument, which no §8.3 evaluation produced, could not be journaled and would have been held. `DecisionMade` gains `exit_origin` (`signal`, `goal_completion`, `removed_instrument`, the three origins of mandate spec §6.1), non-null exactly on a `discretionary_exit`. The numbers are required exactly when the purpose is `open` or `increase` or the origin is `signal`; otherwise they are `null` and `outputs_used`, `model_weights`, and `clips_applied` are empty, since §8.3 step 5 names those among the evaluation's results. Every exit's writer holds what the rule needs, so it never holds an exit. The chain gains a `profit_stop` goal completion after the session, deferred (`session_not_allowed`) with nothing proposed and checked against mandate spec §3.1 and §6.2 and trading spec §9.6 by its own oracle; six invalid drafts, a valid removed-instrument exit without numbers, the mutant `regress.rule_8_numbers_on_every_exit_but_risk`, and four vector mutants. (14) **M7, the cross-event facts are checked where they ship.** Rule 10's second clause makes `append` refuse a batch in which an `IntentProposed`'s action members differ from those of the `DecisionMade` it names, decimals compared by value, at the first differing member. §11 gains two per-range checks on an agent stream, `intent_action_mismatch` and `mode_event_mismatch` (a non-null `mode_event` names an earlier `AgentModeChanged` with reason `kill_switch`), so `mandate journal verify` catches both across batches; a reference before a range's trusted start is left to the full-chain run. The vectors gain `valid_batches` (one), `invalid_batches` (three), and `range_verification` (two cases, each a re-chained tampered chain failing one range check at one seq), with the mutants `rule.10.batch`, `verify.intent_action_mismatch`, and `verify.mode_event_mismatch`. The Rust side (`mandate-journal`'s batch check and the verifier's two codes) is a backlog row: the spec guard keeps code out of this change. (15) **The round's minors and nits are backlog rows** under the freeze rule: a copied `AgentModeChanged` naming its `AgentModeApplied`; bounds and a personal-data scan for the model-supplied free text; `KillSwitchActivated` recording the initiator's step-up; the `journal.yaml` header's founder-approval line and DEC-176; `from __future__ import annotations` and ruff for `reference/`; and the stale version in `mandate-refcases`' journal module doc. (16) **Round 2's M1, the deferred goal exit records the defer code.** Item 13's goal-completion exit recorded `session_not_allowed`, the gate's deny code for an equity opening outside the session, where trading spec §9.6 defers a discretionary exit with `discretionary_exit_regular_session_only` (registered `# defer` in the trading-domain reference cases and asserted there at 17:00 ET). The vector now records that code, and `check_goal_exit` reads it from the trading-domain cases' deferred discretionary exits rather than from a constant. The exit is also a limit order, as trading spec §9.4 requires outside the session, priced at the exit ladder's first rung (reference bid 151 × (1 − 0.005), rounded up to the tick, 150.25; §5.6, §2.1), which the oracle recomputes with exact fractions; no other vector changes outcome. Three vector mutants (the deny code, a market order, the limit rounded down) are each caught by that oracle alone. No rule text changes | Accepted (agent, DEC-176) | DEC-174 found every agent-stream draft refused as `unknown_schema`, with no closed field list and no vector to register a schema from. Each ruling takes the spec's side of a contradiction, and each extra member stays only where another rule depends on it, so the rules tighten what `append` accepts and change nothing the runtime may do (DEC-176 (1) and (2)). Keeping version 3 is the one reading that lands the vectors with CI green and without mixing code into a spec change | Take the runtime's payloads as the schema (the journal would accept the contradictions, and correcting them later would need upcasters, §8); drop the extra members (the fold could not replay an owner's pause, confirm a kill switch, or keep an owner exit's floor from its own events); bump to version 4 here (fails the passing `journal::version` case, and the harness change cannot ship with a spec change, ES-22); close the approval events now (conflicts with M7's spec change, which extends them) |

| ID | Question | Needed by | Inputs |

| ~~OD-01~~ | ~~First exchange~~ | — | **Resolved by DEC-23** (Alpaca first, Kraken Derivatives US second) |
| OD-02 | First fast decision model: Laya (self-hosted) or Jev (hosted, early access) | Phase 3 (P1 in v1) | Bake-off on labeled financial decisions: accuracy, calibration, latency, cost |
| OD-03 | Adopt NautilusTrader connectors later, or keep our own | Phase 3 | LGPL review; connector coverage |
| OD-04 | Minimum paper-trading duration before live | M9 | Design-partner feedback; risk appetite |
| OD-05 | Mobile access to on-site approval services: customer VPN, end-to-end encrypted relay, or both | M10 | Design-partner security requirements |
| OD-06 | Minimum identity data held by the global control plane in hybrid mode | M8 | Privacy review; billing needs |
| OD-07 | Whether Kraken's self-service demo environment covers the US (Bitnomial-listed) perpetual contracts, or a UAT account is needed | Phase 3 | Kraken documentation and support |
| ~~OD-08~~ | ~~Alpaca day-trading regime; cash accounts~~ | — | **Resolved:** Alpaca applies the intraday margin rule since June 4, 2026; all Alpaca accounts are margin accounts (1× below 2,000 USD equity or by setting) |
| ~~OD-09~~ | ~~Fee rates and rounding~~ | — | **Resolved in approach:** SEC (sells), TAF (sells; cap per execution by default, per order configurable pending spec §15 Q6), CAT (buys and sells), accrued per fill and charged as a daily total rounded up; rates transcribed from Alpaca's fee schedule into effective-dated configuration at M3 |
| ~~OD-10~~ | ~~Close-window deferral~~ | — | **Resolved by DEC-70** |
| ~~OD-11~~ | ~~Ban code comments~~ | — | **Resolved by DEC-80** |
| OD-12 | Robinhood Agentic Trading: whether one platform may act for many customers, rate limits, and the MCP tool contract. **Resolved in part (2026-09-26, from Robinhood's support article):** access is self-serve, with no waitlist or developer registration; a customer with a primary individual investing account in good standing connects any MCP client to `https://agent.robinhood.com/mcp/trading` on a desktop, authenticates with their Robinhood login, and is prompted to open the agentic account (one of at most 10 individual accounts). The agent then reads **every** Robinhood account of the customer, including account numbers, positions, balances, transactions, and watchlists, and trades stocks in the agentic account and crypto through a matching Robinhood Crypto account (not in New York; no transfer, staking, or lending). Robinhood states the customer is responsible for the agent's trades. Open: rate limits, the tool contract, and platform-scale terms. Also open: whether the MCP tools accept a client order ID, whether retries with the same ID are idempotent, and whether an order can be queried by that ID; these decide whether journal-before-acting and crash reconciliation (E7-2, E7-3) are possible over MCP. Robinhood has no paper environment, so the go-live path for Robinhood users (E10-4 requires a paper run) needs a founder decision: the paper stage on the simulated broker with Robinhood's rules, on Alpaca paper, or both ([design questions](09-mandate-rewrite-questions.md) 8) | Before the Robinhood connector story (M8) | Robinhood support article "Agentic Trading overview"; compliance question 32 |
